# THE COGHEIM MASTER PLAN
## Limitless Game Makers LLC — Confidential

**This document has no version number, and will not have one again.** Founder ruling, 5 September 2026. V1 through V48 are history, not a pattern to continue. There is one living record from here on. What changed, and when, is recorded in the change log below, and every change is dated in place where it lands.

**Lineage:** grown from Master Index V48, compiled 3 September 2026, by applying the V49 change set of 4 September 2026 to the real V48 file rather than regenerating it from memory. **Supersedes all prior versions, V1 through V48.**

**Where this lives:** the Cogheim folder in Google Drive, as `master_index.md`, beside `cogheim_production_status.json`. Every session reads both from there. Only the Canon session and its nightly sweep write to them. The filenames do not change.

---

## CHANGE LOG

### 5 September 2026

**Version numbers retired.** One living document from here on.

**The record moved to Google Drive as the session readable store.** Project knowledge is at a hard cap near 2.0 MB and has already refused two uploads, so keeping the largest file in the project there was costing assets. Every session has Drive access, so every session reads the same two files. `COGHEIM_MASTER_INDEX_V48.md` and `COGHEIM_MASTER_INDEX_V49_CHANGESET.md` were both filed to Drive this day, which closes the single copy warning that had stood unsatisfied since V47.

**A private production console was published**, reading these two files live through the founder's own Drive connector. It holds no figures of its own.

**A nightly sweep was scheduled for 1:00 a.m. Eastern.** It reads what changed in the Drive folder, updates the two files, and files a field report named `claude_COGHEIM_Field_Report_<date>.md` in the same folder.

**The replication question was closed** from Epic's own documentation. See §5E. Stay on Replication Graph, `NET-12` is the decision point, Iris is the named fallback.

**Engine Law amended** so it can never be read as a constraint on ambition. See §38 standard #43.

**An admin area at cogheim.com/backstage/ was ruled and then set aside the same day.** The console lives on Drive instead. The backstage build and its Cloudflare Access instructions are kept in the Drive folder if that decision is revisited.

### 4 September 2026 — applied from the V49 change set

**Five standing locks overturned, each recorded visibly where it lands:** the war table is a physical lit relief model and not a hologram (§4B); class determined vehicles are retired and §13C stands (§13); three characters per account, not one (§37A-1); Greeters are canon people, one per class (§12B-7); the Loan Office identity varies by class (§3A). **And a sixth, a reversal:** the Christine Chronos retirement is reversed and she is restored to game canon in full (§11A).

**Six new sections written in:** the three UI layers (§16C), the optical family (§16D), the camera ladder (§16E), controls (§16F), navigation (§16G), and the boundary of the fiction (§16H).

**Fourteen rulings folded in** and the open lists reworked. See §37A.

**Documentation defects corrected**, including the `OPS-11` evidence line, which cited a `Deploy-CogheimAlpha.ps1` that does not exist.

**Four new session standards, #40 to #43.** The change set drafted them as #30 to #32; those numbers were already taken, and they were renumbered rather than allowed to overwrite.

**Not applied, deliberately:** the Schallûsan to Sael-Vori sweep. It needs a judgment per occurrence and a naive replace destroys two real people tributes. See §37A-2.

### 3 September 2026

**Storage policy ruled**, recorded as §38 standard #39.

### 31 August 2026

**The 30 percent net proceeds commitment removed in full.** Retired, not reassigned. No named beneficiary.

**The Einwechtëran language unsealed** for the website, the game and the Academy as a learnable ability. Book V's reveal stays sealed. Players learn to read the language, not what it is. The 5th glyph and the Hollow Crown Stage 5 glyph stay sealed.

**Harmonics fully unparked**, design and build, overturning the 30 August park.

> # ⚠ TWO CORRECTIONS TO WHAT V47 SAID ABOUT ITSELF. READ BOTH BEFORE TRUSTING ANY LINEAGE CLAIM.
>
> **Correction 1. V47's headline warning was false. V46 was NOT purged.**
> V47 opened by stating that every prior version had been retired from project knowledge and that this file was the only surviving Index. **`claude/COGHEIM_MASTER_INDEX_V46.md` is still in project knowledge as of 3 September 2026.** V41 through V45 are genuinely gone; V46 is not. The claim was written as a description of an intent rather than of a verified result, and nobody checked. **The law this breaks is the project's own: a delete is proven by the file being gone, never by the intention to delete it.**
> *Practical effect:* there IS one document to diff against, and V46 should be read rather than assumed absent. It should also be purged deliberately once V48 is confirmed good, at which point the single-copy warning becomes true rather than aspirational.
>
> **Correction 2. V47 was compiled without reading four documents that already existed.**
> V47 was written at 16:30 on 1 September. These four were created earlier the same day and were not read:
> `COGHEIM_Syndicate_System_Addendum_V1.md` (01:19) · `COGHEIM_Syndicate_System_V2_Sep1_2026.md` (02:36) · `COGHEIM_Harmonics_BookV_Submission_Brief_V1_Sep1_2026.md` (03:55) · `COGHEIM_Specialty_Badge_Spec_APPROVED_Sep1_2026.md` (04:04).
> This is the **third recurrence** of the §38 std. #29 failure — V43, V44 and now V47 were each written without the full newer-document sweep. **The standard is not the problem. Executing it under time pressure is.** See §38 std. #29A, added by this version.
>
> ### ✅ THE SINGLE-COPY WARNING IS SATISFIED, 5 SEPTEMBER 2026
> Project knowledge is not a backup. V47 said a copy must be kept outside it and V48 recorded that it still had not been done. **It has now been done.** `COGHEIM_MASTER_INDEX_V48.md`, 666,494 bytes, and `COGHEIM_MASTER_INDEX_V49_CHANGESET.md` are both in the Cogheim Google Drive folder, verified present by listing rather than asserted. **V46 can now be purged from project knowledge deliberately**, which is what V48 said should happen once V48 was confirmed good.
> **The standing rule does not relax:** refresh the Drive copy every time this document changes, in the same sitting, never as a second action on a second day.
>
> **What must NOT be purged:** the founder-decision documents, the session and handoff documents, `cogheim_production_status.json`, `render_production_status.py`, and the terrain generator scripts (`generate_world_terrain.py`, `carve_meridian.py`, `meridian_finish_v2.py`, `generate_vegetation.py`) — which are now **source, not artefacts**, per architecture ruling D7.

## 🆕 WHAT CHANGED IN V48

**Nothing in V47's founder decisions is overturned here.** The 30% removal, the language unsealing and the Harmonics unparking all stand unchanged. V48 is an addition-and-correction pass, not a reversal.

### 1. 🔴 Two V47 self-description defects corrected
Recorded in full in the warning block above. **V46 survives; V47 was written without reading four same-day documents.** Neither defect touches canon. Both touch trust in the document, which is worse.

### 2. 🏛 The Syndicate System is at V2.1 and V47 does not contain it — §12B, new
Eleven specialties, eleven co-signing founders, 121,000 IM founding, tenure-weighted mutiny, the Docket economy, The Larder and the Feast, the territory rework, five named Greeters, and The Wreck as a renewable site. **The June 2026 Addendum V1 is formally superseded** and now carries its own superseded banner. Six of its rules were changed. §12 and §12A of this Index predate all of it.

### 3. 🦿 Two silhouettes founder-locked, and both carry unresolved scale conflicts — §4D, new
**Vector** (six legs, three per side, purely lateral splay) and **Grindwalker** (nine wheels in three bogies, the arch, the glass command sphere as sole bridge). **Both locks are on the silhouette, not on the numbers behind it**, and three numeric conflicts remain open. **The Grindwalker deck count is contradicted between two founder-approved documents — 14 total (7+7) against 8 per hull — and that is the highest-priority open number in the roster.**

### 4. 🌍 The architecture-to-terrain handoff answers D1–D8 — §3F, new
Fixed five landscape layers per region; one region per map; Nanite on rocks and off foliage; versioned materials as build artefacts; baked-if-terrain, placed-if-geometry; a single `SEA_LEVEL_UU = -3400` constant; the two-tier runtime sampler withdrawn in favour of one composite baked field. **D7 is the load-bearing one: the generator scripts ARE the world**, and losing them makes every region a one-off.
**A measured defect is now on the record:** `carve_meridian.py` samples a ±12 texel window and squashes it 3.87× into 8 km, so Meridian disagrees with its own disc footprint by **203 m mean, 422 m worst.** Meridian is **not** to be re-carved. Future regions sample a footprint-sized 6.45-texel window.

### 5. 🔔 Harmonics: sixteen rulings, a canon reconciliation, and Ring designed — §26E, new
The Threefold (Body, Motion, Witness), the four Registers, glyph addresses as sentences, three schools plus the Registrars, the Manifest, chutes composed with the account model. **Reading now replaces the walk-there attunement gate, superseding Level Architecture V2 §7.3 gate two.**
**Then the novel ruled and the game folded.** Ruling 17 is overturned: **the underside of the disc is HELD** — not to be built, modelled, art-directed or scripted, and when eventually unheld it is *light, not structure*. **Do not run the v4 panel 6 prompt.**
**Ring is designed with its accessibility route rather than before it**, and the design changed shape because of it: the real cost is server-computed coarsening of returns, which is sense-independent, closing a balance inversion in which a player not using sound paid nothing. **No thresholds, locked** — which closes the §13 Ring exploit by construction.
**Terminology correction: the ancient builders are the Sael-Vori, not the Schallûsan.** A project-wide sweep is required and is **blocked** pending the §4.2b ruling.

### 6. 📚 The research floor and the act of researching — §26F, new
240 Academy nodes across eight disciplines plus 20 Applied Synthesis capstones, paid in Pages kept in a Commonplace notebook, read off a lit floor rather than a menu. **Two founder locks: no skip, paid or free, in any form; and twelve operation types with fifteen as the hard ceiling.**
**🔴 Authored blind.** 1,430 Iron Veleth nodes are already built and this design has not been diffed against them. **The reconciliation pass outranks every other action on that system.**

### 7. 🎓 Professor Evan House — §23B, new. 🎖 The specialty badge set — §33B, new
House is founder canon as the principal Harmonics quest giver. The badge set is **approved as concept**, eleven badges passing the 64-pixel silhouette test, with a flat vector pass outstanding.
**One art-direction correction with project-wide reach:** the blanket "no glow" negative was wrong and contradicted §33's locked dual-light rule. **Instruments, lamps and machinery may glow. Harmonics never does.** Replace the blanket line in every future prompt.
**One reusable lesson, paid for once:** to make something read as distant or half-glimpsed, **draw it completely and wash it back — high detail, low contrast, never low detail.** Faintness is a value problem.

### 9. 🗄 A storage rule is now written down — §38 std. #39, new
**Project knowledge has a hard ~2.0 MB cap, it is close to full, and there is no known way to expand it.** It has already refused two uploads. Std. #39 sets what belongs in it (the current Index, the founder decisions, the session trail, the status JSON and renderer, the generator scripts) and what does not (images, boards, renders, superseded versions, prompt packs already folded in, anything reproducible from a script and a seed). **Everything else goes to the Google Drive `Cogheim` folder, which exists and is writable.**
**The load-bearing line: the Drive backup and the purge of the previous Index are one action in one sitting, never two actions on two days.**

### 8. 📉 §0 has NOT been re-rendered for this version — stated rather than hidden
`cogheim_production_status.json` still carries `index_version: V47`, `as_of: 2026-08-31` and 137 items. **The 1–3 September scope in §3F, §4D, §12B, §26E and §26F is not yet declared in it**, so §0 below understates the declared scope and its percentages are stale in the direction of looking better than they are. **Re-declaring that scope will move several bars DOWN, which is the tracker working correctly.** Tracked as the first action in §37.

## 0. PRODUCTION STATUS — WHERE COGHEIM ACTUALLY IS

**As of 2026-08-31 · rendered from `Scripts/cogheim_production_status.json` by `Scripts/render_production_status.py`. Do not hand-edit a bar in this section — change the item state in the JSON and re-render.**

> **⚠ READ THIS BEFORE QUOTING A PERCENTAGE.** Every number below is a **weighted rollup of declared item states**, not a measurement of work done. Weights are t-shirt estimates (S=1, M=3, L=8, XL=20) and are **assumptions, not founder-approved sizing**. A bar says *how much of the declared scope has reached which verification state* — nothing more. **It is not a schedule, it is not an estimate of time remaining, and it must never appear in investor material without this caveat attached.**

### The state ladder — this is why the bars are honest

An item cannot skip a state, and **the top four are this project's own verification discipline**: `compiled and linked ≠ loaded in editor ≠ verified`. **Nothing reaches 100% without evidence a human can re-read.**

| State | % | What it means |
|---|---:|---|
| `not_started` | 0 | Nothing exists. |
| `specced` | 15 | A written spec exists. No founder lock, nothing built. |
| `locked` | 25 | Founder-locked design. Nothing built. |
| `in_progress` | 40 | Partial implementation exists. |
| `built_unverified` | 65 | Code or content committed. **No log read.** This is the most dangerous state — it looks finished and is not evidence. |
| `compiled` | 80 | A build log was read and it succeeded. |
| `loaded` | 90 | An editor log was read: the module loads or the asset is present. |
| `verified` | 100 | Automation or PIE evidence read from a log. |

### The four gates

| Gate | Target | Maps to |
|---|---|---|
| **VS — Vertical Slice** | One Strider, walkable, under way, with a player inside. The proof the game is possible. | New gate, ahead of section 36 Phase 1 |
| **ALPHA — Alpha** | Feature-complete for the core loop: Drifter tutorial, Meridian, Iron Frames, RFA at load. | Section 36 Phase 1 |
| **BETA — Beta** | Content-complete: all five Strider classes, economy, Syndicates, events, 6-10 regions. | Section 36 Phase 2 |
| **LAUNCH — Launch** | Full live service: 35 regions, full roster, live ops. | Section 36 Phase 3 |

**Bars are cumulative.** A track's Alpha figure includes its Vertical Slice items, because you cannot reach Alpha without them. That is the only reading that cannot be made to flatter by choosing a convenient denominator.

**Non-profit commitment:** ~~30% of all net proceeds (founding commitment, permanent)~~ — **REMOVED IN FULL, founder decision 31 Aug 2026.** Cogheim carries no mission commitment, no named beneficiary, and no committed percentage. Do not reinstate without an explicit founder ruling recorded as a visible overturn (§38 std. #12).

## 1A. CONTRIBUTOR ROYALTY FRAMEWORK (Founder Lock, July 2026)

**Status:** Non-binding term sheet drafted ✓ — explicitly framed as pending attorney review before any participant signs

**Purpose:** Governs likeness rights (memorializing real people as in-game characters — see §22) and creative naming-contribution royalties, separate from equity and separate from any investor SAFE.

**Two-track royalty structure — both use a pool-formula mechanic (fixed percentage pool ÷ number of active participants):**
| Track | Scope | Rate |
|---|---|---|
| **Track A — Likeness Royalty Pool** | Real people memorialized as in-game characters | 3.0% of net sales — covers both game and merchandise |
| **Track B — Creative Contributor Royalty Pool** | Naming contributions only (weapon/item/character/location names) — **not** gameplay mechanics or design, which remain solely the Founder's domain | 1.0% of net game sales only, per-person cap regardless of contribution count |

Contributors who opt into both tracks receive independently calculated shares from each pool.

**Document contents (16 numbered sections):** Both royalty tracks with formula tables and illustrative scenarios; mutual NDA; non-disparagement (scoped to false/malicious statements only, to avoid chilling legitimate disputes); assignment/successors; death/incapacity provisions; audit rights; independent contractor/tax status; dispute resolution; portrayal notice; severability; General Protective Terms & Conditions (limitation of liability, participant indemnification protecting Founder/Studio/investors/advertisers, no-interference-with-financing clause confirming royalties create no equity, advertiser/sponsor protection, no warranty, force majeure, entire agreement/amendment); open items section reserved for attorney resolution.

**Document styling:** Built as a branded Word doc using COGHEIM's own locked palette (Iron, Brass, Bronze, Ember, Parch), Cambria headings, brass page borders, header band, page-numbered footers — matches the studio's visual identity rather than a generic legal template.

**Critical distinction confirmed this session:** No royalty percentages had been previously locked anywhere in project history — earlier references were illustrative examples only, not commitments. This document is the first actual lock of these figures, and remains non-binding until attorney-reviewed and signed.

## 2. GAME OVERVIEW — COGHEIM

**Genre:** Steampunk 4X MMO — PC (primary), console (secondary target)
**Setting:** Post-apocalyptic flat-disc world, Year 2526
**Core mechanic:** Players command and inhabit massive mobile fortress-cities called **Striders**
**Scale:** 200–300 players per Strider (Syndicate); 500 Striders per server = up to 150,000 players
**Studio:** Limitless Game Makers LLC
**Engine target:** Unreal Engine 5 (migration from engine-agnostic C# prototype systems)

**Elevator pitch:** A steampunk MMO where your city is alive, your city moves, and your city is your only home. Each Strider is a walkable fortress the size of a small town, carrying 200–300 crew members across a flat disc world where civilizations grind against each other for terrain and survival.

**What makes it different from every existing MMO:**
- The Strider is not a base — it is a living, moving city that players walk, fight from, and call home
- Every Strider has full walkable interior (12–14 vertical decks depending on class)
- Main weapon requires 3-player minimum crew cooperation to fire
- Colossus events: a 30-story walking boss targets the weakest online Strider cinematically
- The Iron Veil: persistent, non-player PvE mega-event with seasonal lore payoffs
- Social layer: Running Lights docking mode, Syndicate founding gates, political NPC per Strider
- Narrative backbone: 5-novel Cogheim Quintet transmedia series feeds direct lore into the game world

---

## 2A. THE COGHEIM MANDATE

**Status:** LOCKED ✓ (founder, July 6, 2026)

**Mission:** COGHEIM ships gameplay that does not currently exist in any MMORPG. Every system must pass the Rival Test before it locks. COGHEIM is built to rival any currently existing MMORPG.

**The Rival Test — three questions, every spec:**
1. **Does this already exist?** If FFXIV, WoW, New World, ESO, Albion, or EVE already ships it, we don't build a reskin — we build past it or we don't build it.
2. **Does it only work because the Strider is home?** Our structural advantage is the moving fortress-city as community, identity, and battleground simultaneously. Systems that could be ported to a static-hub MMO unchanged are suspect.
3. **Does it survive contact with 200–300 real people?** Everything is designed for the Syndicate scale, not the solo player with NPC companions.

**The five pillars no rival currently combines:**
1. **The city moves and you live inside it** — full walkable interiors, diegetic everything, no pop-up UI (MAXIOM is the interface)
2. **Memory as a game system** — SME means NPCs remember, servers evolve, player actions leave witness marks other players discover
3. **Cooperative-mandatory power** — 3-crew main weapon, Colossus dual-pilot, nothing world-shaking fires solo
4. **The novels are the truth; the game is the archaeology** — a completed five-book canon feeding a mystery economy (7 hints, 11 eggs) no live-service writer's room can match
5. **Zero pay-to-win, permanently** — Crownmarks cosmetic-only, no currency conversion, no gifting; monetization can never touch the Rival Test

**Standing rule:** Any proposed system that fails the Rival Test gets flagged in the spec header before Boom reviews it. Kaelic Can does not present me-too features as innovations.

## 2B. ENGINE & CODE STANDARD — UE5.8 / C++ (FOUNDER LOCK, July 12, 2026)

**Status:** LOCKED ✓ — supersedes the prior "pure C# with NUnit" standard sitewide, retroactively.

**New standing rule:** All code going forward must be built to run inside **Unreal Engine 5.8**, written directly in **UE5 C++** (UCLASS/UPROPERTY/UFUNCTION, Actor Components, subsystems) rather than engine-agnostic C#/NUnit logic mirrors.

**What this changes:**
- **Target engine version updated:** UE5.4 → **UE5.8** for all future work, including the Disc Slice foundation and its M2/M3 milestones
- **Language:** C++ is now the only code standard. The previous "pure C#, zero Unity dependencies, verified via Python logic-equivalent mirrors" approach is retired.
- **Testing:** UE Automation Spec tests replace NUnit as the verification standard going forward
- **Structure:** UCLASS/UPROPERTY/UFUNCTION conventions, Actor Components, and UE Subsystems are the default architecture for new systems (e.g. an SME module becomes a UGameInstanceSubsystem or similar, not a standalone C# class)

**What this means for existing systems:** Every system logged in §35 as "Coded (C# + NUnit)" is **spec-complete but implementation-stale** — the design, data structures, and test logic remain valid and authoritative, but the actual code must be rebuilt in UE5 C++ before it can run in-engine. This applies to all 10 Interior/Combat/HUD/Colossus/Quest systems plus all 5 SME modules plus all 5 Quest System V1 modules — **~1,000+ tests' worth of verified logic to port, not redesign.**

**Priority order for rebuild (recommended, not yet founder-locked):** Interior systems (5) → SME (5 modules) → Quest System V1 (5 modules) → Combat/HUD/Colossus (3) → remaining quest categories as newly built work, in C++ from the start.

**Workflow impact:** Spec-before-code still applies unchanged (§38) — specs do not need to be rewritten, only re-implemented. Kaelic Can flags at the start of every future code session whether the target file is a **fresh C++ build** or a **port from an existing C# spec**, and confirms UE5.8-compatible API usage before writing.

## 3. WORLD CANON

**The Disc:**
- Earth's compressed geological crust — NOT a constructed mechanical surface
- Dr. Marcus Einwechter built the **Terran Stabilization Engine** in 2274 (compression nodes embedded in Earth's crust)
- The government weaponized it in 2301 to force the continents together: **Suture-Day**
- This origin is the Book IV reveal (The Einwechter Line) — **sealed; do not spec further**
- All terrain = natural Earth geology: mountains (collision zones, upthrust terrain, ancient impact ridges), valleys, flats. Striders/Colossi/Iron Veil stand on real ground. No mechanical terrain, no glowing grids — **Environmental Render Rule, locked June 2026**

**Year 2526 — Game Present**

**Key historical timeline:**
| Year | Event |
|---|---|
| 2211–2274 | GAIA-9 built |
| 2274 | Dr. Marcus Einwechter completes Terran Stabilization Engine |
| 2301 | Suture-Day — continents forced together |
| 2372 | Mordechai Stridefall asks GAIA-9 "What do you want?"; receives 14-minute Sael-Komm |
| 2374 | Mordechai liberates GAIA-9; builds first Colossus; encodes VELK; disappears through gate alone |
| 2376 | Suture-Day (Year 0 of disc reckoning) |
| 2426 | Weston Ember-Hold (last Ironvast engineer) goes through gate; reunites with Mordechai; carries 17 dead colleagues + 47+ volumes |
| 2463 | Soot-Bridge's eleven contact reports (Colossus encounters) |
| 2509 | Christine Chronos finds Weston's Transit Strider archives; has known GAIA-9 full scope for ~27 years by game present |
| 2518 | Sealed lore entries (Candy Mind-Stone named in sealed archive) |
| 2521 | Child aboard Venn Hollow draws Sael glyph from memory, age 6 |
| 2522 | Christine stands before the glyph for 11 minutes during the trading summit |
| 2524 | Sael-Vor node activates (11 years to threshold) |
| 2525 (Day 332) | Commander Rade Voss-Iron disappears at 0341 hours |
| 2526 | Game present |

**Gate departure order (sealed):** Ironvast (2426) → Sunderveil → Ashborne → Pale Reach
- Rita Marie Schallus departed last; sent final transmission: **"Found."** at 0312

**Civilization-to-class lineage:**
| Ancient Civilization | Seeds Class |
|---|---|
| Ironvast | Juggernaut |
| Sunderveil | Vector |
| Ashborne | Scavenger |
| Pale Reach | Grindwalker |
| **Blackjack** | Born from the blend — Transit Striders where all four peoples mixed; youngest class that believes itself oldest; proof the Forgetting happened |

**The Oasis:**
- Accessible from Iron Veil; 7 breadcrumb hints across the game world
- First opened in **Book V: The Full Account**
- Blur sharpens when the Einwechter name is spoken as a permanent commitment
- **Lyra Cross easter egg:** exists; GAIA-9 tattoo on her back is mark of designation; she is a GAIA-9 emissary placed to find whoever GAIA-9 marked as ready. The tattoo is the answer to all 7 Oasis hints. **End-game timing held by founder — existence logged only. Do not spec mechanics.**

**Schallûsan = "The Protectors of People"**
- Likely became/connected to the Oasis
- Possibly the Sunderveil civilization
- Pre-compact sealed manifest named Kael before his birth
- Root word *sael* = "the question that opens"

**Kael Einwechter (protagonist name):**
- Born Kael Voss-Dren in the Black-Maw Station orphan system
- Sealed manifest did not rename him — it returned the name he was always born to carry
- Descendant of the Schallûsan; arrives running on grief

## 3A. WORLD MAP SYSTEM (Founder Lock, July 2026)

**Status:** **Fully locked ✓** — all previously-open geometry/scale items resolved this session. Spec complete (`COGHEIM_World_Map_System_V1.md`), C++ build package status tracked in §35.

**Context:** This closes a real gap — no dedicated session had ever defined disc shape, zone tiling logic, biome distribution, Black-Maw Station placement, or inter-zone transit mechanics, despite multiple other systems (Colossus spawn-locking, Weather zones, MAXIOM World Map tab) already referencing a 24-zone disc structure informally.

**Disc geometry — LOCKED, final:**
- **Pangea-analog supercontinent**, single connected landmass surrounded by one contiguous world ocean
- **Land-to-ocean ratio corrected: 81.3% land / 10.7% ocean** (supersedes the earlier 75/25 figure — the terrain generator was miscounting off-disc image pixels as ocean, producing a false reading; continent rescaled 1.00→0.85 to fix, requiring full regeneration of the location registry below)
- **Direct lore payoff, not arbitrary art:** Suture-Day (2301, §3) is the Terran Stabilization Engine physically forcing Earth's continents together — a Pangea-shaped result is the mechanically correct outcome of that canon event
- Natural Earth geology throughout, per the Environmental Render Rule (§33) — no hex grid, no pie-slice sectors, no visible grid lines
- **World ocean solves the water-vehicle footprint automatically** — Tide-Runner, The Waterhammer, Iron-Tide (§13C) now have a natural, lore-justified home in the ocean ringing the supercontinent plus inland seas/bays

**Water depth system — LOCKED (July 17):**
- Three depth bands: Shallow / Deep / Trench
- Land Striders can wade shallow water up to a tunable depth limit (not yet numbered)
- All water is traversable — no impassable ocean tiles

**🟢 2,500km rescale — EXECUTED (July 22, resolves the V29 🔴 critical flag):**
- Full regeneration completed against the 2,500km gameplay diameter (was deferred in V29, now done)
- Compression ratio held at **1:13.4**, yielding a precise **~20,816-mile lore diameter**
- Grindwalker hull speed used for traverse-time math: 14 km/h (was 4 km/h); Blackjack at 10 km/h
- Pipeline: `regenerate_all_locations.py` → `world_map_locations.json` → `emit_location_data.py` → `CogheimLocationData.cpp`; all positions stored as fractions of disc radius for scale-invariance
- Delivered as `COGHEIM_WorldMap_AlphaTrack_Handoff.zip` — **data-verified but not yet compile-verified**, pending Shadow PC

**Location registry — EXPANDED, 738 total entries (up from 350, July 22):**
- The Blend density decision roughly doubled the cheap layers plus a 2× sensor-range bump, restoring ~15 discovered locations per crossing at the new, much larger scale
- ✅ **3 Black-Maw Stations** — Coldforge Landing, Meridian Bastion, Cindermere Bastion (names confirmed **deliberately unchanged** — see terminology note below)
- ✅ **8 Academies total** — 5 land-biome + 3 Maritime, all sharing one `AcademyNetwork` progress group regardless of which physical instance a player uses; each Maritime Academy is a separate physical instance with its own population
  - Coldforge and Meridian Maritime Academies: open-ocean, ~850m depth

## 3C. TRAVERSAL, ANCHORS & THE EXCURSION BANDS (Founder rulings, 29 Aug 2026)

**Status:** Four founder rulings LOCKED ✓ | architecture spec written (`COGHEIM_Level_Architecture_Whitepaper_V2.md`) | **nothing built**
**Why this section exists:** every prior world-model section treated the Strider as the unit of traversal. Players cross this world on foot, in Iron Frames, in ground vehicles, on water, under water and in the air, frequently with no Strider anywhere near them. That single correction reshapes streaming, relevancy, content siting and the §5C scaling claim.

### The four founder rulings

| # | Ruling |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Off-hull play is regional** — tens of km, expedition scale. Not local-only, not continental |
| 2 | **Aircraft split by class** — light aircraft tactical and hull/station-tied; one class is long-range transport |
| 3 | **Diegetic anchor network** — your Strider, the 8 academies, the 3 Black-Maw Stations, plus personal-vehicle retrieval at any anchor |
| 4 | **Players on foot stay faster than Striders** — the Strider's value is logistics, protection and capacity, not speed |

### The measured problem

Every way a player can move is faster than every Strider. Measured against locked figures.

| Class | km/h | 8km region | Full disc | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| On foot, sprint | 32.4 | 14.8 min | 3.22 d | UE default 900 cm/s — **assumption** |
| On foot, walk | 21.6 | 22.2 min | 4.82 d | UE default 600 cm/s — **assumption** |
| **Iron Frame, Tier 5** | **18.0** | 26.6 min | 5.78 d | **LOCKED**, 11.2 mph (§13H) |
| Grindwalker / Vector | 14.0 | 34.3 min | 7.44 d | **LOCKED** (§4) |
| Scavenger | 11.0 | 43.6 min | 9.47 d | **LOCKED** (§4) |
| Blackjack | 10.0 | 48.0 min | 10.42 d | **LOCKED** (§4) |
| Juggernaut | 8.0 | 60.0 min | 13.02 d | **LOCKED** (§4) |
| Ground vehicle · watercraft · submarine · light aircraft · transport aircraft · Iron Frame Wings/Waterjet | — | — | — | **NOT LOCKED** |

**Six of eleven traversal classes have no locked speed. Lock them against a streaming budget, not for feel** — cheap now, expensive to retrofit. Aircraft is the hardest case because altitude multiplies view distance while speed shrinks the time available to load.

### The organizing principle — ANCHORS

**Anchors decide where authored content exists. Everything between anchors is procedural.** Discovering an anchor extends the authored world, which turns the Explorer/Scholar charter (§12A) from a box-tick into a structural advantage no competitor in the Rival Test list offers.

| Class | Count | Fixed? | Provides |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A. Black-Maw Stations** | 3, LOCKED | Fixed | Depot, garage, mooring, respawn, no-attack |
| **B. Academies** | 8, LOCKED | Fixed (already a Strider-kind RFA frame with static `HullWorldPosM`, §5C) | Fixed-price Depot, translation, research consumables, garage, mooring, respawn |
| **C. Striders under running lights** | up to 500 | Mobile | Home, Depot merge, garage, respawn, protection zone |
| **D. Einwechtëran monuments** | 24 **PROPOSED** | Fixed | Person-only transit between attuned nodes — **canon-gated, see V43 header block** |

**Fixed anchor total under this proposal: 35**, plus up to 500 mobile.

### The excursion bands — not walls, bands of support

| Band | Radius | Content | Feel |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A. Sensor Envelope** | 0–16 km | Full authored density, recovery possible, contact with home | You are covered |
| **B. Expedition Band** | 16–48 km | Tier 1 plus sparse authored POI and PCG-dressed encounters, no support | You are on your own and it is worth it |
| **C. Open Disc** | beyond 48 km | Tier 1 only, deliberately empty | You are crossing, not exploring |

**Neither radius is invented.** 16 km is the weakest Strider-mounted sensor (Scavenger, §3A), protected by the sensor split-role invariant — so the rule reads diegetically as *you are covered as far as your city can see you*, and that existing lock gains a second job. 48 km is the Iron Veil's gameplay-scale width (§3A).

**Measured cost:** Band A across 35 fixed anchors is 28,149 km², **0.57% of the disc**. Bands A+B are 253,338 km², **5.16%**. One authored 8km region per fixed anchor is 2,240 km², **0.046%**.

**Why authored terrain could never cover the disc, stated once so it is never re-litigated:** covering Pale Reach (18.6% of the disc, the locked Alpha zone) in standard 8km tiles takes **14,266 tiles**. Covering all 738 registered locations takes 738, which is 0.96% of the disc. Procedural Tier 1 terrain is not a compromise, it is the only architecture the locked numbers permit — and the moving-city fantasy is what makes it acceptable, since players are inside a hull for most of their playtime and the outside is horizon-scale.

### Founder rulings, all closed V44

| Item | Ruling |
|---|---|
| §5C shared-root scaling claim | **AMENDED IN PLACE** (§5C), with anchor-partitioned root recorded as the intended post-Alpha scheme |
| Off-hull concurrency | **10% design target, 25% stress ceiling.** 15,000 in the shared root at the 150k target, stress-tested to 37,500 |
| Excursion band radii | **16 km and 48 km, LOCKED** |
| Long-range air travel | **CORRIDOR ONLY, anchor to anchor. The transport class is the LANTERN.** It moors, it does not land arbitrarily |
| Vehicle retrieval | **THE SAME OBJECT, MOVED**, with recovery as a paid service (§23A, Captain Beugois) |
| Monument network | **SHIP UNNAMED** — unexplained pre-Suture relics, Book V names them |

**Consequences of the Lantern ruling, recorded so they are not rediscovered later.** Corridor flight means both endpoints of any long-range flight are anchors and are therefore prefetchable, which is what makes the project's worst streaming case tractable. It also means **the Lantern requires mooring infrastructure at every anchor it serves** — that is a real content requirement on academies, Black-Maw Stations and any Strider rated for it, and it should be in the anchor region template before 35 regions are authored. Free-roaming continental flight is explicitly rejected.

**Consequence of the retrieval ruling.** A vehicle left in Band B stays there as a real object. That makes recovery a paid service rather than a non-event, gives Strider Recovery Operations (§23A) a second use beyond hulls, and keeps vehicles as objects in the crafting economy rather than instances. It also means **stranding is a genuine consequence**, which is the intended feel for Band B.

### Still open — one item, and it is a session rather than a ruling

**Lock the six unlocked traversal speeds** (ground vehicle, watercraft, submarine, light aircraft, transport aircraft, Iron Frame Wings and Water Jetpack) **against a streaming budget rather than for feel.** Needs the velocity-projected streaming maths in the room. Recommended sequencing: after the RFA netcode work completes, before any air or water content is authored.

## 3D. STRIDER TERRAIN LIMITS & LOCOMOTION CANON (FOUNDER-LOCKED, 30 Aug 2026)

> ### 🔴 V46 AMENDMENT — THE GRINDWALKER BEAM MOVED. READ THIS BEFORE ANY FIGURE BELOW.
>
> **Founder-locked, 30 Aug 2026, in the Grindwalker build session: the bounding box is now `950 m L × 330 m W × 140 m H`, beam split `90 / 150 / 90`.** This supersedes the 420 m beam carried through V41, V44 and V45, **and** supersedes the 100 m hull / 220 m gap ruled earlier the same day in `COGHEIM_Founder_Decisions_Aug30_2026.md` §4. Nothing else in that decisions document is affected.
>
> | Property | New | Previous | Status |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | Length | 950 m | 950 m | Unchanged, locked |
> | **Total beam** | **330 m** | 420 m | **Locked, revised** |
> | **Per-hull width** | **90 m** | 100 m (and 420-era options 80–140) | **Locked, revised — the open parameter is CLOSED** |
> | **Inner straddle gap** | **150 m** | 220 m | **Locked, revised** |
> | Hull height / decks / pitch / arch decks | 140 m / 14 / 10 m / 8 | unchanged | Locked |
> | **Arch underside clearance** | **60 m** | 60 m | Unchanged, derived |
> | **Straddle envelope** | **150 m wide × 60 m tall** | 220 × 60 | **Locked, revised** |
> | Locomotion | Wheeled, `ERFALocomotion` bit **1** | bit 1 | **Unchanged. Do not renumber** |
>
> **Why 90/150/90 and not the founder's proposed equal thirds (110/110/110):** a 110 m hull against a 140 m height is a hull almost as wide as it is tall, the proportion the concept art most clearly rejects. 90 m keeps the hull reading as a ship at **10.6 : 1** length to width, and buys back 40 m of straddle gap, which is the class capability. **Equal thirds spent the narrowing on the wrong third.** The narrower 330 m ship — the substance of the founder's request — is preserved.
>
> **Derived figures, corrected (arithmetic from locked values, not measurements):** length:beam **2.88 : 1** (was 2.26) · hull length:width **10.6 : 1** (was 9.5) · hull width:height **0.64 : 1**, taller than wide · front aspect beam:height **2.36 : 1** (was 3.0) · **hull centre-to-centre spacing 240 m** (was 320) · arch clear span : truss depth 1.88 : 1 · one deck both hulls 171,000 m² · total plan footprint 313,500 m² · spans **168.3** landscape vertices at 1.9608 m (was 214.2) · **4.13%** of the 8,000 m Meridian region width (was 5.25%).
>
> **Three consequences are real. Everything else is a number swap.**
>
> **1. Roll sensitivity rises 33%, and it lands on the plane fit (§3E).** Hull centres are 240 m apart instead of 320 m, so the same elevation difference produces a larger roll angle — 10 m of difference is now **2.39°** where it was 1.79°; 40 m is **9.46°** where it was 7.13°. **Contact points in the plane fit move inward from ±160 m to ±120 m.** Whoever holds the RFA hull-Z work **re-derives the roll rate limiter and any comfort clamp. Do not carry the old numbers forward.**
>
> **2. The straddle window narrows 32%, to 150 m × 60 m.** The class capability is fully preserved — no other Strider straddles at all — but terrain features **between 150 m and 220 m wide** that would have passed under the old arch now block the hull. **This is a live gameplay tuning knob, not a loss**: a narrower slot makes straddling a matter of pilot judgement and route reading rather than something that happens automatically. **Author passability against 150 m.** Nothing shipped is invalidated because no passability data exists yet, but any straddle route sketched against 220 m needs rechecking.
>
> **3. The silhouette reads more vertical.** Front aspect 3.0 : 1 → 2.36 : 1 — a taller, narrower gate rather than a wide low table, closer to the gothic-industrial direction. An observation to carry into the blockout, not a decision.
>
> **⚠ Do not cite structural credibility as the reason for this revision.** Both 1.88 : 1 and the previous 2.75 : 1 span-to-depth are far more material than a truss of this span requires — real trusses run 10 : 1 to 15 : 1. The arch is 80 m deep because it contains eight decks of command and council space, not because the span demands it. **The reason for the revision is proportion and gameplay.**
>
> **Capacity is unaffected in any way that matters.** Locked housing is 300 seats, 150 per hull, over 5 decks at 30 doors; one deck of one hull is 85,500 m². The Grindwalker is nowhere near volume-limited, so 300 seats remains a design choice about Syndicate size, not a physical constraint. The 10% loss of deck area does not touch it.
>
> **Explicitly NOT affected:** `ERFALocomotion` bit values · arch underside at 60 m · length, height, deck count, deck pitch, hull speed · hull Z as a pure function of `(Course, Frame)` · Pass 2 Decisions 1, 2, 3 and 5 · the interior spec (Drive Line, Promenade Spin, A5 Spindle, A7 Longrest sealed, crew stations at A1/A6/A8, 300 seats — none beam-dependent at this granularity). **⚠ The shipped `GrindwalkerInteriorLibrary` C++ and its 75 checks are *not known* to encode beam. Requires verification against the source before that is asserted as safe.**
>
> **Documents this invalidates, all corrected forward in V46, none of it shipped code:** `COGHEIM_Founder_Decisions_Aug30_2026.md` §4 · V45 §6 and its hull-scale physics paragraph · V44 §6 and V41 §6 (historical, correct forward only) · `COGHEIM_Strider_Terrain_Limits_Spec_V2.md` §3 and §7A · `COGHEIM_Handoff_Grindwalker_Build_Session.md` §2 and §2.1.
>
> **Implementation status: nothing has been built, modelled, compiled, loaded or tested.** No Unreal project files were opened for this ruling. The blockout proceeds against these numbers **with per-hull width still parameterised**, so a further revision costs a rebuild and not a remodel.

**Status: FOUNDER-LOCKED, Michael Einwechter, 30 Aug 2026 — decisions 1 through 6 all approved.** Source: `COGHEIM_Strider_Terrain_Limits_Spec_V2.md`, which **supersedes V1 of the same spec written the same day.**

### The six locked decisions

1. ✅ **Per-locomotion constraint model** replaces V1's single leg-travel model.
2. ✅ **Passability is authored per-class data, never derived from heightmap gradient.**
3. ✅ **The Grindwalker straddle is a real mobility capability**, arch underside derived at 60 m; width still open.
4. ✅ **Ride feel comes from authored events, not terrain.**
5. ✅ **Vector's counterweight approved** — destructible legs, and reduced capacity which also reduces Docked Exchange host income. **Shape locked, magnitudes not set.**
6. ✅ **Scavenger runs many small distributed ski-like tracks along an articulated body, not legs of any kind** — corrected 30 Aug 2026. Lowest ground pressure in the roster, best local conformance, cannot bridge a gap.

### Locomotion canon per class — founder-stated, 30 Aug 2026

| Class | Locomotion |
|---|---|
| Grindwalker | Twin-hull catamaran, wheels, "kinect drive" |
| Blackjack | Caterpillar tracks |
| Juggernaut | Multiple tracks at its base |
| Drifter | Half-track — front wheels, rear tracks |
| Scavenger | Many small distributed ski-like tracks on an articulated body |
| **Vector** | **Spider-like, 6 to 8 massive legs — the ONLY legged class in the roster** |

**The Scavenger correction resolved a real risk at its root.** V2 had flagged Vector as potentially strictly dominant. Reading Scavenger as legged was the error; once corrected, the dominance problem disappears. **An index carrying that flag as open would be wrong.**

### Why passability had to be authored — the load-bearing measurement

The baked disc heightmap is **2017 × 2017 over 2,500 km = 1,240 m per texel.** At that resolution a vertical cliff reads as a gentle slope:

| Cliff height | Gradient as the heightmap sees it |
|---|---|
| 50 m | 2.31° |
| 100 m | 4.61° |
| 200 m | 9.16° |
| **400 m** | **17.88°** |

**Heightmap gradient literally cannot see cliffs.** That is the whole argument for decision 2, and it is Alpha-critical.

### Hull-scale physics (arithmetic from locked values, not measurement)

Grindwalker 950 m × **330 m** × 140 m at 14 km/h traverses its own length in **244 seconds**. Terrain features under ~**475 m** are physically bridged and cannot pitch the hull. A 40 m step descended over ~2 minutes is **0.0005 g**. The hull is **2.88× longer than wide** (V46 — was 2.26× at the retired 420 m beam), so it tolerates 2.88× more roll than pitch. Bow-to-stern elevation difference over 950 m: 1° = 16.6 m, 5° = 83.1 m, 20° = 345.8 m.

### The Grindwalker straddle (derived, with a named assumption)

14 decks over a 140 m hull gives a uniform 10 m pitch; 8 Arch decks = 80 m; **arch underside = 60 m**. Assumption named explicitly: that Arch decks share the hull's 10 m pitch. Sanity: an Iron Frame at 2.6 m straddles, a three-storey ruin at 12 m straddles, a 60 m rock spine is an edge case, a Colossus at ~95 m is blocked, and the **Blackjack hull at 152 m — the tallest Strider — is blocked.**

**Passability bitmask:** `wheeled(1) | half-track(2) | tracked(4) | multi-track(8) | distributed-track(16) | legged(32)`. **Zero is fail-closed** — an unmapped region admits nobody.

### Built and verified

`RFAPassability.h/.cpp` in `CogheimNetcode`, `rfa_passability_mirror.py` 24/24, `RFANetcodeSpec` block 8 checks **P01–P24, 24/24 Success**, verified in the automation log 30 Aug 08:41–08:42 UTC. **Verified in Automation, not in PIE.** Two named properties worth keeping: **P12** legs sink where distributed track floats and distributed track cannot bridge what legs step over; **P23** no locomotion's capability set is a superset of another's, so *dominance is impossible by assertion, not by balance intent*.

**The passability data layer does not exist.** No region in any level carries a mask yet. Only the validation code that will read it.

### A consequence worth stating plainly

A Strider **cannot react to a cliff at runtime.** Pass 2 Decision 3 makes hull motion a pure function of (Course, Frame), Decision 5 says a Strider is never physically displaced, and Decision 2 says there is no physics anywhere. **The safety bar must therefore be a constraint on what course can be plotted, not a reaction to terrain.**

### Open

**Grindwalker per-hull width — ✅ CLOSED V46 at 90 m, with the beam revised to 330 m (see the amendment at the head of this section). The paragraph below is retained as the record of why it had to be a decision.** It **must be authored as a decision, not read off concept art** — proportion in a rendered three-quarter view is not dimension, and this value feeds passability data that ships. *(Retired option table, kept for the record: at the then-locked 420 m beam, hull width 80 m gave a 260 m inner gap, 100 m gave 220 m, 120 m gave 180 m, 140 m gave 140 m. **The ruling instead moved the beam itself to 330 m and the hull to 90 m, giving a 150 m gap.**)* Also open: the §6 physical parameters (wheel radii, idler heights, leg lift), which are **not blocking implementation**; arch underside is the one to lock first. Vector counterweight magnitudes.

## 3E. HULL Z & TERRAIN CONFORMANCE (Founder Decision 4 ruled, 30 Aug 2026)

**Status: FOUNDER-RULED design, NOT BUILT.** The sampler underneath it is built and tested. Everything above the sampler is a design proposal. Nothing in this section has been compiled, run, or measured in-engine.

### The ruling

**Authoritative hull Z is a deterministic sample of baked heightfield data — never a query against streamed runtime collision.** Leg, wheel and track articulation is **cosmetic and client-side** and must never feed back into authoritative position.

The reason is structural, not preference: hull position is a pure function of `(Course, Frame)`, evaluable for **any** frame — past, present or future — on any machine with no history buffer. A dedicated server has no landscape streamed in, and two clients streaming different regions would trace against different geometry. A line trace destroys that property; a sampled array preserves it.

**Upgraded from the original proposal:** the single-sample design was rejected. At **1,239.46 m per disc texel** the entire 950 m Grindwalker fits inside one texel, so a single sample yields one Z for the whole ship — no pitch, no roll, no cresting a ridge — and every figure in §3D (the roll tolerance, the 475 m bridging threshold, the pitch-rate table) becomes decorative. The ruling is a **multi-point plane fit over a two-tier heightfield.**

### The two tiers

| Tier | Source | Resolution | Authoritative |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Coarse** | Baked disc heightmap, 2017 × 2017 over 2,500 km | **1,239.46 m / texel** | Everywhere outside an authored anchor region |
| **Fine** | Authored region heightfield, 4081 × 4081 over 8,000 m | **1.9608 m / vertex** | Inside a region's authored footprint |

**633× resolution difference.** Both are baked project data, so both satisfy the ruling. Selection is **by footprint, not proximity**, and must be a pure function of position plus the region manifest, with no engine state. A defined boundary blend prevents an unbounded step at a region edge.

### The plane fit

Contact points are **authored per class, in hull-local metres**, colocated with the passability bitmask because both describe how a class meets the ground and both are authored rather than derived.

| Class | Locomotion | Contact set | Min points |
|---|---|---|---|
| Grindwalker | Twin catamaran hulls on wheels | Fore and aft under each hull, at **±120 m** lateral (per the 330 m beam lock) | 4, prefer 6 |
| Juggernaut | Multiple tracks at the base | Along each track run | 6 |
| Blackjack | Caterpillar tracks | Fore, mid, aft on each track | 6 |
| Scavenger | Distributed ski-like tracks, articulated body | Along the articulated spine | 8 |
| Vector | 6–8 massive legs | One per leg | 6–8 |
| Drifter | Half-track | 2 front, 2 rear | 4 |

Least-squares plane through the sampled points, equally weighted. Yields **hull Z at centroid, pitch, and roll**. **Hull yaw is never touched by the fit** — heading comes from the course and only from the course. Reject and fail closed to the previous frame's plane if the residual exceeds a class threshold; that condition means the contact points straddle something the passability data should have refused, and it is **a data defect worth logging**, not a case to smooth over.

**It still costs zero bandwidth.** `(Course, Frame)` → XY by closed-form evaluation → N sample points by class data → N heights from baked data → plane. Every input is already replicated or is static project data. **Nothing new replicates.** That is Decision 3 doing its job and it must not be broken by any later change.

**The interior does nothing, for now.** Rotating SRF is held at identity until M3. The exterior may pitch and roll while the interior frame stays level. That separation is the correct build order — **but see §4C.6, where the bridge ruling makes it visible and forces a decision.**

### ⚠ A determinism recommendation that was withdrawn, and why

An earlier draft called for sampling on an integer grid with specified rounding, to harden float determinism. **That is wrong.** Cogheim has no resimulation and no rewind comparison that could amplify a last-ulp difference (Decision 2 removed networked physics), so the requirement is that **machines agree on a position, not that they produce bit-identical doubles.** Elapsed time already derives from an integer frame delta and evaluation is closed-form, so nothing accumulates. No new determinism machinery is specified.

### Sequencing — locked

| Stage | Delivers | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | The interface: contact-point class data, plane-fit signature, one-point set per class so behaviour is identical to today | Nothing. Can land the day the tree is quiet |
| **B** | Real contact sets, plane fit live, pitch and roll applied to the exterior | A, plus §3D residual thresholds |
| **C** | Fine tier: region heightfield as runtime data, footprint selection, boundary blend | B, plus the side-car cook decision |
| **D** | Feed the plane into rotating SRF | M3 |

The Z seam is already collapsed to **one call site** — `USRFLibrary::HullPos3AtFrame` — precisely so this is a data change rather than a rewrite. `SRFSpec` check **35** currently asserts Z is still flat. **It is a tripwire. When the heightmap lands it must be rewritten, never deleted.**

---

### 3E-1. THE TERRAIN SESSION'S ANSWER — measured, and it changes the shape of the problem

The netcode session asked four questions it could not answer from its own side. The terrain/level session measured the actual assets and answered them. **Every number below is measured; every recommendation is labelled.**

**⚠ READ FIRST — the heightmap changed and the level has not been updated.** `Meridian_Heightmap_4081.png` is **not** the file to bake from. It was regenerated as `Meridian_Heightmap_4081_v2.png` because the original carried **254 closed basins deeper than 9 m, 200 deeper than 25 m, the worst 346 m** — inescapable pits — plus one authored glyph formation. **39.8% of cells changed, largest change +346.6 m, mean +6.0 m.** The new file **has not been imported into the level.** Nothing may be baked until the landscape and the bake read the same file.

**Q1 — which map is authoritative?** **The region. And the disc map is not usable at hull scale at all.**

| Measurement | Value |
|---|---|
| Region footprint on the disc | **6.45 × 6.45 texels** |
| Disc says that ground is | 27.3 – 46.0 m |
| Region says that ground is | −59.5 – 568.7 m |
| **Mean absolute disagreement** | **203.4 m** |
| Worst disagreement | **+422.1 m** |
| A 330 m Strider beam spans | **0.266 disc texels** / ~168 region vertices |

**And the disagreement is structural, not rounding.** The carve script sampled a **±12 texel window — 31 km of disc — and resized it to 8 km**, a 3.87× squash, then added its own mountains, erosion, rivers and hydrology on top. **The region was never a refinement of its own disc footprint.** It is a new terrain seeded by a stretched neighbourhood. So the disc is not even a strategic-scale approximation of the region; it is a different surface.

**Recommendation:** the authored region is authoritative wherever one exists, full stop; **the disc becomes a derivative**, regenerated by downsampling authored regions into their footprints with a blend collar, so the two agree *by construction* and can never drift; and the carve script is fixed so future regions sample a footprint-sized window. Re-carving Meridian is **not** proposed — it would destroy terrain the founder has approved, including the Wingwater and the new glyph. **Requires founder approval** (open item 3 in the banner).

**Q2 — the seam.** **Recommendation: delete it rather than manage it.** One composite elevation field, one sampler, no runtime mode switch — a single baked asset holding the disc grid plus high-resolution patches at known origins, resolving internally to the finest patch covering the point. **Blending happens at bake time, never at runtime.** The pure-function property is preserved exactly, and there is no seam behaviour to specify, test, or get wrong.

**One addition worth pushing for:** hull Z as the **mean of the field over the hull footprint**, not a point sample. A 330 m machine point-sampling 2 m terrain vertices will bob. Averaging also collapses most of the tier resolution difference, because a footprint mean over region data approximates what a correct disc texel would say.

**Q3 — Meridian's origin on the disc.** `SITE_Y, SITE_X = 971, 1821` — the **centre** texel on the 2017² disc. **Note the (Y, X) order; getting it backwards puts Meridian on the opposite side of the disc.**

```
disc: 2017 texels across 2500 km        -> 1239.4646 m per texel
disc centre texel (1008, 1008)          -> odd N, exact centre
Meridian centre: texel (row 971, col 1821)
                 disc metres  east +1,007,685   south -45,860
                 1008.7 km from disc centre, 80.7% to the rim, bearing -2.6 deg
region spans 6.454 texels: rows 967.77-974.23, cols 1817.77-1824.23

disc metres -> region metres     region_x_m = disc_east_m  - 1007684.7 + 4000
                                 region_y_m = disc_south_m +   45860.2 + 4000
in-region test                   0 <= region_x_m <= 8000 && same for y
region metres -> region texel    col = region_x_m / 1.960784 ; row = region_y_m / 1.960784
region metres -> unreal world uu world_x_uu = (region_x_m - 4000) x 100
                                 world_y_uu = (region_y_m - 4000) x 100
metres above sea -> world Z      world_z_uu = -3400 + m x 100
16-bit sample -> metres          m = (u16 - 32768) x 0.03124952
```

**⚠ The disc's own origin convention is an inference, not a documented fact.** Texel (1008, 1008) as disc centre with a 1250 km radius is consistent with every number in the carve script, but it is **not written down anywhere. Requires validation before it ships in a sampler.**

On the landscape actor: the import rule doc says location `(0, 0, −3400)`; the actor's own transform measures `(−399999.1, −399999.1, −3400)`. **Both are correct** — the actor sits at the region's north-west corner and the region is centred on the world origin, extent −4000..+4000 m. **Use the centred convention.**

**Q4 — how samples reach C++.** **Ruled: a baked `UDataAsset` holding a `TArray<uint16>`.** CPU-readable, deterministic on server and client, no texture streaming or compression in the path, cooks predictably. `Terrain/png_to_r16.py` already exists.

**With a mandatory validation header**, which is the part that matters given the stale-heightmap mess above:

| Field | Why |
|---|---|
| `SourceFile` | which PNG this came from |
| `ContentHash` | **so a stale bake fails loudly** |
| `SamplesPerSide`, `MetresPerSample` | no hard-coded 4081 or 1.9608 |
| `DiscOriginTexelX/Y` | the 971 / 1821 above, as data not a constant |
| `SeaLevelUnit` (32768), `MetresPerUnit` | the two magic numbers |

Then `SRFSpec` check 35 asserts against a **known hash**, not merely a known coordinate — the tripwire fires on a stale bake as well as on a flat Z. **Better the build breaks than a hull floats.**

---

### 3E-3. 🔴 STAGE A IS ALREADY WRITTEN IN PYTHON — AND IT IS BUILT ON THE RETIRED BEAM

`rfa_hull_conformance_mirror.py` exists in project knowledge, dated **31 Aug 2026, 03:22** — the newest artefact of the weekend. It mirrors the Stage A interface **before the C++**, per standing discipline: two-tier selection (`in_region` half-open on the far edge so abutting regions cannot both claim a point), `sample_region` / `sample_disc` both routed through the **existing** `sample_heightmap_bilinear`, a `blend_weight` on normalised inset distance, a least-squares `fit_plane` returning `(a, b, c)` with `pitch = atan(a)` / `roll = atan(b)` and **yaw never touched**, and `hull_conformance` returning `(z, pitch, roll, residual)`.

**It is well built.** Stage A ships a **one-point contact set per class** so behaviour is provably identical to today (**G05** asserts the single-point path reproduces the existing sample *exactly*, tolerance 0.0). The fit **fails closed** to a level plane at the mean on a degenerate system (**G12**, **G13**), it is **order-independent** (**G11**), it is a **pure function** across 200 identical calls (**G29**), and a straddled discontinuity leaves a **large residual — the signal that the passability data is wrong, not the fit** (**G24**). The honesty note in the file is exemplary: only the Grindwalker's Stage B contact set is derived from canon; the other five are **shaped correctly for their locomotion but their offsets are placeholders**, marked as such, and **no check asserts their values — only their shape.**

> **🔴 The defect: three of its checks assert the retired 420 m beam.** The file was written **ten hours after** the beam revision and did not receive it.
>
> | Check | Asserts | Must become |
> |---|---|---|
> | `GW_BEAM_M`, `GW_HULL_W_M`, `GW_GAP_M` | 420 / 100 / 220 | **330 / 90 / 150** |
> | **G15** | *"the hull is 2.26× longer than wide"* | **2.88×** |
> | **G16** | *"100 + 220 + 100 = 420"* | **90 + 150 + 90 = 330** |
> | **G17** | *"contact points sit on each hull centreline at ±160 m"* | **±120 m** (`(150 + 90) / 2`) |
>
> `_GW_LAT` is computed as `(GAP + HULL_W) / 2`, so it corrects automatically once the three constants move; **G15, G16 and G17 assert the old literals and will fail loudly, which is the correct behaviour.** Fix the constants, fix the three literals, and **re-derive the roll limits** — the plane fit now sees a third more roll for the same terrain (§3D).

**A second, smaller discrepancy, unreconciled and worth a ruling:** the mirror computes `DISC_M_PER_TEXEL = 2,500,000 / (2017 − 1) = 1240.079 m`, while the terrain session — reading the actual carve script — measured **1239.4646 m**, i.e. `2,500,000 / 2017`. **That is an off-by-one in the sample-spacing convention (`N − 1` intervals versus `N` cells) and it propagates into every disc↔region coordinate conversion in §3E-1.** It is 0.6 m per texel and therefore invisible at hull scale, and it is **not** invisible over 1,008 km of disc. **The two sides must agree on one convention before anything is baked.** Related: the mirror's `G02` accepts *either* 2.0 or 1.9608 m per vertex, which is a tolerance that should be tightened to the real value once the convention is settled.

**Status: 31 logic checks, no in-engine verification, no C++.** The file's own closing note says it plainly: *"no engine profiling and no in-editor verification. These are logic checks only. Compiled ≠ loaded in editor ≠ verified in PIE."*

---

### 3E-2. PASSABILITY REGION DATA — format ruled, authoring open

`CogheimNetcode/Public/RFAPassability.h` is **built, 24/24 in Automation and 24/24 in the Python mirror.** It answers "can this hull class take this route" and **nothing in any level currently gives it an answer.**

**Locomotion bits. STABLE — baked region data will encode them. Renumbering silently invalidates every authored region.**

| Bit | Value | Class |
|---|---|---|
| `Wheeled` | 1 | Grindwalker — twin-hull catamaran |
| `HalfTrack` | 2 | Drifter — front wheels, rear tracks |
| `Tracked` | 4 | Blackjack — caterpillar |
| `MultiTrack` | 8 | Juggernaut — multiple tracks at base |
| `DistributedTrack` | 16 | Scavenger — many small ski-like units |
| `Legged` | 32 | Vector — 6–8 massive legs |

A region's passability is one `int32` mask. Two reserved values: **`−1` Unmapped** (nobody authored this) and **`0` Impassable** (someone decided nothing may pass). Both block; **only the first is a bug**, and QA must be able to tell them apart. Zero is the fail-closed default, so forgetting to author a region can never grant passage.

Conveniences: `All`=63, `AnyTracked`=30, `AnyWheeled`=3, `SoftGround`=24, `UnevenGround`=48, `SoftAndUneven`=16, `BigGapOnly`=32, `StraddleOnly`=1.

API: `IsPassable`, `ClassifyRegion`, `ValidateCourse` (returns the index of the **first** blocking region, so a captain sees *where* a route fails), `FurthestLegalIndex` (the last legal region before the block, so rejection moves you to the edge of what you can do rather than simply refusing). **Check P23 asserts no locomotion's capability set is a superset of another's** — class dominance is prevented by assertion, not by balance intent.

**Why authored, not derived — founder-locked.** At 1,240 m per texel a gradient cannot distinguish a cliff from a ramp, and it can never express the thing that actually matters: *a boulder field a Vector walks through stops a Blackjack outright.* The design payoff is that hull class becomes a **map-access decision** rather than a speed-and-capacity stat: some routes are legged-only, some are straddle-only, and chokepoints become authored 4X geography that can be **asymmetric**.

**Format — recommended, in the same asset family as the elevation field. Not level geometry, not Data Layers, not spline volumes**, because `ValidateCourse` must be answerable on a dedicated server with nothing streamed, for any frame.

| Level | Cell | Cells | Size |
|---|---|---|---|
| Disc base | 1,239 m (one disc texel) | 2017² | 16 MB int32 → **4 MB paletted `uint8`** |
| Region patch | **62.5 m** | 128² per 8 km region | **64 KB per region** |

62.5 m is chosen against the hull, not arbitrarily: a 330 m beam covers ~5 cells, so a chokepoint can be authored **narrower than a Strider** and actually block one. At 1,239 m a chokepoint cannot exist.

**Authoring — recommended:** a palette-indexed **PNG per region**, painted in any image editor, baked to the asset by Python. No editor mode. PNG → Python → data asset is the pipeline already in daily use here. An editor mode is right eventually, at a hundred regions; it is the wrong answer for proving the format.

**Static now, structured for runtime later:** baked base immutable; a **sparse runtime overlay** (`cell index → new mask`) when the Iron Veil or the Memory Engine needs to change passability. Authoring-source PNGs live under an **Editor** Data Layer; the baked asset is not a Data Layer at all; runtime changes switch a **Runtime** Data Layer *and* write the overlay — world state and passability state must move together or they will disagree.

**Scope, agreed by both sessions: format plus ONE authored region.** One ridge, one straddle-only gap, one legged-only crevasse, one soft debris field, and a course plotted through each with two hull classes. That answers whether route asymmetry reads as **geography** or as an **arbitrary gate**, which is the only question that matters before authoring at scale. **Acceptance adds one thing: a captain who is refused must be able to see why.** If it reads as "the game said no" rather than "the ridge is too steep for tracks", the geography has failed even if the code passes.

**⚠ `Meridian_Features.json` (16 KB) can seed part of it** — 24 waterfalls, 24 lakes with areas, 3,541 river cells, 14 river polylines, the Wingwater easter egg, biome mix and region metadata, plus five layer weightmaps. That is enough to derive the **unambiguous** cases only: standing water, river channels, and slopes beyond any locomotion's limit. **Whether a derived seed may do so, with everything else left `−1`, is founder open item 4.**

**⚠ Straddle authoring must use 150 m, not 220 m.** Nothing shipped is invalidated because no passability data exists yet, but any straddle route sketched against the old 220 m gap needs rechecking (§3D).

## 3F. TERRAIN GENERATORS & THE ARCHITECTURE→TERRAIN HANDOFF (3 September 2026)

**Source:** `claude/COGHEIM_Handoff_Architecture_to_Terrain_Sep3_2026.md`, `claude/COGHEIM_Terrain_Generators_README.md`. Eight decisions answered (D1–D8), two escalations raised, eight items escalated to founder.

### 3F-1. 🔴 D7 — THE GENERATOR SCRIPTS ARE THE WORLD

> `generate_world_terrain.py` and `carve_meridian.py` **are the world.** Everything downstream — the disc map, every region, every bake, the elevation asset, the passability grid — is a derivative of those files and a seed.

**They are source, not build artefacts. Commit them.** Losing them makes the world unreproducible and every region a one-off. `meridian_finish_v2.py` and `generate_vegetation.py` are covered by the same rule.

**Seed: `25260817`.** One seed for everything. Changing it regenerates a different world and requires a founder ruling.

### 3F-2. THE PIPELINE, AS BUILT

| Script | Produces |
|---|---|
| `generate_world_terrain.py` | `Cogheim_Heightmap_2017.png` (2,500 km disc) + 5 weight layers |
| `carve_meridian.py` | `Meridian_Heightmap_4081.png` raw + 5 weights + `Meridian_Features.json` |
| `meridian_finish_v2.py` | `Meridian_Heightmap_4081_v2.png` / `.r16` (ships), 5 weights v2, `Meridian_Landmarks.json`, `meridian_v2_manifest.json` |
| `generate_vegetation.py <name>` | `SM_Cogheim_<name>.fbx`, **one mesh per process** — `read_factory_settings()` twice in one process segfaults |

**Reproducibility is verified, not assumed.** `meridian_finish_v2.py` was rebuilt from three interactive scripts and every shipped output reproduces byte-identically; `Meridian_Landmarks.json` matches with one deliberately added field (`glyph_status`). Every run writes a manifest carrying the seed, library versions and a SHA-256 per input and output. **A bake that disagrees with the manifest is a stale bake.** Runtime ~6 minutes at 4081²; the priority-flood depression fill is deliberately single-threaded for determinism.

**One bug the hash check caught, and the reason the check exists.** The first consolidation derived layer weights from the **float** working array. Wrong: slope and flow accumulation must come from the **quantised 16-bit heightmap**, because that is the surface the landscape is actually built from. Sub-unit differences cascaded through the flow routing and moved wetlands from 9.9% to 17.2% of land. **A visual check would not have caught this.**

### 3F-3. 🔴 MEASURED DEFECT — MERIDIAN DOES NOT MATCH ITS OWN DISC FOOTPRINT

`carve_meridian.py` samples a **±12 texel (31 km) disc window and squashes it 3.87× into 8 km.** The region is therefore not a refinement of its own footprint. **Measured disagreement: 203 m mean, 422 m worst.**

- **Meridian is NOT to be re-carved.** That would destroy founder-approved terrain including the Wingwater and the mark.
- **Architecture gate 3 for region two:** future regions sample a **footprint-sized 6.45-texel window.**

### 3F-4. THE CANONICAL COORDINATE CHAIN

```
disc          2017 texels / 2,500 km      1239.4646 m per texel
disc centre   texel (1008, 1008)          INFERRED — requires validation
Meridian      texel (row 971, col 1821)   note the (Y, X) order
region        4081 samples / 8,000 m      1.960784 m per sample
height        m = (u16 - 32768) * 0.03124952
world Z       uu = SEA_LEVEL_UU + m * 100
```

**Escalation 1: `SEA_LEVEL_UU = -3400` must become one authored project constant** read by the landscape transform, the water actor, spawn placement and the netcode Z conversion. Until then each file carries its own copy and the manifest records it. **A duplicated constant that drifts is how a world ends up with two sea levels.**

**This chain reconciles against §3E-3's known discrepancy** — the hull conformance mirror computes 2,500,000/2016 = 1240.079 m/texel while the carve script uses 2,500,000/2017 = 1239.4646. **Still open. The mirror is the file that is wrong.**

### 3F-5. D1–D8 AS RULED

| # | Decision | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Vegetation placement | **Grass Output for ground cover; PCG for actors.** Two systems, one boundary |
| D2 | Region-to-map mapping | **One region per map** |
| D3 | Ocean | **Per-region ocean actor reading one project constant.** Not a global body |
| D4 | Versioned materials | **Build artefacts. Delete on promote.** A `_v2` that survives promotion is a defect |
| D5 | Landscape layers | **Fixed 5 layers, every region, no exceptions.** The 32-sampler wall is the reason and it is not negotiable per-region |
| D6 | Nanite | **ON for rocks, OFF for foliage.** LODs gate density |
| D7 | Generators | **Source. Commit them.** See 3F-1 |
| D8 | Bake vs place | **Baked if terrain, placed if geometry** |

**Withdrawn:** the two-tier runtime sampler, in favour of **one composite baked field.** Simpler, and it removes a runtime cost from a system that does not need one.

### 3F-6. KNOWN DEFECT — THE GLYPH IN THE FINISH SCRIPT

**The glyph in `meridian_finish_v2.py` is a design proposal, not canon.** Swap `STROKES` and `RING` when the real Einwechtëran mark is approved. **A placeholder mark baked into shipped terrain is a canon leak waiting to happen** — the language is now public (§21), which makes an unapproved inscription in the ground a live risk rather than a cosmetic one.

### 3F-7. OWNERSHIP AND SEQUENCE

**The terrain/level session owns:** `Terrain/`, `Content/Landscape/`, `Content/Foliage/`, `Content/Textures/Cogheim/`, `Maps/Regions/`, `Maps/Disc/`, the generators, and the elevation and passability bakes.

**Meridian finish order:** 12 items, in the handoff document. **Second region recommendation: Coldforge Landing.**

**Eight items escalated to founder — carried into §37:** the disc↔region relationship; the land:ocean ratio (three contradicting figures on record); the derived passability seed; whether Meridian is the Pale Reach build continuing; which biomes Pale Reach contains; the scrub tint; and the two constants above.

## 4B. THE IRON TABLE (War Table) — Command Bridge System (Founder Lock, July 11 + 14, 2026)

**Status:** Fully specced ✓ | C# reference build complete, 7 subsystems ✓ | **Spec-complete, implementation-stale** — pending UE5.8 C++ port, same status class as Iron Frame pre-rebuild (§13B)

**Naming note:** "Iron Table" is the in-fiction/marketing name; "War Table" is the original session/system name. Same system — this was corrected mid-World-Map-session after an initial (incorrect) treatment of it as new canon. It isn't. It was fully built July 11, 2026.

**Location:** Deck 13, Command Bridge. A **physical lit relief model** at the center of the room: a raised circular plate roughly 20 meters across, carrying a solid relief model of terrain and settlement at its centre, with a lit chart surface around it. Visitor access **closed by default**.

> ### OVERTURNED 4 SEPTEMBER 2026 — THE WAR TABLE IS NOT A HOLOGRAM
> **Overturns:** the Founder Lock of 11 and 14 July 2026, which specified "a 20-meter circular holo-surface".
> **Replaced by:** a physical lit relief model, as described above.
> **Why:** this resolves a three way contradiction inside the Index. §4B locked a hologram, §4C.7 recommended a relief model, and §33B-1 forbade holograms outright. `COGHEIM_Bridge_and_ExteriorFacing_Interiors_Ruling_Aug31_2026.md` §7 is now canon rather than recommendation.
> **Closes:** §4C.10 open decision 4, and §37 item 11.

**Named crew stations:** **First Officer — VACANT, see below** / Danielle (Navigation) / Joel (Tactical) / Astin (Sensor) / Louis (Comms).

**⚠ DEFECT, 4 September 2026.** This list was stale where it named **Gina** as First Officer. Gina is the **Scavenger Greeter** under the §12B-7 slate and cannot hold both postings. The First Officer station needs a new occupant. Danielle, Joel, Astin and Louis are unaffected.

**Structural clarification (locked):** Docking/Diplomacy and Repair/Recovery are **not separate rooms** — they're additional computer stations within the same Deck 13 Command Bridge space.

**Seven locked subsystems (all built as C# service classes + NUnit tests, composing off a single shared Strider entity):**
| # | System | Function |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Navigation Command** | Plot march course, destination/speed, live ETA. Standard mode; Overdrive mode hits higher speed but doubles resource costs and adds Corethrum draw. **Overdrive automatically forces Running Lights off** — makes the Strider vulnerable while sprinting. Balance numbers tunable, not final-locked |
| 2 | **Crew Command** | Assign minor roles (Helmsman, Fire Operator), standing orders, visitor access toggle |
| 3 | **Tactical Overlay** | Two-tier sensor detection, Blackout Order (requires **both** Signal Chief AND Commander co-sign), intel sharing |
| 4 | **Combat Authority** | 3-person firing crew gate (Commander + Iron Chief + Fire Operator). **Iron Chief veto is Colossus-weapon-only** — does not extend to crew appointments. General Quarters toggle |
| 5 | **Battlefield Proximity** | Collateral damage with linear falloff by distance from blast center. Colossus shots exempt from friendly fire **at launch** (may revisit post-launch) |
| 6 | **Fleet Autopilot** | Patrol is default behavior for unpiloted units; behavior preserved across pilot toggles |
| 7 | **City Capture** | 2-city cap per Strider. Iron Veil holding is **mutually exclusive** with claiming a second city — enforced by flat rejection, not auto-release of the first |

**Deliverable:** Navigation storyboard, 7 panels, built to Master Style Lock V2 base clause for external image generation.

**Deferred, explicitly not decided here:** Whether to expand the 8-role Council (§12/§23) with a separate Senate layer of up to 16 members — founder's instinct references Final Fantasy: New Empire governance models. **Flagged as a major canon decision requiring its own dedicated session**, correctly not resolved mid-bridge-build.
