## 4C. BRIDGES & EXTERIOR-FACING INTERIORS (Founder ruling, 31 Aug 2026)

**Status: FOUNDER-RULED design and art direction. NOTHING has been modelled, built, imported, compiled or tested.** No Unreal asset exists for any bridge of any class. `L_Strider_Grindwalker` remains an empty 8,533-byte stub.

### 4C.1 The ruling

**Every bridge is an interior that occupies exterior space. The sightline to the real moving world is mandatory, not decorative.**

Founder reasoning, recorded in substance: *you have to see what is attacking you.*

This promotes the window from set dressing to a **gameplay instrument**. The view out of a Strider bridge is tactical information delivered physically rather than through a HUD — the diegetic pillar doing real work instead of being asserted. The ruling defines a class of space:

> **Exterior-facing interiors.** Any inhabited, walkable interior volume with a real, unfaked sightline to the simulated world outside the hull.

Founder-named members beyond the bridge: **vehicle deployment areas, air-vehicle deployment areas, outdoor town squares on deck.** **The list is open. More will be added.**

### 4C.2 The three sightline classes

Author every Strider space as exactly one. They cost wildly different amounts and obey different rules.

| Class | Sightline | Examples | Cost and consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Sealed** | None | quarters, holds, machinery spaces, corridors | Cheap. Occlusion culling works normally. **The default** |
| **Glazed** | Through glass | command bridge, observation, gunnery, wardroom | Expensive. Two-way culling problem. **Forces §4C.6** |
| **Open** | Open to air | deployment bays, air launch decks, deck squares, promenades, rail galleries | Expensive, plus weather, wind and fall volumes |

**Sealed is the default and must stay the majority of authored volume.** Glazed and Open are deliberate, budgeted choices, not a mood applied everywhere.

### 4C.3 The reference set — explicitly incomplete

Three references supplied by the founder in session, 31 Aug 2026. **The founder has stated more are coming.** ⚠ The image files could not be stored — project knowledge was at ~1.42 MB of its 2.0 MB cap — so they are described here to be re-identifiable and **should be uploaded to the project directly.**

**Reference A — the hero. "Blackjack Class Strider, Command Bridge."** A circular room under a ribbed glazed dome:
- **A continuous ring of tall arched windows, a full 360°.** Mountains and cloud beyond, and the ship's own lattice superstructure visible through them — which is what sells it as a real vessel rather than a room with a painting in the window
- **A large raised circular table dead centre**, roughly half the room's diameter: a luminous chart plate with a **physical raised terrain and city model standing proud at its middle**
- **A ring of individual brass console desks around the table**, officers seated **facing inward**
- **The commander's station raised on a platform**, centred on the far side, table between him and the crew
- A brass pendant with a cyan lens on the dome centreline; inlaid timber floor with a compass rose; a brass celestial globe on a stand
- Wall panels cast with `BLACKJACK CLASS STRIDER`, `COMMAND BRIDGE`, `VICTORY IS EARNED`
- **Palette: warm amber lamplight and brass against cold cyan off the table** — the locked world palette contrast, arriving for free

**Reference B — the zoning. "Command and Control, Deck 21."** Useful for exactly one thing: it names **four functions sharing one volume** — Command Bridge (navigation and tactical control), War Room (strategic planning), Comms Center (communications and signals), Battle Deck (coordinating air, land and sea).

> **⚠ CONTAMINATION WARNING. Take the room from this reference and discard the entire specification sheet around it.** The sheet carries content that is **not Cogheim** and must never be quoted into a design document, a brief, or an investor deck: the Statue of Liberty, United States flags, 12,000 crew, four primary reactors, a satcom array, drone control, tactical AI assist, 120 main battle tanks, multi-fuel jet turbine plant. Cogheim is post-apocalyptic 2526, grounded steampunk, ~200–300 players per Strider, **no satellites, no drones, no AI assist anywhere in the art direction.** The **layout** is useful. **Every number and system name on that sheet is foreign material.**

**Reference C — the small class. "Command Deck."** The humble version: a large **physical ship's wheel and telegraph mechanism** as the dominant object in place of a war table, a bench of instrument consoles, a **wide horizontal band of windows**, warm lamps, dark iron, **no cyan at all**.

### 4C.4 The per-class ladder — RECOMMENDATION, REQUIRES FOUNDER APPROVAL

The three references are the same room at three scales. Using them as a ladder makes hull size legible from inside the ship.

| Tier | Room | Dominant object | Ref | Fits |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Command Deck** | ship's wheel and telegraph | C | Drifter, Scavenger |
| 2 | **Command Bridge** | circular war table + inward-facing console ring | A | Grindwalker, Vector |
| 3 | **Command and Control** | zoned: bridge, war room, comms, battle deck | B | Blackjack, Juggernaut |

**The production consequence is good news and should be stated plainly.** The Alpha's hull is the Drifter. Under this ladder the Drifter gets **Tier 1, the wheelhouse** — by far the cheapest, and it needs no war table at all. **The expensive hero bridge is not Alpha-critical.**

| Item | Classification |
|---|---|
| Tier 1 Command Deck for the Drifter | **Alpha-supporting** |
| Tier 2 Command Bridge for the Grindwalker | **Post-Alpha** |
| Tier 3 Command and Control | **Full-production** |
| The war table as a shared reusable asset | **Post-Alpha** |

### 4C.5 The Grindwalker's bridge is already located

Locked interior canon places it — Grindwalker Interior Spec V1.0, founder-locked 23 July 2026: **Commander — A1, Crown Bridge.** So the Reference A room is **A1, the Crown Bridge, in the Arch.** Dimensionally comfortable: at the 150 m straddle gap a circular bridge of that kind is nowhere near constrained.

**🔴 OPEN — the arch deck numbering direction is not stated in any document read.** Arch decks A1–A8 span Z 60 m to 130 m at 10 m pitch. Whether **A1 is the top deck (130 m) or the bottom (60 m)** is undefined, and it decides whether the Crown Bridge looks down on the world from the highest point of the ship or sits directly above the straddle gap. **Both are good. They are not the same. Requires founder ruling.** ⚠ The Grindwalker generator v1 prompt (§6) has already been written assuming **A1 = top**, with the dome apex at 150 m. That is a working assumption and is flagged as such.

### 4C.6 🔴 WHAT THIS RULING BREAKS — and it is not small

**It makes the deferred interior rotation visible.** Founder Decision 2 of 30 Aug holds the interior frame level while the exterior may pitch, with rotating SRF held at identity until M3, and calls that the correct build order.

**A window makes that visible and wrong.** If the hull pitches and the interior floor does not, the window aperture — which is hull skin — pitches away from the floor the player is standing on. **The geometry separates at exactly the place the player is looking.** Reference A is the worst case in the roster, because its windows run a full 360° and the horizon is visible in every direction at once.

Every Sealed space can ignore this forever. **No Glazed space can.**

**🔴 FOUNDER DECISION REQUIRED. Two options, no third:**

| Option | Consequence |
|---|---|
| **Bring rotating SRF forward from M3** | Solves it properly. Costs schedule on a system that was deliberately deferred |
| **Glazed spaces only while hull pitch is zero** | Costs nothing now. Means the bridge is correct on flat ground and wrong the moment the ship crests anything — which is precisely when a commander would be looking out |

**It gives up occlusion, in both directions.** The cheapest standard technique — fully occlude the exterior while the player is inside — is now unavailable for Glazed and Open spaces, and the reverse also holds: these interiors **must not be culled when viewed from outside the hull.** This is the concrete reason behind Level Architecture Whitepaper Rule 8, *set HLOD and streaming budgets before content, not after*. **Reference A is the worst case in the roster and should be the budget's test scene.**

**It raises a shader and permutation risk.** Glass, interior lamps, exterior atmosphere and the table's emissive all overlap in one small screen area. **One master material with parameters, skin and decal split. Do not author a bespoke glass material per class.**

### 4C.7 The war table, and what makes the light

Reference A answers most of the question left open earlier. The table is **not a screen and not a floating hologram.** It is a physical object: a raised circular plate carrying a **solid relief model of terrain and settlement** at its centre, with a lit chart surface around it.

**Recommendation, requires founder approval:** lock that reading explicitly. A physical lit relief model is steampunk-legal without special pleading — it can be damaged, repaired, walked around, leaned on, and altered by a crew member. A hologram can do none of those and drifts toward the sleek futurism the art direction explicitly rejects.

**A second finding worth acting on.** A separate gallery image shows **the same disc map inside a brass-rimmed handheld lens.** If adopted, the commander's war table and a crew member's pocket instrument become **the same object at two scales** — a complete diegetic system arriving for free, which is what MAXIOM is supposed to be, and which connects to the existing `CogheimWorldMap` module and the Tier 0 Disc Record rather than requiring new systems. **🔴 OPEN: whether the handheld and the table are formally one system.**

### 4C.8 🔴 The runway conflict

Gallery images show aircraft launching and landing on a deck running **through the gap between the two catamaran hulls**, with the arch spanning overhead. Locked Grindwalker exterior canon (§6) says something different: a **forward-projecting runway causeway** with a phased 3-stage breach point at its circular tip, vehicles and watercraft launching from its underbelly, the tip doubling as the Black-Maw Station docking interface.

These may be the same structure from different angles, or two incompatible answers to where aircraft launch from. **Requires founder ruling.**

### 4C.9 Interior versus exterior — the standing clarification

Folded in at founder request because three separate sessions have had to re-derive it. **Nothing here is new; this restates existing locks in one place.**

**The Strider interior is separate from the outside world as a *server authority*, and is NOT separate as a world, level, or player experience.**

| Aspect | Separate? | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **Server authority** | **Yes.** Strider Authority owns the interior frame and the hull transform | RFA founder lock 2 |
| **Server process** | **Not at Alpha.** One World Authority + one Strider Authority, logically separated, physically co-located | RFA founder lock 3 |
| Server process, post-Alpha | Yes. Multi-process is lock 4, deferred | RFA founder lock 4 |
| **Client world** | **No.** One continuous world | Level Architecture Whitepaper §6 |
| **Level and streaming** | **No.** One persistent level per class, interiors as Level Instances | Six-tier world model, Tiers 3–4 |
| **Player experience at the hull boundary** | **No transition.** No loading screen, no portal | Whitepaper §6, rejected alternatives |

**The one-sentence version: the hull boundary is an authority handoff, not a level transition.**

Cogheim splits the authority without splitting the world. Most MMOs that shard per zone also cut the world and charge the player a loading screen. What makes this possible is SRF: interior characters replicate in a stationary local frame composited onto the hull transform, so the Strider Authority owns the whole interior simulation in its own coordinate space while the client streams both at once.

**Untested.** `SRFInteriorCharacterComponent` carries an atomic boarding and disembark handoff, and Whitepaper V2 Prototype 2 is the direct test of it. **Nothing has been proven.**

### 4C.10 Open decisions from this ruling

| # | Decision | Blocks |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Rotating SRF forward from M3, **or** Glazed spaces restricted to zero hull pitch | All Glazed authoring |
| 2 | Approve the per-class ladder, or assign rooms differently | Alpha art scope |
| 3 | Arch deck numbering direction — A1 top or bottom | Crown Bridge placement; the generator contract |
| 4 | Lock the war table as a physical lit relief model | The whole bridge art brief |
| 5 | Are the war table and the handheld disc lens one system | MAXIOM scope |
| 6 | Runway conflict — causeway, straddle-gap launch, or both | Grindwalker exterior |
| 7 | Is the four-zone Command and Control breakdown canon, or reference only | Tier 3 scope |

## 4D. VECTOR & GRINDWALKER SILHOUETTES — FOUNDER-LOCKED (2 September 2026)

**Sources:** `claude/COGHEIM_Vector_Silhouette_LOCKED_Sep2_2026.md`, `claude/COGHEIM_Grindwalker_Silhouette_LOCKED_Sep2_2026.md`, `claude/COGHEIM_Grindwalker_BareHull_Arch_Starboard_Pack_Sep2_2026.md`.

> **⚠ WHAT IS LOCKED IS THE SILHOUETTE. THE NUMBERS BEHIND IT ARE NOT ALL LOCKED, AND THREE OF THEM CONFLICT.**
> Both documents are titled LOCKED and both are founder-approved as *shape*. Reading them as dimensional canon is a mistake this section exists to prevent.

### 4D-1. VECTOR — LOCKED

- **Six legs, three per side**, at **15% / 50% / 85%** of hull length.
- **Splay is purely lateral.** No forward or aft rake. This is the distinguishing read against every other walker in the roster.
- **Belly clearance 28%** of hull height.

**🔴 Two unresolved scale conflicts, both material:**

1. **The elevations are compressed roughly 3× against the top plan.** The plan derives a **950 × 310 m body** inside a **950 × 554 m stride envelope**. The elevations do not agree with that. One of the two is wrong and the plan is the more defensible.
2. **The chassis and town bands are inverted** relative to the approved deck plan.
   **Proposed reconciliation, requires founder ruling:** belly 38 m · chassis 38 m · Face 10 m · Town 54 m · **11 decks.**

### 4D-2. GRINDWALKER — LOCKED

| | Value | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Overall vessel | **950 × 330 × 140 m** | **founder-locked, V46.** Beam split 90 / 150 / 90 |
| Wheels | **9 per hull, ~40 m diameter, 3 bogie groups of 3** | founder-locked |
| Hull / city split | **40 / 60** | founder-locked |
| Brass domes | **One only** — the Chronicle Hall, the sole civic ornament on either hull | founder-locked |
| Arches | cut ~20 m into the hull | founder-locked |
| Command centre | **The glass sphere at the arch crown. Sole bridge. The starboard hull has NO command deck** | founder-ruled 2 Sep |
| Per-hull beam | ~180 m | **DERIVED, NOT LOCKED** — and it is derived from the retired 420 m figure |
| Arch, galleries, crown sphere geometry | A1–A7 at 7 m, A8 crown sphere 28 m dia to 139 m | **PROPOSAL. No founder approval** |

### 4D-3. 🔴 THE DECK COUNT CONTRADICTION — HIGHEST-PRIORITY OPEN NUMBER

**Two founder-touched documents disagree:**

| Source | Figure |
|---|---|
| The V46 beam revision | **14 decks total, 7 + 7** |
| The 2 Sep silhouette lock | **8 decks per hull** |

**These cannot both be right and neither is safely inferable from the other.** Every interior brief, every module footprint and every walkable-volume estimate for the class depends on it. **Requires a founder ruling before any Grindwalker interior work proceeds.**

### 4D-4. 🔴 THE PER-HULL BEAM IS DERIVED FROM A RETIRED NUMBER

The prompt pack derives ~180 m per hull from **420 m total minus a 60 m gap.** **420 m is the retired beam** — V46 founder-locked it to **330 m, split 90 / 150 / 90.** The 90 m outer bands are the hulls and the 150 m is the centre; the ~180 m figure and the 60 m straddle gap both belong to the retired geometry.

**Consequence: every dimension in the bare-hull, arch and starboard prompt pack that references 420 m or a 60 m gap is built on a retired number and must be re-derived before any of those prompts is run again.** The silhouettes they produced are still valid as shape. **The numbers in the prompt text are not.**

### 4D-5. THE THREE DOCUMENTED STATES

Founder-ruled 2 Sep: the class needs three documented states, and the approved silhouette is the third.

1. **Bare founding hull** — everything below 56 m plus wheels, arch, sphere, doors, mooring gear, two engine vents, one deck crane, one signal mast. Above 56 m: **bare tiered structural deck plates**, railings, bolt-down footings, capped risers, painted module outlines. Empty. The arch and sphere are the tallest things by far and it reads as **visibly an unfinished home.**
2. **The module set** — **undefined.** The bare-hull absence list is its first draft: tenements, market hall, furnace towers, headframe, gasholders, cooling tower, silos, conveyor bridges, sawtooth workshops, hangar sheds, water tanks, the Chronicle dome, aft stacks. **Each needs its own asset brief and a specialty-research unlock before the growth system is buildable.**
3. **Built-out city** — the approved silhouette.

**Port and starboard are not mirrors.** Port is heavy metallurgy, ~60% plant / 40% town, angular. Starboard is provision and trade, ~60% town / 40% plant, rounded — silo clusters, market hall, airship mooring mast. **Round against angular at silhouette distance.**

**Open, requires approval:** the port furnace towers drop from 140 m to ~132 m so the command sphere crowns the ship. This edits an approved plate.

### 4D-6. TOOLING NOTE — TRIPO OUTPUT IS A BLOCKOUT

Generate the three orthographic views in one session, same style, scale, lighting and plain background. **Expect blobby topology, no interiors, no clean edges.** Use it to prove massing and proportion in engine, then retopologise. **It is not shippable geometry.** Model modules as separate generations, one per building type — asking for the built-out city as one mesh produces an unusable blob and loses the modular grammar the whole design depends on.

### 4D-7. TERRAIN LIMITS SPEC V2 — CORRECTIONS OUTSTANDING

Deck count 7–9 per side · wheel diameter 40 m · **the Drifter removed from the class list** · per-hull beam to be added once locked · the V3 cutaway prompt still says "Command Deck & Observatory" on starboard deck 8, which is now the grain elevator and Larder.

## 5B. STRIDER REFERENCE FRAME (SRF) NETCODE (Founder Lock, July 2026)

**Status:** Fully specced ✓ | **UE5.8 C++ port COMPLETE ✓** (July 14, 2026) — corrects prior versions showing this as pending

**⚠ Provenance note (important, do not lose this):** When the C++ port was built, the original C# reference source and its 31 individual test bodies were **not present** in that session's loaded project knowledge — only the §5B spec describing them. The delivered test suite is therefore a **spec-faithful reconstruction**, not a line-for-line translation of the original C# tests. Functionally equivalent, verified via a fresh Python logic mirror (31/31 passing) before the C++ was trusted, but flagged here so this distinction isn't lost the way the Iron Frame "complete" status was.

**The problem solved — FIGURES CORRECTED V45:** Players walk inside a massive Strider (**950 m × 330 m × 140 m** Grindwalker, §6 — V46 beam revision) that is itself translating through the disc slice at **14 km/h = 3.8889 m/s** (§4). Naive world-frame replication and sanity checks produce false positives — a genuinely standing player inside a moving Strider reads as moving at **3.89 m/s** in world coordinates.

> **V45 note.** This section carried `900×260×320` at `4 km/h (1.11 m/s)` through V44. Both were superseded long ago — §6 calls the old box *"confirmed placeholder only"* and §4 says the 14 km/h lock *"supersedes the old 4 km/h Grindwalker-only placeholder"* — and §5B's prose was the last copy of the retired figures. `SRFTypes.h` carried the same stale constants and was corrected in the relevancy patch (half-extents 450/130/160 → Z half-extent 160 m → 70 m). **The load-bearing proof gets stronger, not weaker:** a faster hull makes the world-frame false reading larger, so the local-frame choice is more load-bearing after the retarget. **1.11 m/s is kept as a labelled regression anchor in `RFANetcodeSpec`, not as a live figure.** Consequence to watch: `ServerAdvance()` now moves 3.5× further per tick.

**Approach locked: Approach A — the Strider Reference Frame system.** Interior characters replicate in a stationary **local frame** composited onto the Strider's world transform, rather than replicating in absolute world coordinates against a moving parent (Approach B, rejected).

**Why Approach A won:**
1. **Prediction/reconciliation cleanliness** — characters simulate in a stationary local frame, so server correction deltas are uncontaminated by base motion
2. **Future sharding compatibility** — interior state is position-independent, making Strider migration between server processes a single transform swap, consistent with the "one Strider = one authoritative server unit" seed shape for the 500-Strider endgame

**System components (delivered as 5 header/source pairs):**
- `SRFLibrary` — pure Blueprint Function Library (core math)
- `SRFStriderTransformComponent` — low-frequency Strider world-transform replication channel
- `SRFInteriorCharacterComponent` — local-frame character replication channel, CMC integration hooks, atomic boarding/disembark handoff
- `SRFReplicationNode` — per-Strider Replication Graph relevancy cluster + World Partition streaming source composition
- `SRFTypes.h` + `SRFSpec.cpp` (31 UE Automation Spec checks across 8 describe blocks) + `CogheimSRF.Build.cs` + Python mirror + README
- **Critical rule preserved:** all sanity checks (speed clamp, teleport detection, interior bounds) run in the **local frame**, never world frame

**Load-bearing proof (empirically verified, checks 04–07):** World-frame speed checks falsely read the hull speed on a genuinely standing player (**3.89 m/s** at the locked 14 km/h; the original 1.11 m/s figure is retained in the spec as a labelled regression anchor); local-frame checks correctly read zero. This is the reason Approach A is not optional.

**Baseline:** Server-authoritative via UE5's Character Movement Component with client prediction/reconciliation and basic sanity checks (speed clamps, teleport/no-clip detection, server-validated interactions). Full anti-cheat and economy fraud detection explicitly excluded at this stage.

**Open items (flagged for future sessions):**
- Boarding handoff timestamp precision — tick-precise vs. slightly smoothed; relevant once disc-edge geometry is introduced (M2)
- Rotating SRF / banking physics — math is already built and tested, held at identity until M3 (hull locomotion, now retargeted 4→14 km/h, §4)
- ✅ **The broader server-model netcode implementation spec is no longer unspecced** — see §5C, Reference-Frame Authority (RFA), which generalizes SRF into the full replication architecture

---

## 5C. REFERENCE-FRAME AUTHORITY (RFA) NETCODE MODEL (Founder Lock, July 20, 2026)

**Status:** Spec drafted and 6 founder locks made ✓ | Python logic mirror 32/32 passing ✓ | Pass 1 (Blueprint Function Library layer) built, and now **compiled, linked and loaded in-editor** ✓ | **🔴 V45 CORRECTION: PASS 2a DOES NOT EXIST.** The claim that a transition FSM, rewind ring and snapshot cadence scheduler were "built + validated 38/38" is **false and survived V41, V43 and V44.** The full `Source/` tree was enumerated twice and contains none of those three components, in `Source/`, `_ManualIntegration/`, `_ModuleDocs/` or `Scripts/`; `CogheimNetcode.Build.cs` says so in its own header. **Pass 2 as a whole had not begun.** | **Pass 2b gate CLOSED** — the `CogheimSRF` headers were read, `CogheimSRF` now depends on `CogheimNetcode`, and the result compiles, links and initializes. Pass 2 is blocked on **founder decisions, not artifacts**
**Resolves:** the previously-open "netcode implementation spec" item (§37 Open Founder Decisions) — no longer open, now an active build track
**Depends on:** SRF Netcode (§5B) — RFA *generalizes* SRF; SRF becomes the first instance of the RFA pattern rather than a special case
**Source doc:** `COGHEIM_Netcode_Spec_RFA_V1.0_DRAFT.md`
**Model routing:** Sonnet for the spec/design pass (done); Opus 4.8 for all C++ (authority handoff, relevancy graph, per-frame rewind buffers) — multi-system, interdependent, sits directly on top of SRF

**The core bet:** stop organizing netcode around world position — organize it around reference frames. Classic MMO interest management assumes zones stand still, so it's a static world grid. COGHEIM's zones are 900m fortress-cities crawling across a disc and clustering during combat, so a static grid fights the design the whole way. RFA generalizes the one interior hack SRF already proved (§5B) into the universal primitive for the **entire** netcode stack — authority, relevancy, prediction, lag compensation, quantization, persistence, and crash containment all key off frames.

**Why this decomposes the scale problem — AMENDED V44, founder-approved, supersedes the original wording:** 150,000 players stop being one bottleneck and become 500 embarrassingly-parallel ~300-player interior simulations, **plus a shared world root holding the hulls themselves and whatever fraction of players are off-hull at any moment — not only a few thousand hulls.** The original wording of this paragraph claimed the shared octree "only ever holds a few thousand hulls — never 150k characters." **That was wrong at target scale and is corrected here.** It holds for Alpha, where there is one World Authority and one Strider Authority co-located and off-hull population is trivial. It does not hold at 150,000, because off-hull play is a supported regional playstyle (traversal ruling 1, §3C) and was already locked design in three places — the running-lights protection zone exists explicitly so Iron Frames and salvage crews can work outside the hull (§12A), the Explorer/Scholar charter wins territory by finding and marking ruins (§12A), and 30 underwater sites are submarine-gated (§3A).

**The sizing number, LOCKED V44:** **10% off-hull design target, 25% stress ceiling.** At the 150,000 target that is 15,000 concurrent players in the shared root, stress-tested to 37,500. The RFA load-test matrix is sized against these two figures.

**Why this is a design answer and not a walkback.** Off-hull players do not scatter uniformly across 4.9 million km². They cluster inside the excursion bands of 35 fixed anchors and up to 500 mobile ones, because that is the only place there is anything to do or any way home (§3C). **Root partitioning therefore has an obvious shape: partition the shared root around anchors.** An anchor and its 16 km Sensor Envelope is a natural authority cell with a natural population bound, exactly the way a Strider interior already is. **Anchor-partitioned root is recorded as the intended post-Alpha scheme** — it stays deferred for Alpha, but the scheme the deferred item was missing now exists.

Alpha and Launch still run the **identical architecture at different sizes**, so there is no scale-up rewrite waiting to reintroduce jank later. **Fundraising discipline note:** the superseded wording appeared in fundraising-adjacent material. Use the amended paragraph, not the original, in anything external.

**What's genuinely new beyond standard MMO netcode:**
- Mobile zones (crawling Striders) turn from the hardest problem into the scalability win
- Local-frame coordinates are numerically small → aggressive bandwidth quantization falls out for free
- Per-frame server rewind keeps hit-reg honest for a player standing still inside a moving Strider — the exact 1.11 m/s error SRF killed would come back under naive world-time rewind
- Crash blast-radius = one Strider (~300 players), not the whole session
- A netcode validation workflow (deterministic replay harness + adversarial latency/jitter/loss matrix) — the CI-grade analog of the studio's existing Python-mirror discipline, letting a solo studio validate MMO netcode before burning engine time

**Six founder locks (all confirmed):**
1. ✅ **RFA adopted as the netcode organizing model** — the core bet, everything else cascades from this
2. ✅ **Authority split** — Strider Authority owns both its interior frame *and* its hull transform in the shared root; World Authority is compositor + shared-root writer only
3. ✅ **Alpha co-location** — 1 World Authority + 1 Strider Authority, logically separated but physically co-located in one process for Alpha
4. ✅ **Deferred set confirmed post-Alpha** — multi-process, host migration, Root partitioning, Strider-to-Strider boarding, full anti-cheat. **V44 note:** Root partitioning is still deferred, but it now has a scheme — **partition around anchors** (§3C, and the amended paragraph above). **V44 also closes the Iris vs Replication Graph fork: Cogheim does not run server-authoritative networked physics, so the Replication Graph stays and `SRFReplicationNode` is unaffected** (§35K).
5. ✅ **Validation workflow adopted** — deterministic replay harness + adversarial latency/jitter/loss matrix, CI-grade discipline
6. ✅ **Tick tiers + rewind depth acknowledged as placeholders** — pending a tuning pass, not blocking

**Shadow PC build issues resolved this session (logged for §38):**
- `$PSScriptRoot` returns empty when invoked via `powershell -File` — fixed by passing `-Zip` explicitly (found July 30, three-round-trip fix)
- Visual Studio compiler version newer than UE5.8's preferred version surfaced backward-compatibility warnings — non-blocking, noted
- `CppCompileWarningSettings` API moved from flat `TargetRules` properties to a nested struct in UE5.6+ — updated
- Missing `IMPLEMENT_MODULE` entry point in the `CogheimNetcode` module — patched and repackaged
- Stale intermediate build artifacts caused a `CoreUObject` access violation (`EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION`) — resolved via full clean rebuild
- **`Deploy-CogheimNetcode.ps1`** established as a reusable PowerShell deployment script (full install pipeline, `-DryRun`, backups, idempotency, engine auto-detection) for future Shadow PC pushes
- **Component manifest confirmed:** 4 Blueprint Function Libraries — `RFAAuthorityLibrary`, `RFARelevancyLibrary`, `RFAQuantizationLibrary`, `RFAFrameLibrary` — plus a parity check confirming `std::ceil`/`log2` produce byte-identical results to the Python mirror

**Terrain heightmap generator (July 30, feeds §3A):** Pure NumPy tool (`generate_world_terrain.py`, 19/19 sanity tests passing) producing a 2017×2017 16-bit heightmap and five biome weightmaps (snow_mountain, badlands, plains, wetlands, ocean — summing to 1.0) in UE5.8 Landscape import format, matching the locked biome layout (Iron Veil north wall, snow mountains, badlands SW, golden plains center, wetlands SE, southern peninsula).

**Next session:** Opus 4.8 — Python mirror of the relevancy graph + authority handoff, numeric expectations verified, then the full `CogheimNetcode` C++ build (Pass 2b), gated on real `CogheimSRF` headers.

## 5D. RFA PASS 2 — ALL FIVE DECISIONS LOCKED, MODULE ARCHITECTURE RULED (30 Aug 2026)

**Status: the five Pass 2 decisions are now ALL founder-approved.** V45 correctly recorded that only Decision 2 was locked and that the other four were recommendations Increment 3 had already built against. That gap is closed.

### 5D.1 The five decisions, final

| # | Ruling | State |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Replication Graph for Alpha.** Portability standard adopted: **no relevancy *policy* lives in a Replication Graph node class — nodes are thin adapters over pure predicates.** Iris reviewed when multi-Strider load data exists, or immediately if Decision 2 is reopened | **LOCKED 30 Aug** |
| **2** | **No server-authoritative networked physics** — nowhere, not in any module, not at project-settings level | **LOCKED (V44 session)** |
| **3** | **The hull transform is a pure function of a Cogheim-defined server frame. `ServerAdvance` is retired.** Rewiring the SRF component and its dependents was increment 2 | **LOCKED 30 Aug** |
| **4** | **Hull Z is a deterministic sample of baked heightfield data**, upgraded to a multi-point plane fit over two tiers — see **§3E** | **LOCKED 30 Aug** |
| **5** | **Striders are never physically displaced.** Impacts produce damage and forced, **frame-stamped course amendments**. Any future exception is a founder decision that reopens Decision 3 | **LOCKED 30 Aug** |

### 5D.2 🔒 Module architecture — a third module, `CogheimRFARuntime`

```
CogheimNetcode        pure, world-free, Python-mirrorable      the math
      ^
CogheimRFARuntime     replicated components, authority,        the frames
      ^               handoff, frame-stamped actions
CogheimSRF            Strider frame     CogheimAcademy         static frame
```

**Why, and it is not "smallest change".** §5C already states that **RFA generalizes SRF, and SRF becomes the first instance of the RFA pattern rather than a special case.** Putting the general RFA runtime inside `CogheimSRF` inverts that hierarchy. A second frame kind already exists in spec — academies are a Strider-kind RFA frame with a static `HullWorldPosM` — and under the alternative, `CogheimAcademy` would have to depend on the Strider module to obtain frame runtime. That is wrong on the day it happens and worsens with every non-Strider frame after it.

**This closes the dependency-direction question.** `CogheimSRF` depending on `CogheimNetcode` stays as-is and is correct; UBT forbids the reverse edge.

**🔴 Mandatory build discipline for this module: route it through `Deploy-CogheimAlpha.ps1`, not hand placement.** This project has had **four missing-`IMPLEMENT_MODULE` incidents**, and the one that slipped past both existing defenses (`CogheimDialogueUI`) did so precisely because it was hand-written into `Source/` and never ran through the script (§38 std. #23).

### 5D.3 🔒 Arch deck relevancy — authored open-volume override

**The arch is marked as an open volume where the 12 m vertical deck band relaxes to the horizontal radius only.**

Solves the defect exactly where it exists without weakening the cylinder rule elsewhere. The 12 m band exists because deck plates are hard occluders; **across an open arch there are no plates to occlude, so the gate is simply wrong there.** Cost: one authored volume per hull class plus a branch in the relevancy predicate. **Preserves the 21× directed-pair reduction** everywhere the band is correct — and that figure remains a **modeled algorithmic count, not a measurement.**

### 5D.4 Increment 4 — the Shared World Clock. BUILT, LOADED, VERIFIED IN AUTOMATION

**Closes** the shared-world-clock item and, with it, the per-Strider frame counter introduced as an explicit interim in increment 2.

**Why this stopped being an architectural tidy.** Increment 2 put the frame counter on `USRFStriderTransformComponent` and flagged it in the source as interim, with *"do not build anything that depends on per-Strider frames."* **Increment 3 then built exactly that** — `USRFLibrary::TransferAtFrame(CourseFrom, CourseTo, Frame, …)` takes **one frame for two hulls**, which is only meaningful if both express their courses against a common time origin. With per-hull counters, two Striders that began ticking an hour apart report frames **108,000 apart for the same instant.** The pure course math never noticed, because each course carries its own `StartFrame` — but a dock composes both sides at once, and that is precisely the comparison that breaks.

| | Delivery |
|---|---|
| **4a** | `URFAWorldClockLibrary` in CogheimNetcode — pure frame math and client estimation. **RFA spec block 9, 17 checks** |
| **4b** | `USRFWorldClockComponent` in CogheimSRF — the engine-bound clock on the game state, and deletion of the per-hull counter. **SRF Group 12, 4 checks** |

**The server half is trivial. The client half is not.** A replicated `int64` at low cadence does not hand a client the current server frame — it hands one that was current a network trip ago. Between updates the client must estimate under three requirements that pull against each other:

- **It must never run backwards.** Frame-stamped composition evaluates hull position at a frame, so a clock that steps back makes the hull — and every player standing on it — jump aft. Slightly wrong is survivable; **backwards is not**
- **It must not jitter**, or the hull stutters at the replication cadence
- **It must still make large corrections.** Reconnect, seamless travel and long hitches are real discontinuities

**Policy: SLEW small corrections (≤2 frames/tick), SNAP large ones (≥30 frames, either direction), HOLD rather than rewind on small negative ones.**

**The realistic rewind cause is not packet loss — it is latency improving.** The estimate leans on half-RTT, so a client whose round trip falls from 400 ms to 0 sees its raw estimate drop **six frames** with nothing lost and nothing out of order. A naive clock steps back there. Check **W07** measures exactly that and asserts the clock holds.

**The fail-closed choice worth knowing about.** `USRFStriderTransformComponent::GetCurrentFrame()` returns **0** when no clock is bound, so an unbound hull sits at its course origin. It deliberately does **not** fall back to a local counter — that fallback is the exact thing this increment deletes, and it fails *silently*: every hull looks correct in isolation and they disagree the instant two are compared. **Check 40 pins the behaviour so the fallback cannot be helpfully re-added.**

**Side benefit:** one replicated `int64` per Strider per update is gone. Five hundred hulls were each replicating a private copy of what should always have been the same number.

**Checks.** RFA block 9 (pure, mirrored, 17): W01–W03 server-side integer discipline (**floor, never round — a frame is not current until it has begun**) · W04–W06 the estimator · **W07 the guarantee, measured** · W08 out-of-order samples discarded · W09–W11c slew/snap/hold · W12 fail-closed on a non-positive rate · W13 an hour is exact · **W14 the defect, measured** · W14b the fix. SRF Group 12 (engine-bound, not mirrored, 4): **40** no clock fails closed to frame 0, not a local counter · **41** two hulls on one clock read the same frame · **42** advancing one clock moves every bound hull at its own speed · **43** a transfer between two hulls on one clock preserves world position.

**Check 43 is where increments 3 and 4 close on each other.** Increment 3 made the handoff frame-exact but could not make two hulls agree on which instant a frame named. Neither is sufficient alone.

Supporting: `rfa_world_clock_mirror.py` **17/17**; encoding conformance clean (0 CRLF, no BOM, balanced) across all changed files.

**⚠ A build failure worth keeping.** The first 4b build failed:

```
SRFSpec.cpp(473,7): error C2039: 'CurrentServerFrame': is not a member of 'USRFStriderTransformComponent'
```

Check 37 — written in increment 3, two increments earlier — set that field deliberately. After deleting it, the component's own `.h/.cpp` were swept and reported clean; **the module was not.** The compiler caught it in 0.79 s, but the cost was a full cycle. **Rule adopted (§38 std. #33):** deleting a public symbol is followed by a sweep of the **whole module**, not of the files the change was being made in. Specs reference production symbols and are easy to forget precisely because they are not "the code".

### 5D.5 The four remaining RFA items

| # | Item | State |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Shared world clock** | ✅ **CLOSED** |
| 2 | **Hull Z heightmap wiring** | **Open.** One call site; sampler tested (RFA C23–C29); `Cogheim_Heightmap_2017.png` exists at exactly the assumed 2017². `SRFSpec` check 35 is the tripwire. **The design is now ruled (§3E) and the blocking questions are answered — what remains is the founder ruling on the Meridian bake and one session of wiring** |
| 3 | **`rfa_tests.py`** | **Open.** `_ModuleDocs/CogheimNetcode/` holds three mirrors and no runner; **RFA blocks 1–5 (19 checks) still have no in-repo mirror**, and `RFANetcodeSpec.cpp`'s own header says so |
| 4 | **Passability region data** | **Open.** Validation code is 24/24; **no region in any level carries a mask.** Format is now recommended and scoped (§3E-2); the authoring itself is level-side work |

| Item | Trigger |

| Execution item, no decision required | Action |
|---|---|

| **`SRFReplicationNode.h` byte anomaly** | Normalize: strip the UTF-8 BOM, convert the 2 CRLF lines to LF, repair the mojibake em-dashes. Preserved deliberately until now rather than silently normalized |
| **`StriderSpeedKmh`** | Replace the single global constant with a per-class lookup. §4 locks Grindwalker 14, Vector 14, Scavenger 11, Blackjack 10, Juggernaut 8 km/h |

## 5E. THE REPLICATION SYSTEM — VERIFIED FROM PRIMARY SOURCE (Escalation closed, 5 September 2026)

Verified against Epic's own documentation, not against community sources. This section exists because the question is foundational and the answer must be re-checkable.

### 5E.1 EPIC'S DOCUMENTATION CONTRADICTS ITSELF

| Epic page | What it says, verbatim |
|---|---|
| **UE 5.8 Release Notes** | *"Iris is now production-ready for licensees in UE 5.8."* |
| **Migrate to Iris in Unreal Engine** | *"Learn to use this **Experimental** feature, but use caution when shipping with it."* |

Both were live Epic pages as of 5 September 2026. The release notes announce the promotion; the feature banner on the migration page was never updated. Read as documentation lag rather than a real disagreement, **but under Engine Law, which forbids omitting experimental status, the ambiguity is itself the recordable result.** It is not resolved by preferring one page.

### 5E.2 REPLICATION GRAPH IS **NOT** DEPRECATED BY EPIC

The UE 5.8 release notes do not mention Replication Graph at all, in any form, including any deprecation notice. A community source characterised it as "considered deprecated"; **Epic has not been seen to say so. Do not repeat the community claim as canon.** Nothing is forcing Cogheim off Replication Graph.

### 5E.3 THE TWO SYSTEMS ARE MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE

Verbatim, Epic's Migrate to Iris page:
> *"Iris does not support replication graph. Iris and Replication Graph are separate systems, and the network driver can only use one or the other."*

**This is not a system you layer on later. It is a fork.**

### 5E.4 AN UNRESOLVED QUALIFIER, RECORDED RATHER THAN GUESSED

The release notes say production-ready **"for licensees."** Whether that means every studio using the engine, or specifically custom licence partners, is not stated on that page. It matters to Limitless Game Makers LLC and it is not answerable from the documentation. **Ask Epic directly.** Still open, §37.

### 5E.5 THE MIGRATION SURFACE, AND WHY IT UNDERSTATES COGHEIM'S COST

Epic's stated migration requirements are modest: enable `bReplicateUsingRegisteredSubObjectList = true`, implement `RegisterReplicationFragments` for custom `UObject` derived subobjects, and add `DefaultEngine.ini` entries for structs with custom serialization. Epic states Iris "maintains backward compatibility with UE's generic replication system as much as possible."

**That is the engine's migration cost. It is not Cogheim's.** `NET-05` is Replication Graph **node** work: the relevancy design, the modelled 21x directed pair reduction, and the arch open volume override ruled 30 August. **Iris has its own filtering and prioritisation model, so none of that ports. It gets rebuilt.** The engine side migration is small. The project side migration is not.

### 5E.6 RULING — STAY ON REPLICATION GRAPH. THE LOAD TEST DECIDES.

1. **Cogheim remains on the Replication Graph.** Nothing forces a move.
2. **`NET-12`, the M2 concurrency and multi-Strider load test, runs on Replication Graph as planned.** It is not blocked or delayed by this question.
3. **The load test is the decision point.** If Replication Graph holds the concurrency targets, this question closes permanently. If it does not, **Iris is the fallback**, and it is the right one: higher player counts and lower server costs are the problem Epic built it for.
4. **Do not switch before `NET-12`.** Switching after a load test is expensive. Switching after Alpha is close to impossible. Switching before any numbers exist is a guess.

**This converts a foundation gamble into a measurement**, which is the only honest way to answer it.

**Re-verify before acting on this section.** Engine documentation status changes between releases, and the two pages quoted above already disagree.

## 6. GRINDWALKER INTERIOR SPEC V1.0 (Founder Lock, July 23, 2026)

> ### 🔴 V46 AMENDMENT — BOUNDING BOX, BRIDGE, AND BUILD STATUS
>
> **1. The bounding box below is superseded. It is now `950 m L × 330 m W × 140 m H`, beam `90 / 150 / 90`, straddle envelope `150 m × 60 m`.** Founder-locked 30 Aug 2026. Full ruling, derived figures and consequences in the §3D amendment. Blackjack remains tallest overall at 152 m.
>
> **2. The Crown Bridge now has an art direction.** Per the 31 Aug ruling (**§4C**), **A1 Crown Bridge is a Tier 2 Command Bridge** — a circular room under a ribbed glazed dome, a full 360° ring of tall arched windows, a physical raised circular war table carrying a **solid relief model** of terrain and settlements at its centre, a ring of brass console desks facing **inward**, the commander's station raised on the far side, warm amber against cold cyan off the table. **Classification: Post-Alpha.** At the 150 m straddle gap the envelope is not remotely constrained.
>
> **🔴 3. Arch deck numbering direction is OPEN.** A1–A8 span Z 60 m to 130 m at 10 m pitch. Whether **A1 is the top (130 m) or the bottom (60 m)** is undefined and decides whether the Crown Bridge crowns the ship or sits directly above the straddle gap. **Requires founder ruling.** ⚠ The Grindwalker generator v1 prompt was written seventeen minutes after that was flagged and **assumes A1 = top, with the dome apex at 150 m**. Treat both as **working assumptions**, not locks. The 150 m apex is a founder *recommendation* — it exists so the dome breaks the 140 m hull line while staying below Blackjack's canonical 152 m — and the generator was instructed to build it **parametrically so the apex can move.**
>
> **🔴 4. The runway conflict is open.** Gallery images show aircraft launching from a deck running **through the straddle gap** with the arch overhead. The locked exterior canon below says a **forward-projecting causeway** with a 3-stage breach tip. These may be one structure from two angles or two incompatible answers. **Requires founder ruling (§4C.8).**
>
> **5. Build status, stated plainly. No Grindwalker geometry exists on the build machine.** `L_Strider_Grindwalker` is an empty 8,533-byte stub. A **generator v1 prompt** was written on 31 Aug for an external builder — locked dimensions, Z-up convention (the Drifter's negate-Z is deliberately *not* used here), named anatomy, the Crown Bridge as the hero, a strict node-naming contract, and the three JSON sidecars (`Collision.json`, `Apertures.json`, `Traversal.json`) that the Drifter deliveries proved are the part that actually matters. **It establishes the data contract every later Grindwalker version will be diffed against.** Nothing has been delivered against it.
>
> **6. Suggested first build scope, from the handoff: a blockout that proves the scale and the straddle, not a finished asset.** Grey-box both hulls and the arch at locked dimensions with per-hull width still parameterised; 14 deck-plane markers at 10 m pitch with the arch underside at 60 m; **stand a character at the base of a hull on the terrain and screenshot it** — scale is not believed until it is seen next to a person; drive it across a ridge using the deterministic sampler, straddling; profile draw calls and triangles. **Acceptance: the straddle reads as *geography being passed over* rather than a vehicle clipping through a hill, the 60 m clearance is visibly true, and there is a real frame-cost number rather than an estimate.** Explicitly out of scope for session one: interiors, materials beyond greys, LODs, livery, docking, and anything with the word "final".
>
> **7. Scale sanity, because 330 m is hard to hold in your head:** the beam still spans **~168 landscape vertices** and **4.13%** of the entire playable Meridian region's width. A deck 130 m in the air is a legitimate vantage point that can see the whole region. **This is the reason HLOD and streaming budgets must be set before content, not after.**

## 11A. THE DRIFTER — TUTORIAL VESSEL (Founder Lock, July 26–30, 2026)

> ### 🔴 V46 AMENDMENT — THE DRIFTER CHANGED MORE THAN ANY OTHER SYSTEM THIS WEEKEND
>
> Read this before any figure below it. The section body remains an accurate record of the July build; the live geometry state is here.
>
> #### A. The core was sealed. This was the blocking bug, and it was not visual.
>
> Michael walked `L_Drifter_V5` and reported the stairs blocked by a doorway on the wrong side. Investigating found something larger behind it. Verified from `drifter_blockout_v5.json`: **`deck_plate` was one solid box per deck spanning the entire envelope**, and each one fully covered **both** the lift footprint and the stairwell footprint. **The stairs climbed into a solid ceiling and the lift shaft had no shaft. The core connected nothing to anything.** No door position fixes that.
>
> Three smaller defects in the same area, all from magic offsets (`+10`, `STAIR_W/2`, `+60`, `−120`, `−40`) instead of deriving from `WALL_T`: the stairwell door opened into the **side of a flight**; flight 1 buried 20 cm in the east wall and the half-landing 20 cm in the north wall; and the first step sat **~2.3 cm below the walking surface**. The rebuild derives everything.
>
> #### B. Then the measurement that decided the shape: the well could not make a walkable stair at all
>
> Against the comfort rule (riser 15–18.5 cm, tread ≥ 25 cm, **2R + T between 60 and 66 cm**), **every one of the six decks failed** in a 300 × 500 well — 2R+T between 51.8 and 58.6, and Engineering at a **16.7 cm tread, narrower than a boot.** The risers were fine; there was simply not enough run. **Adding the floor-level landing the design needed made it worse**, because a landing eats run. This was not a generator bug — the footprint was too small for the deck heights.
>
> #### C. 🔒 The ruling: Option C, the parking-garage wrapping core — Michael's own reference
>
> > *"most large parking lots have a square column and stairs wind up inside, stair set to landing and so on. so maybe we just need a half landing between floors?"*
>
> **Four quarter-flights wrapping a square column, landing at every corner — and the column is the lift shaft.** Every deck lands inside the comfort band, and the part worth pausing on: **it is smaller than what it replaced.** Folding the lift into the column removes the 100 cm dead gap and the stairwell's own fourth wall — one square core instead of two boxes and a slot. It also fixes things that are not the stairs: the shaft and the stair share **one** opening per plate instead of two, the lift doors face landings instead of a 100 cm slot, and a landing every quarter turn gives the diegetic pass a natural place for signage, a MAXIOM plate, or a window.
>
> **The core socket as built, in centimetres** (enforced by validators — an asset that ignores them fails the suite rather than merely looking wrong):
>
> | | cm |
> |---|---:|
> | Core outer (square) | **620 × 620** |
> | Wall thickness | 20 |
> | Core interior | **580 × 580** |
> | Lift column outer | **360 × 360** |
> | Lift shaft interior | **320 × 320** |
> | Annulus = flight width | **110** |
> | Straight run per side | **360** |
> | Corner landing | 110 × 110 |
> | Lobby (south side, full length) | 580 × 110 |
> | Doorways (core entry, lift) | 100 w × 200 h |
> | Pawn capsule | **68 w × 176 h** |
> | Core centre, hull coords | X **−420**, Y **0** |
>
> Three quarter-flights wrap the column — east side climbing S→N, north side E→W, west side N→S — with a landing at each corner. **The south side is not a flight: it is the lobby**, carrying both the core's entry door and the lift door facing each other across 110 cm. **Geometric identity worth knowing: the straight run per side equals the *column* width, not the core width.** Widening the core only widens the flights; changing the column moves every step count.
>
> **Plate openings:** decks **1–5 get the opening** (deck N's stairs arrive at deck N−1's plate); **deck 6's plate stays solid** — it is the bottom of the ship and the lift pit floor. The opening covers the flights and corner landings and **stops short of the floor landing**, which is what you step onto. Cut additively as bands around the opening — **no CSG**, the same jamb/header discipline the doorways already use. **Fall protection is a named follow-up**: the old model had `GenerateLiftSafetyBoxes`; V5's equivalent is the landing barriers, now validated at 5 barriers + 1 car floor.
>
> #### D. ⚠ The deck heights are now UNIFORM 3.3 m — and this change has no source document
>
> The section below, and the 30 Aug 16:13 and 17:26 documents, specify six **different** deck heights: **550 / 480 / 650 / 600 / 480 / 550 cm**, a 33.1 m hull. Every document from 30 Aug 20:10 onward instead assumes **six uniform 3.300 m decks**, with a **0.252 m slab** giving **3.048 m = exactly 10 ft clear**, six inhabited decks spanning **19.8 m** inside a 33.0 m overall height (chassis 0.50–6.60 m, decks 5.00–24.80 m, bridge/roof/Slipstreamer 24.80–33.08 m). The current V5 generator validates at **304.8 cm clear on every deck** with a uniform **18.33 cm riser / 26.67 cm tread**.
>
> **The change is real and is in the committed geometry. No document in project knowledge records the decision, the reasoning, or the founder approval** — it is referenced three times as having happened and having cost most of a day. **Recorded here from its downstream evidence and flagged: requires founder confirmation that the uniform 3.3 m stack is locked.** The related question an external builder raised independently — *are the Drifter's deck heights right?* — is thereby largely overtaken by events, but was never formally ruled.
>
> #### E. 🔒 The envelope conflict, and the rewrite that closed it
>
> **The finding, measured, not estimated:** the v2 hull measures **3397 cm long × 2192 cm wide**. Four of six V5 decks were **physically larger than the shell meant to contain them** — Deck 4 was 6.0 m too long and 6.6 m too wide. The 30 Aug deck-height correction fixed the vertical dimension and left the plan dimensions untouched; **this was the other half of the cathedral problem, and it survived because nothing had a hull to check against until the shell arrived.**
>
> **Ruled: option (a), shrink V5 to the hull.** Reasoning: the hull dimensions come from the founder's reference image and were hit exactly by an external builder **five times running**, which is the more expensive thing to re-derive; the Drifter is explicitly *"a life raft of a city, not a Strider"* and a 40 m footprint pulls against that; and **the V5 envelopes were never derived from anything** — they were assumed early and inherited, which is precisely why they drifted.
>
> **The estimate of the cost was wrong, and the correction is worth keeping.** It was called "a real day of work, the same class as the deck-height correction." Measured, it was not. **The rooms were never the constraint** — zone footprints occupy **6–16% of each deck**, 29–79 m² against 481 m² available, and the packer had enormous slack. And **the target did not have to be invented**: the shell's own `Collision.json` declares a real, measured interior floor plate for every deck, so the interior is now **derived from the exterior rather than guessed alongside it.**
>
> | Our deck | Name | Old (cm) | **New (cm)** | Derived from |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | 1 | Wake-Up Bay | 2090 × 1730 | **2240 × 1610** | their DECK_6 |
> | 2 | Bridge Remnant | 3020 × 2190 | **2360 × 1700** | their DECK_5 |
> | 3 | Engineering | 3650 × 2700 | **2470 × 1780** | their DECK_4 |
> | 4 | Cargo Hold | 4000 × 2850 | **2570 × 1850** | their DECK_3 |
> | 5 | Crew Deck | 3960 × 2770 | **2680 × 1820** | their DECK_2 |
> | 6 | Lower Hold | 3870 × 2570 | **2750 × 1750** | their DECK_1 |
>
> **The ship now tapers** — widest at Deck 4, narrowing toward the roof. That is the shoulder massing the shell gained in v4, and the interior inherits it instead of being six identical rectangles.
>
> **🔴 Deck numbering is inverted between the two models. Their `DECK_n` = our Deck `(7 − n)`.** Their DECK_1 is the bottom (walk surface at glTF Y 5.000 m); our Deck 1 is the top — Wake-Up Bay, emitted Z 0. **Anything aligning by deck number without this mapping puts Engineering in the bilge.** The mapping lives in one function in `import_drifter_shell.py` and must be stated to the builder in any future prompt so both sides use one convention.
>
> **Box count: 1,070 → 1,122 → 1,023 → 549.** Per deck now **81 / 95 / 85 / 97 / 97 / 94**. **Fewer boxes is the interior fitting inside the exterior, not content going missing** — every named zone still places, and that is asserted. Centre-Z extent is **unchanged** at −1970.83 .. −9.17, so `test_world_z_extent_matches_hull` is untouched.
>
> **All ten validators re-run on the new layout, all PASS:** positive dimensions · deck plates present and ordered · headroom clears the 176 cm pawn (310 cm every deck) · lift aligned across all six decks (shaft walls at −805 / −635 / −465) · stair comfort every deck (18.33 riser, 26.67 tread, **2R+T 63.3**) · stair fits inside the well (240.03 + 50 ≤ 300) · plate openings connect decks (1–5 cut into 4 pieces, 6 solid) · flights reach the ceiling (18 steps, 9 per flight) · lift shaft guarded every deck · every named zone inside its envelope, **0 outside**. **Nothing about the stair, core or lift needed to change** — the circulation core at 830 × 530 fits comfortably in the narrowest deck.
>
> **Files committed:** `Scripts\drifter_blockout_v5.py` (`ENVELOPE` rewritten, derivation and deck inversion documented in place) · `ArtFiles\drifter_blockout_v5.json` (regenerated, 549 boxes) · `Source\CogheimDrifter\DrifterBlockoutV5.cpp` (`GEnvelopeCm` mirrored 1:1, each row annotated with its source deck) · `Source\CogheimDrifter\DrifterSystemsSpec.cpp` (`TotalBoxCount` 1023 → 549; `Expected[6]` → { 81, 95, 85, 97, 97, 94 }). Backup at `drifter_blockout_v5.pre_envelope.bak`.
>
> **🔴 STATUS: COMMITTED, NOT BUILT. No log has been read. The 549 figure and the validator results are generator-side; they are not an Automation result.**
>
> #### F. A standing contract this creates
>
> **Deck footprints are now derived from the shell.** If the shell's `Collision.json` plates ever change, `ENVELOPE` must be **re-derived, never hand-edited.** State this to the builder in any future prompt.
>
> #### G. Open, and unaffected by any of the above
>
> - **Interior lighting is not physical.** The sun is **6 lux** against a real-world ~100,000, so exposure sits at **EV100 −3.5** and the interior lights are **~20× under nominal** to compensate. It works, but every lighting decision inherits the distortion. A daylight-value sun with a normal exposure is the clean fix and **changes the approved exterior look — founder call.**
> - **The box format has no rotation.** `FDrifterBlockoutBox` is centre + dimensions only and the spawner uses identity rotation. An imported mesh sidesteps this for the stair, but the limitation still blocks any future angled element — ducting, ladders, the 45°/22.5° angled walls raised in the kit work.
>
> #### H. Next, in order
>
> 1. **Build and run automation.** Expected 549 boxes and the new `Expected[6]`. **Unverified.**
> 2. **Respawn `L_Drifter_V5`** from the new JSON — `spawn_drifter_v5.py` first, since it destroys everything prefixed `DrifterV5_`.
> 3. Run `import_drifter_shell.py` (combine-meshes **off** in the dialog) and `build_drifter_shell_collision.py`.
> 4. **The shell can now be welded** — the blocker is cleared; every deck fits inside the hull that carries it.
> 5. Then the two PIE tests that decide things: **walk the ramp, stair and a catwalk circuit stationary, then under way.**

## 11B. THE DRIFTER EXTERIOR SHELL — FIVE EXTERNAL DELIVERIES (30–31 Aug 2026)

**Status: ACCEPTED, NOT IMPORTED.** Every figure below is measured by parsing the shipped files directly. **Nothing has been imported, compiled, or run in the editor. No build log exists for any of these packages.** Two import scripts are written, syntax-checked and dry-run against real data; **neither has been run in the editor.**

`UDrifterBlockoutV5` remains **authoritative for walkable interior space, collision and stair geometry.** The shell is the skin over it, plus the chassis, bridge, roof and Slipstreamer that V5 has never modelled at all.

### 11B.1 What the five deliveries did

| | v1 (30 Aug) | v2 (31 Aug) | v3 | v4 | v5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Unique meshes | 254 (all baked) | 52 | 54 | 58 | **104** |
| Placements | 254 | **6,013** | 6,208 | 6,982 | **8,199** |
| Instancing ratio | 1 : 1 | 115.7 : 1 | 115.0 : 1 | 120 : 1 | ~79 : 1 |
| Unique-library tris | 113,400 | 2,822 | 2,854 | 3,126 | **13,262** |
| Scene tris | 113,400 | 207,368 | 216,328 | 243,032 | **523,272** |
| Collision primitives | **0** | 108 | 108 | 108 | 108 |
| Apertures data | — | — | **32** | 32 | 32 |
| Traversal data | — | — | **57 wp / 62 edges** | frozen | frozen |
| Authored (non-primitive) shapes | 0 | 0 | 0 | **0** | **48** |
| Materials complete (BaseColor+Normal+ORM) | **0 of 11** | 6 of 11 | 6 of 11 | 6 of 11 | **11 of 11** |

**Verdict at v5: accept, and stop iterating the exterior.** All three requested changes landed completely. **Nothing further from the builder unblocks the walkability proof.**

### 11B.2 The dimensional lock held, and the geometry now carries it

**3300 − 252 = 3048 mm = exactly 10 ft clear**, measured with full transform composition on all six decks. **The founder lock is in the geometry, not just the spec sheet.** Stowed hull: **33.97 m long × 22.178 m wide × 33.0 m tall** (v5; width grew 26 cm from v2, all to port).

⚠ **Their `span_xyz_m` X value reports ~26.15–26.35 m. That is bounds *with the boarding ramp deployed*, not beam.** Use the stowed figure. This has misread once already.

### 11B.3 The data sidecars — the strongest work in any delivery

`Traversal.json`: **57 waypoints, 62 edges** (37 walk, 12 aperture, 10 stair, 2 ladder, 1 ramp). Run through an **independent breadth-first search** from `GROUND_RampFoot` over supported edges only: **57 of 57 waypoints reachable, no orphans; all nine destinations reachable** (decks 1–6, bridge, roof, Slipstreamer pad); **zero dangling edges; zero supported edges routing through an uncut wall.**

`Apertures.json`: **32 entries, 28 `wall_is_cut: true`, 4 false** — and the 4 false are exactly the 4 hatches with 0.91 m sills, none of which sits on a walking route. **That is the honest answer rather than the convenient one.** They could have reported 32/32 cut and nobody would have caught it before a wasted test cycle.

`Collision.json`: **108 box primitives** with `schema_version` and `extents_semantics` declared; `EXTSTAIR_Flight_A/B` carry `rotation_euler_deg: [17.6, 0, 0]` and the note *"one slope per flight"* — the sloped-box-per-flight instruction followed precisely. **It also turned out to be the source of truth for the interior envelope rewrite (§11A.E).**

### 11B.4 The freeze held three consecutive times, twice unprompted

From v3 onward the builder preserved the data contract our scripts are written against — **0 collision primitives changed, 0 apertures moved or re-cut, 0 traversal waypoints or edges changed** across v3→v4→v5. At v5 they shipped a `QA_Baseline/V4/` folder and diffed against it; **their baseline was verified genuine byte-for-byte before anything computed from it was trusted** — a self-reported diff against a substituted baseline would prove nothing.

**`import_drifter_shell.py` and `build_drifter_shell_collision.py` remain valid across three deliveries with no edits.** `ASSERT_SLAB` still passes.

**v4 is worth a specific note: the prompt was never sent.** That build was the builder's own initiative and should be read as what they chose to do, not as compliance — and what they chose was a **concept-convergence pass** rather than the detail pass that would have been specified. It was the better call. **The value hierarchy is now right**, which was the single biggest visual improvement: patched iron 0.291 luminance, blackened iron 0.139, aged brass 0.378 — **dark iron dominant with brass as accent.** v3 read pale and beige; v4 reads as the dark iron settlement in the reference.

### 11B.5 The import contract

| Setting | Value | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Pipeline | Interchange (UE 5.8 default for GLB) | — |
| **Combine Meshes** | **OFF** | Preserves the named groups. **Non-negotiable** — combining loses the deck tags |
| Generate Collision | OFF | Drive it from `Collision.json` instead |
| Generate Lightmap UVs | OFF | Lumen, `r.AllowStaticLighting=0` |
| Generate Tangents | ON | File ships POSITION / NORMAL / TEXCOORD_0 only |
| **Import Materials** | **OFF** | Rebuild as instances of our `M_Drifter` master. **Their metallic constants must not enter the project** |
| **Import Textures** | **OFF** | Import by hand |
| Destination | `/Game/Drifter/Shell` | Keeps art separate from `/Game/Drifter/Geometry` (V5 blockout) |
| Nanite | ON for shell/chassis, **OFF** for glass and rails | Rails are thin two-sided trim; glass is translucent |

**Coordinate contract:** source is glTF **Y-up, metres**; Cogheim emits **−Z down** (`EmitZ`, 28 Aug lock) in **centimetres**. Uniform scale 100, then rotate glTF +Y to local +Z-down before emission. The GLB's deck-1 slab top at Y = 5.000 m is pinned to our **Z = 0**, a −500 cm offset in source space. **The shell extends 28.08 m above Z = 0** — bridge, roof and Slipstreamer live above deck 1, which V5 has never modelled. **`ValidateWorldZExtent` covers V5 boxes only and must not be extended to cover imported art.**

**Collision to author, by priority.** Everything without authored collision imports at **Default / no collision — never Complex-as-Simple.** A 113k-triangle complex hull beneath a moving RFA root is exactly the case being avoided.

| Group | Priority |
|---|---|
| `INTERACTIVE_BoardingRamp` + rails · `EXTSTAIR_*` treads, landings and rails · `DECK_n_Catwalk_*Rail` (fall protection) | **Alpha-critical** |
| `DECK_n_PerimeterFascia` · `ROOF_SlipstreamerPad` + rails · `BRIDGE_*` walkable floor and wing rails | Alpha-supporting |
| `CHASSIS_*`, `SHELL_*` decoration, `SLIPSTREAMER_*` | **None** — not walkable |

**Attachment,** all three sharing Drifter-local space under the RFA moving root; **nothing in this import may set a world transform:**

```
RFA_AttachRoot
├── SM_Drifter_Shell_*            (imported shell)
├── DrifterV5_*                   (blockout, authoritative interior)
└── SM_Drifter_Circulation_Core   (delivery 1, at local Z +170 cm)
```

**Acceptance criteria.** Groups land uncombined · **no imported material asset exists** — only instances of `M_Drifter`, every metallic driven by a mask · shell deck-1 slab top coincides with V5 Z = 0 within 1 cm · shell perimeter does not intersect any `DrifterV5_` wall box · ramp, exterior stair and every catwalk rail have authored simple collision, nothing on Complex-as-Simple · **a character can walk the ramp, the exterior stair and one full catwalk circuit in PIE with the Drifter stationary** · **the same walk with the Drifter moving under RFA, with no base-drift jitter.** The last is **the real test** and the one that decides whether exterior decks are walkable under way. **Both are PIE verification and neither may be reported as met without the log.**

### 11B.6 The circulation core asset (delivery 1) — assessed, NOT imported

**Verdict: excellent asset, built to a different building. It does not drop in.** 25,832 triangles, 49 mesh groups, 7 materials, 4 embedded PNGs, 0 external texture URIs, 0 rotated nodes. The stair anatomy is right — stringers, handrails and rail posts are exactly the three raked elements a box stair cannot express — and the lift is better still: guide rails, counterweight and channel, cage floor/frame/mesh/gate, chains, winch drum, sprockets, gearbox, flywheel, buffer pit with springs and rams, emergency handwheel, maintenance controls. **That is diegetic machinery of the kind the pillars ask for, not a box that moves.** Stair proportions arrived at independently and correctly: **183.3 mm riser, 266.7 mm tread, 2R+T = 63.3 cm.**

**Why it cannot be imported as delivered:** it is built for a **uniform 3.3 m floor-to-floor** — which, ironically, is now what the Drifter has, but the asset is **monolithic** (one GLB spanning chassis plus six levels; `Stair_Treads` is a single mesh holding every tread on every level), so it cannot be reassembled or retimed; its footprint is **8.30 × 5.00 m against the 6.20 × 6.20 m socket**; its topology is **lift and stair side by side**, which is the *superseded* arrangement Option C replaced; it ships **no collision**; its metallic factors are 0.78–0.90 constants; and **its `BUILD_SPEC.json` contradicts the file** — the sheet says Z-up, the file is glTF Y-up. **Trusting the sheet over the file would have put the core on its side.** One thing lines up exactly: **the lift shaft clear opening is 3.20 × 3.20 m in both** — the one dimension the spec pinned that the builder hit.

**Revised ask: deliver a KIT, not a building** — `SM_Core_Stair_Step`, `_Stringer_1m`, `_Handrail_1m`, `_RailPost`, `_Landing_110`, `_Lobby_580x110`, `_Lift_Cage` (keep the 3.20 m), `_Lift_Machinery`, `_Lift_Buffer`, `_LiftGuideRail_1m`, `_Landing_Gate` — modules the generator assembles per deck exactly as it places boxes today. **Keep from delivery 1 unchanged: the stair anatomy, the whole lift mechanism, the 3.2 m shaft, the amber/cyan fixture split, and the 183/267 riser-tread. Only the assembly has to change.**

### 11B.7 🔴 The plugin — REJECT AS CODE, KEEP AS SKETCH

`DrifterDropIn` shipped with v1. The intent is right and the code is real (no invented APIs). Four problems, each a project-law violation:

1. **`UFUNCTION(Server, Reliable)` with no `WithValidation`, no rate limit, no proximity check.** Any client anywhere on the map can call `ServerRequestLiftFloor` at any rate — **a reliable-buffer overflow vector.** Violates the RPC validation rule outright
2. **The lift moves on wall clock, not physics frame.** `FInterpConstantTo(…, DeltaSeconds, …)` with a replicated float position. **Project law is that gameplay events synchronise by physics frame.** A player standing on the car will drift against their own prediction
3. **`UBoxComponent` as a walkable moving base, nested inside RFA.** A box does not make a character ride it. **This is a moving base inside a moving base** — it belongs on the SRF path
4. **`bCanEverTick = true` unconditionally.** At 500 Striders that is 500 always-on actor ticks for lifts that are idle almost always

**Take `ADrifterDropInActor` as a design sketch of the mount hierarchy — which is a good sketch — and implement the lift in `CogheimDrifter` against SRF, where frame-stamped handoff already exists.**

### 11B.8 Two small notes on v5, neither blocking

**Seventeen authored meshes are placed fewer than four times**, six exactly once. A single-use unique mesh is a draw call for one object; low priority, worth a pass if there is ever a v6. And **a craft note on the chains**: they are applied as continuous balustrade infill on every rail on every deck, where in the reference chain is *situational* — slung, hanging, securing something. **The shape is right; the placement rule is uniform where the reference is opportunistic.** A note for final art dressing, not a reason to send another round.

### 11B.9 Open

| # | Decision | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Weld the shell to `L_Drifter_V5` now, or hold for the V5 respawn?** | The respawn destroys everything prefixed `DrifterV5_`; the imported shell would survive, so **ordering must be deliberate** |
| 2 | **Do players walk exterior decks while the Drifter moves?** | Acceptance criterion 7. **Touches SRF/RFA architecture, not art** |
| 3 | Is the Slipstreamer a separate asset? | **Recommend yes** — it should fly, not be welded to a roof |
| 4 | Does a 33 m Drifter with a bridge and antenna mast still read as **a life raft, not a Strider**? | The reference image says yes. Worth confirming before the shell is committed |

## 12. SYNDICATE SYSTEM V1.0

**Status:** Locked June 2026 ✓ (Addendum V1.0 in project knowledge)

- **Each Strider IS the Syndicate** — not a guild owning a base; the Strider is the community
- 200–300 players per Syndicate; 500 Syndicates per server
- **Founding gate:** Minimum 7 players + RSS cost + 10 days playtime
- **The Drifter:** New-player tutorial Strider; 8–10 NPC skeleton crew; tutorial ends when player walks gangplank onto a Syndicate Strider (cinematic, non-skippable, scored)
- **Black-Maw Stations:** 3–5 permanent Blackjack-class neutral hubs; one per biome; always non-attackable; Running Lights permanent (Louis Wire-Heart does NOT announce here)
- **Joel's Green Fund:** Ironmark stipend for unaffiliated players — administered at Black-Maw Stations
- **Crew Board:** Diegetic bulletin board in Black-Maw Stations for Syndicate recruitment posts
- **Greeter NPC:** Political interface NPC at every Syndicate Strider gangplank
