## 12A. SYNDICATE CHARTERS & THE MERCHANT ARCHETYPE (Founder-ruled, August 27, 2026)

**Status:** Design proposal, founder-ruled in session. **Post-Alpha — nothing here is Alpha scope.** Nothing implemented, compiled, or tested. Source: `COGHEIM_Syndicate_Charters_and_Merchant_Archetype_V1.md` v1.1. Related to §17A.

**The problem it solves:** in most MMOs a wealthy, lightly-armed trade group is not a merchant, it is a target — the trade role gets captured by whoever is strongest, and the result is a protection racket. This system makes the merchant/non-combat role structurally survivable without weakening PvP for players who want it.

**🔒 LOCKED — the Charter System.** A Syndicate publicly declares one of four charters, displayed on hull and world map. Each charter is a verb:

| Charter | Verb | Business |
|---|---|---|
| **Martial** | Fights | Conquest, territory, escort work, war |
| **Commerce** | Moves | Hauling, market capitals, berths, Depot reach |
| **Industry** | Makes | Refining, manufacturing, Order Book capacity |
| **Explorer** | Finds | Survey, salvage, ruins, translation stock, sensors |

The four charters are economically interdependent by construction (Explorer recovers → Industry refines → Commerce hauls → Martial escorts one commodity), and the same chain reuses the Lamp Oil supply line from §17A.

**🔒 LOCKED — the constitutional PvP rule:** *only a Martial Syndicate can initiate an attack, and only against another Martial Syndicate.* A Commerce/Industry/Explorer player is never attackable by another player, anywhere, under any circumstance — not geographic safety, charter-based safety. **No region of Cogheim is PvE-only; every square of the disc is contested terrain.** Safety is manufactured, not located: a Strider going Running Lights projects a protection zone (generous working radius, requires validation) for the duration of its twelve-hour hold, and nobody — including two Martial Syndicates at war — can fight inside it.

**🔒 LOCKED — territory by charter-appropriate method:** claimable cities (Martial, by force), resource sites (Industry, by production output), dig sites/ruins (Explorer, by finding/marking the most), trade posts (Commerce, by trade volume/route control). Any charter may attempt any territory type but must win it by that type's own method.

**🔒 LOCKED — military capability cap:** non-Martial hulls are denied offensive systems only (main guns, siege weapons, boarding gear, the primary Strider weapon) — a build-time restriction, not a combat penalty. All defensive systems (armor, shielding, point defense, evasion) are retained, so a Commerce hull can survive the world; it cannot conquer anyone.

**🔒 LOCKED — charter integrity:** a 30-day cooldown on charter changes (closes the "flip to Martial, gank, flip back" exploit); charter is always publicly visible; switching charters does not carry forward any charter-gated capacity.

**🔒 LOCKED — market capitals and hosting:** a Commerce Syndicate that invests in berths/gantries/Depot tier over weapons becomes a market capital. **Hosting alone is not a livelihood** — modeled at roughly a sixth of a Syndicate's income at best (§17A's earn model); the real merchant money is hauling between locked regional markets. Framing, locked: *"a merchant Syndicate is not a landlord, it is a hauler that hosts."*

**🔒 LOCKED — Colossus exposure:** Commerce hulls are military-capped, so they are structurally among the likelier Colossus targets — **weighted, never guaranteed** (exact multiplier requires validation). A market capital cannot buy its way out with Iron Marks; what protects it is the goodwill of the Syndicates who dock with it, which is the intended Pillar Three payoff.

**🔒 LOCKED — escort contracts:** *escorts are hired against the world, never against other players* — a Commerce Syndicate hires Martial Syndicates for firepower against NPC raiders, the Colossus, the Iron Veil, terrain, and weather, never for "protection" from a player threat, because player attack on a non-Martial hull is already impossible by charter. This closes off the protection-racket failure mode structurally rather than by policy. Runs on the existing Order Book (§17A §10) as a contract type — no new system. Detachment (Iron Frames + crew riding aboard) is the default; a full Strider escort is reserved for runs above roughly 250,000 IM of cargo (requires validation). NPC escorts exist as a costlier, less effective fallback for off-hours coverage — routing through the Academy network vs. the Order Book directly is open (§13.2).

**Rival Test:** EVE Online, Albion, and New World all have deep trade economies, but in each, wealth without firepower is prey — the strongest players are still those who can defend or project force. Cogheim's charters make the merchant role immune to player attack **by declaration, not geography**, and cap it militarily so the immunity is paid for. Advantage type: accessibility and cooperation, with persistence from reputation (Syndicate Memory Engine, §25) accumulating over years. Fallback if charters prove unworkable: permanent geographic no-attack corridors between Academy nodes — weaker, less distinctive, but functional.

**Alpha scope classification:** every system in this section is Post-Alpha or Full-production; regional price divergence (needed for hauling to matter) is Alpha-supporting and can ship with a reduced region set. **Per the V41 directive (§36), this work is now authorized to proceed in parallel with the Alpha-critical track rather than waiting on it** — the classification here is sequencing guidance (charters depend on Syndicate/attack systems that don't exist yet), not a hold.

**Open — requires validation or founder approval:** protection-zone radius; Colossus weighting multiplier for Commerce hulls; NPC escorts via Academy vs. Order Book; exact denied-hardpoint list (depends on the Strider systems model); territory-contest thresholds per type.

## 12B. SYNDICATE SYSTEM V2.1 — CURRENT (1 September 2026)

**Source:** `claude/COGHEIM_Syndicate_System_V2_Sep1_2026.md`. **This supersedes §12, §12A and the June 2026 `COGHEIM_Syndicate_System_Addendum_V1.md` in full for founding, mutiny, succession, recruitment and economy.** V47 does not contain any of it.

> **The Addendum V1 now carries its own superseded banner and is retained as historical record only.** Six of its rules were changed. **Still valid and carried forward:** the core premise (each Strider IS the Syndicate), The Drifter tutorial, the Greeter's first-contact function, the Succession Window, Colossus naming rights, cosmetic-only monetization, and the unaffiliated stipend — which now serves **the Unsigned**.

### 12B-1. WHAT CHANGED FROM V1

| Subject | Addendum V1 (June 2026) | **V2.1 — CURRENT** |
|---|---|---|
| Founders required | 7 players | **11 co-signers** |
| Founding cost | RSS earned on The Drifter | **121,000 Iron Marks** (22k fee + 99k seed) |
| Account-age gate | 10 real days to found | **7 days to count toward the member target** |
| Mutiny trigger | 30 days membership, filed at the Greeter | **35% tenure-weighted Articles, signed below decks** |
| Succession | 60+ days standing declares candidacy; random fallback | **Senior active officer, after 14 days Commander absence** |
| Recruitment hub | "Transit Bastion", Crew Board | **Black-Maw Station, Muster Board** |

**Founder's stated reasoning for 7 → 11:** a mid-size streamer can gather seven people in ninety seconds. **The original design intent — that founding represent genuine commitment and that the world map never fill with dead vanity Striders — is preserved and strengthened.**

**Naming corrections applied:** *Bastion* is retired to the Naming Graveyard (§32) as both a class and a hub name. The five classes are **Vector, Scavenger, Grindwalker, Blackjack, Juggernaut.** "Transit Bastion" → **Black-Maw Station.**

### 12B-2. THE ELEVEN SPECIALTIES

Ordnance · Ironclad · Marque · Rail & Route · Counting House · The Exchange · Broadsheet · The Forge · The Seam · The Larder · The Almanac.

**A Syndicate holds up to five**, shown as five lamp housings on the Charter Sign. See §33B for the approved badge set and the lamp rules, which are **system rules, not art notes** — an unearned lamp is completely empty, never a dimmed symbol.

### 12B-3. FOUNDING, RANKS AND GROWTH

- **11 co-signers**, at the **Writ of Founding** at the Black-Maw registrar's counter: eleven numbered signature lines and a seal press.
- **121,000 IM.** Account-age gate: 7 days to count toward the member target.
- **60 members by day 30**, with **one 15-day grace period.**
- **Five ranks:** Hand · Crew · Foreman · Officer · Commander.
- **Daughter Syndicates** at 150 members plus one mastery.

### 12B-4. MUTINY, SUCCESSION AND THE TREASURY

- **Mutiny: 35% tenure-weighted Articles of Grievance**, linear by days served, **capped at 365 days** — so a founding member is worth at most a year of tenure and cannot be a permanent veto.
- **Signed below decks, at the boiler deck, and sealed during gathering.** The conspiracy needs a location the crew can be seen approaching. **A public NPC at the main boarding point is the opposite of that**, which is why the Greeter's mutiny-filing role is removed.
- **A 48-hour defence window** is new in V2. Simple-majority crew vote survives from V1.
- **Succession** triggers at **14 days Commander absence with 25% signing**; command passes to the **senior active officer.** No candidacy declarations, no random assignment.
- **Succession Window, 48 real hours** — carried unchanged from V1: Running Lights drop and the Strider becomes attackable, research pauses, the vault loan system freezes, no new Muster Board postings.
- **Treasury drain alarm at 25% in 24 hours.** The visible mutiny-history tag on the Registry survives from V1.

### 12B-5. THE DOCKET ECONOMY

- **2 dockets per player per day.** The cap is the whole balance mechanism and it is why personal research uses a separate currency (§26F).
- **9,000 dockets + 850,000 IM per specialty**, across **five tiers.**
- **Each specialty earns from its own activity only.** You cannot grind one activity to fund the whole tree. **This rule works, and §26F reuses it deliberately.**

### 12B-6. §11A THE LARDER AND THE FEAST · §11B TERRITORY

- **The Larder** is a specialty and a place: hydroponic glasshouse terraces, the grain towers, and **the Feast** as its social payoff.
- **Territory rework: six claim mechanisms and five amplify mechanisms.** Claiming and strengthening are separated, which is what stops territory becoming a single number two Syndicates race on.

### 12B-7. §12 THE GREETERS — CANON PEOPLE, ONE PER CLASS

> ### OVERTURNED 4 SEPTEMBER 2026 — GREETERS ARE CANON PEOPLE, NOT INVENTED ARCHETYPES
> **Overturns:** the five invented names **Evander Sable, Marta Stoke, Cass Magpie, Ines Calliper**, and the statement that this closed a V1 open item.
> **Retained:** Teddy Quickmatch.
> **Also overturns:** `COGHEIM_Syndicate_System_V2_Sep1_2026.md` §12, which locked Greeters as "five class archetypes repeated across hundreds of Striders, not placed characters."
> **The nine retired Greeter names go to the Naming Graveyard, §32.** Note that "Adelaide" appears in two parallel civic rosters with different surnames; retiring the Greeter set leaves **Adelaide Iron-Vow** untouched.

**The slate, locked:**

| Class | Greeter | Manner |
|---|---|---|
| Blackjack | **Chris Ledger-Gold** | Formal, deliberate, ceremonial |
| Vector | **Penny Truex** | Precise, measured, observant |
| Scavenger | **Gina** | Pragmatic, wry, adaptive |
| Juggernaut | **Debbie St. Cloud** | Blunt, loyal, industrial |
| Grindwalker | **Teddy Quickmatch** | Direct, fast talking, no ceremony |

**The five manners are written into the Index here for the first time.** They appeared nowhere in V48; §23 said only "personality matching class DNA." They are founder held material tracing to `COGHEIM_Syndicate_System_Addendum_V1.md` §3.

**Consequence, see §3A:** a Greeter is one per class, so Chris Ledger-Gold cannot also be a ubiquitous identity on every hull. The Loan Office identity now varies by class.

The Drifter's 8 to 10 NPC roster names are **not** open. See §11A: the locked ten stands.

### 12B-8. §15 THE WRECK — RENEWABLE

The Wreck is a **renewable site with three stages** and **per-player 7-day Berth cooldowns.** Per-player rather than per-site is the load-bearing choice: it makes the location durable content instead of a race, and it removes the incentive to camp it.

## 13. VEHICLE COMBAT SYSTEM V2.0

> ### OVERTURNED 4 SEPTEMBER 2026 — CLASS DETERMINED VEHICLES ARE RETIRED
> **Overturns:** this section's Layer 1 description of personal vehicles as class determined.

**Core model:** Each Strider = land aircraft carrier
- 200–300 players man vehicles, turrets, and weapon systems simultaneously
- No commander-controlled RTS model — fully player-distributed

## 13A. ALPHA-SCOPE COMBAT SUBSET (Founder Lock, July 2026 — **CORRECTED V32**)

**Status:** LOCKED ✓ | **Corrected V32** | **Built and compiling** ✓

**🟢 V32 CORRECTION — the stale "Grindwalker aerial" line is gone.** V20 through V31 named the Alpha personal vehicle as the "Grindwalker long-range aerial." That predates §13C, which replaced class-locked personal vehicles with the class-neutral 10-platform roster — no "Grindwalker aerial" exists in the actual roster. It was stale for four Index versions and was caught at the top of the Vehicle Combat session. **The Alpha personal vehicle is the Slipstreamer** (Air domain, Light platform, 3-slot budget). See §13D. §38 rule 9 added so this class of drift is caught earlier.

**✅ CONTRADICTION CLOSED 4 SEPTEMBER 2026.** §13C stands: eleven platforms, class neutral, weapons mounting by slot budget. **§13's Layer 1 class determined language is retired.** See the overturn recorded at the head of §13.

The full three-layer combat system — Personal Vehicle Combat, Strider Combat V2.0 crewing (§13), and Interior Breach Combat (Breach Frame / Sentry Frame) — is **live-service scale, not vertical-slice scope**. Building all three at full fidelity for Alpha is out of proportion to what a vertical slice needs to prove.

**Alpha builds a minimum-viable slice only:**

| Layer | Full live-service scope | Alpha-scope subset |
|---|---|---|
| Personal Vehicle Combat | Full 10-platform roster (§13C) | **1 vehicle only** — **Slipstreamer** (Air / Light / 3-slot), deployed from The Wingworks aboard the Drifter (§13D) |
| Strider Combat (main weapon crewing) | Full 200–300-player crewed stations, collateral damage model | **1 turret station**, tested solo/small-crew — not the full-scale crewing model |
| Interior Breach Combat | Full weak-spot RNG pool per class + Scout-role (Astin Fog-Eye) scouting integration + Resource Holding Area risk model | **1 Breach Frame exoskeleton**, tested against **1 fixed, non-RNG weak-spot zone** — no scouting layer, no RNG layer yet |

**Explicitly deferred to Post-Alpha:**
- Full crewed Strider Combat at 200–300-player scale
- Weak-spot RNG system + Scout-role (Astin Fog-Eye) integration
- Resource Holding Area risk/containment model
- The remaining 9 platforms of the §13C roster
- Multi-turret/multi-station crewed main weapon fire

**Design intent:** This subset exists to prove the *shape* of all three combat layers — that a personal vehicle deploys and fights, that a Strider weapon can be crewed and fired, that a Breach Frame can exploit a weak spot and enter contested interior space — without building the full live-service systems (RNG pools, mass crewing, scouting loops) that only make sense once there's a live population to test them against. Vertical-slice scope proves the loop works; Post-Alpha scope proves it works at scale.

## 13B. IRON FRAME SYSTEM (Founder Lock, July 2026)

**Status:** Fully specced ✓ | **UE5.8 C++ rebuild complete ✓ (July 13 session — see correction note below)**

**⚠ V21 STATUS CORRECTION:** V21 marked this system as having a "complete UE5.8 C++ port." That was stale/inaccurate — no current-session source actually existed. The system was **rebuilt from scratch this session**, not ported, using the design below as the authoritative spec. This is now the accurate, current status. Lesson logged in §38: verify actual file existence before trusting a prior "complete" marker.

**Core redesign (supersedes any earlier "Breach Frame / Sentry Frame variant" language):** There is **one Iron Frame chassis**. Role is not chosen at creation — it emerges entirely from equipped loadout, Fallout-76-style. The frame fills the Interior Breach Combat layer (§13) as the vehicle-scale equivalent for close/interior fighting — COGHEIM has no personal handheld weapons; the Iron Frame is correctly classified as a vehicle, not a character ability kit.

**Platform name clarified:** "Iron Frame" is the single unified platform name. "Breach" and "Sentry" are configuration *descriptors* (what a given loadout is optimized for), not separate chassis types — consistent with the mount-driven role model above.

**Mount system — 5 slots, locked:**
| Mount | Options | Armor Dependency |
|---|---|---|
| Left Arm | **Sucker Punch** only (learned unlock) | Requires Left Arm armor equipped |
| Right Arm | Choose 1 of 3: Aetheric Repulsor / Arc Coil Discharger / Boiler Lance | Requires Right Arm armor equipped |
| Shoulder A | Choose from 6-item roster (no duplicates vs. Shoulder B) | Requires Chest armor equipped |
| Shoulder B | Choose from 6-item roster (no duplicates vs. Shoulder A) | Requires Chest armor equipped |
| Back | Wings / Water Jetpack / Aetherum Grenade Launcher / Aetheric Resonance Array | Requires Waist armor equipped |

**Mount activation rule:** Paired, not global — each mount only functions when its physically matched armor piece is equipped. Bare-frame (zero armor) is pilotable but mounts non-functional, zero damage resistance, negligible unarmed damage.

**Full weapon/tool roster:**
- **Sucker Punch** 🥊 — deep-sea-diver-style gun-knife; melee/point-blank only; single breech-loaded shot; **retractable blade housed in a brass sheath sleeve via a spring-and-rail mechanism** (not fixed-blade, locked July 2026 from concept art); **"OUCH" engraved one letter per knuckle** (index/middle/ring/pinky) across a brass knuckle-duster ridge on the gauntlet; insta-kills unarmored infantry/NPCs and Tier 1 Armor Set pilots; heavy damage only (no insta-kill) vs. mid/high-tier pilots; 2+ minute reload — a true one-shot panic button, not a primary weapon. Concept credited to Boom's friend Nate (dev-notes credit only; in-game name stays "Sucker Punch")
- **Right-arm-eligible (3):** Aetheric Repulsor (compressed-Aether cannon, single blast) / Arc Coil Discharger (Tesla-coil chain-lightning) / Boiler Lance (pressurized steam jet, close-range burn)
- **Shoulder-eligible 6-item roster:** Ram Rod (tool-or-weapon, shoulder-only) / Cinder Volley (flare-rocket barrage pod) / Grind-Barrel Cannon (gyro-flywheel autocannon) / Grappling (tool) / Breach Laser (tool — folds in the old "Cutting-Tool" concept; breach capability = Breach Laser equipped, not a separate part type) / the 3 right-arm weapons above are also shoulder-eligible
- **Back Mount roster:** Wings (air mobility) / Water Jetpack (aquatic/amphibious) / Aetherum Grenade Launcher (AoE offense) / **Aetheric Resonance Array** — squad support, passively broadcasts Zone-Known weak-spot visibility to nearby squadmates (does not reveal exact subpoint — that stays Scout-Officer-exclusive); a novel mechanic only possible because it hooks into COGHEIM's own weak-spot RNG system

**8 Armor Body Slots (distinct from the 4 weapon/tool Mounts — mounts=offense/utility, body slots=defense/stats):**
| Slot | Stat Contribution |
|---|---|
| Chest, Waist | Integrity (core health) |
| Left Arm, Right Arm | Power (weapon/tool capacity) |
| Left Leg, Right Leg, Feet | Mobility (movement/sprint) |
| Helmet | Scan/Visibility (reduces weak-spot scan time) |

**Armor Sets:** 5 tiers per class-specific line (one per Pillar Tree depth checkpoint via Iron Veleth), plus universal cross-class sets unlocking through Universal Academy tiers (8-discipline shared tree).

**Unlock pipeline (per piece, per tier — 8 slots × 5 tiers × 5 classes = 200 class-specific + universal sets):**
1. Iron Veleth research node grants knowledge/eligibility
2. NPC-given Plan quest (one-time find, permanent once acquired) — housed in the existing 625-crafting-quest ledger bucket
3. Crafting itself is deliberately resource-expensive/hard
4. **T5 (capstone tier) additionally gated** behind crafting skill level = Ironwork Mastery (standalone skill, levels from hands-on crafting) **AND** Foundrywork (named Universal Academy discipline) both at threshold tier

**Resourcing chain (3-tier, template for all future vehicle crafting):**
Ironwork Scrap (raw — combat salvage: weak-spot wreckage/Colossus debris/defeated pilots, AND city/urban salvage in Strider interior zones for PvE-focused players) → **Ironwork Alloy** (refined intermediate) → finished piece (armor slot or weapon/tool)

**Acquisition:** The base Iron Frame chassis can be (a) found hidden in the world, (b) earned through quests/events — neither requires crafting — or (c) built from scratch via its own dedicated questline, separate from per-piece armor Plan quests.

**External warfare counter role:** Iron Frame is fragile vs. all vehicle-scale units by design — Light vehicles (Dust-Skimmer, Slipstreamer) deal standard damage; Medium (Gut-Ripper, Sky-Sweeper) deal 2x damage; Heavy (Iron-Jaw, Thronewalker, Iron-Hail) are a 2-shot kill plus AoE splash; Colossus AoE incidentally damages/kills Iron Frames caught in blast radius. Design intent: external role is avoidance/flanking/boarding via speed and mobility (Wings, Water Jetpack), not frontline vehicle combat — no favorable matchup against any vehicle weight class.

**🟢 V32 — stale canon bug found and fixed by the compiler:** the Iron Frame source still carried `EStriderClass::Bastion` from before the Bastion→Blackjack rename. It only surfaced because its enum name collided with MAXIOM's during UHT. Corrected to `Blackjack`, and the enum renamed `EIronFrameStriderClass` to clear the collision. **Real technical debt created:** two enums now describe the same five Strider classes (`EIronFrameStriderClass` in the primary module, `EStriderClass` in `CogheimMaxiom`). The proper fix is a shared core types module — logged in §37.

**🟢 V32 — Iron Frame is NOT its own module.** It lives inside the primary game module at `Source\CogheimAlpha\Public\IronFrame\` and `Source\CogheimAlpha\Private\IronFrame\`. Anyone looking for a `CogheimIronFrame` module will not find one.

**Blackjack weak-spot pool — Sally Port:** Blackjack is a military fortress, not a civilian transit hub (correction locked this session). Weak-spot pool named **Sally Port**, three candidate subpoints locked: **two hatches and a hinge seam**.

**Locomotion, jump, and free-spin — LOCKED (July 2026, from concept art review):**
- **Movement physics:** "Roller skate" model — momentum-based, continuous acceleration/deceleration (no instant start-stop), turn radius widens as speed increases (tight turns at low speed, wide arcs at high speed, no pivot-in-place while moving). Mobility stat (from Leg+Feet armor) feeds max speed.
- **Jump:** Height/distance scales with the Mobility stat — same progression hook as top speed. No double-jump.
- **Free Spin — NUMERICALLY LOCKED (V32):** Base rate **720°/s**, scaled by Pilotage proficiency on the same 40%→100% table below (Untrained **288°/s** → Tier 5 **720°/s**). Always resolves along the **shortest angular path**. Fully decoupled from travel direction/momentum — spinning to attack or defend does not change where the frame is actually moving. Usable on ground or mid-air. Non-spin airborne turning obeys the same speed-limited turn-rate curve as ground movement.
- **`SnapFacingDegrees` — engineering constraint (locked):** instantaneous facing snap exists for **initialization, teleport, and respawn only**. Never a gameplay verb; not exposed to player input or gameplay-facing Blueprint nodes.
- **Canopy:** Chest-mounted open cockpit, exposed pilot seat (per concept art). Player can open/close at will, anytime, like a car door — purely cosmetic/functional (mount/dismount only), zero combat mechanical effect either way (doesn't disable weapons, doesn't expose the pilot to bypass-armor damage).

**Pilotage — LOCKED (Universal Academy discipline, gates all locomotion):** All locomotion (basic skating movement/steering, Jump, Free Spin) must be learned via the Academy research tree — not available by default. An untrained pilot can move, but clumsily/slowly; capability scales progressively as research nodes unlock (not a hard lock-out):
| Tier | Locomotion Capability |
|---|---|
| Untrained | 40% |
| Tier 1 | 55% |
| Tier 2 | 70% |
| Tier 3 | 85% |
| Tier 4 | 95% |
| Tier 5 | 100% |
- ✅ **RESOLVED V32:** Free Spin's Pilotage gating scales on the **same** 40%→100% table (288°/s → 720°/s). No separate curve.
- ✅ **RESOLVED (V33 → V35).** V33 locked "test bug, fix the test only" and parked the tuning. **V35 closes it properly:** `JumpVelocityPerMobilityPoint` raised **2.0 → 6.0**, and a **committed-arc airborne gate** was added to `ULocomotionComponent`. Full tier table, air-control rules, and the unwired-seam caveat are in **§13H (Locks 3, 4, 6)**. Verified by 4 new passing specs.
- **Also open:** exact node placement/naming within the Academy tree (which discipline, how many tiers) — not yet decided

**Balance model (Founder Locked, July 13 rebuild session):**
- **Lock A:** No set bonus — stats are a pure sum of equipped pieces (no synergy bonus for matching-tier full sets)
- **Lock B:** Power is a numeric budget with a two-gate mount-functioning system (a mount needs both its paired armor piece equipped AND sufficient Power budget from that armor's tier to activate)
- Full numeric balance table locked: per-mount Power costs, per-arm Power pool by armor tier (T1 pool of 6 through T5 pool of 16), Integrity/Mobility/Scan stat scaling by tier, external damage multipliers by vehicle weight class (all flagged as **placeholder numbers pending a tuning pass** — see §37)

**Architecture (separation-of-concerns pattern — now the studio's reference pattern for future systems):**
- Pure, world-free resolution logic lives in two Blueprint Function Libraries (Loadout Library, Combat Library)
- Three thin Actor Components serve as replicated state holders that delegate to the libraries — they hold state, they don't compute it
- Ownership/unlock truth (Iron Veleth, Quest, Crafting economies) is consumed through an injected `IIronFrameUnlockProvider` interface — **the frame never re-implements those economies**, it only asks

**Breach resolution mode:** Ships `EBreachResolutionMode::FixedZone` for Alpha, honoring §13A's deferral of weak-spot RNG and the Scout layer. `DeterministicRoll` (the full RNG mode) is reserved for Post-Alpha, built to activate behind a **single flag flip requiring zero rewrite** — the RNG logic already exists in the architecture, just gated off.

**Build package:** 15 C++ source files (9 header/source pairs, Public/Private split) + UE Automation Spec (20 checks across 6 describe blocks) + README. All 20 numeric test expectations independently verified via Python logic mirror (20/20 passing) before delivery — UE headers can't compile in the session sandbox, so Shadow PC handles actual compilation and remains the final verification step.

**Three open founder items (non-blocking, carried forward):**
1. Aetheric Resonance Array squad-broadcast networking — belongs in its own Post-Alpha session
2. Authoritative source of pilot heritage class (§26A) — needs confirmation for the Scavenger scan-speed bonus interaction
3. All balance numbers above are placeholders pending a full tuning pass

**Art direction correction (July 31) — "worn exoskeleton with a visible human pilot," not a standalone robot:** Early 3D/image generations were reading as a sealed, faceless robot rather than a piloted exoskeleton. Global style rules and the base chassis/helmet/chest prompts were updated to explicitly cite **Iron Man, Fallout 76 power armor, BioShock's Big Daddy diving-suit silhouette, and the Matrix Revolutions APU suits** as reference anchors, with negative prompts extended to block faceless robot head, sealed robot torso, and non-human silhouette outputs. **Fallback lever if robot-drift persists:** replace "exoskeleton" as the lead term with "human figure wearing an open mechanical suit."

**Asset sheets delivered (July 31):** Full Iron Frame Exoskeleton Asset Sheet — base chassis, all 8 armor slot pieces, 3 right-arm mount options, 5 shoulder mount options, 4 back mount options, and the locked Sucker Punch left-arm mount — each with a ChatGPT-ready image-reference prompt and a text-to-3D fallback prompt. 3D generation pipeline: Tripo AI (tripo3d.ai, Pro tier, $19.90/mo, confirmed sufficient for commercial/shipped-asset licensing), Smart Mesh mode over HD Model for game-ready topology. Workflow: define asset → ChatGPT reference image → Tripo image-to-3D (complex assets) or direct Tripo text-to-3D (simple assets). → [3D model generation workflow for alpha launch](https://claude.ai/chat/b5247ed1-4c2b-4ddf-bb38-01b82908f559)

## 13C. VEHICLE ROSTER & UNIVERSAL MODULAR ARMAMENT SYSTEM (Founder Lock, July 2026)

**Status:** Fully specced ✓ | Rival-Test validated ✓

**Core design innovation — Universal Modular Armament:** Weapons are not locked to specific vehicle classes. Each weapon carries a **slot cost** (Light = 1, Medium = 2, Heavy = 4) and mounts on any platform with sufficient slot budget. Platform slot budgets range from Iron Frame (2 slots) through Strider turret hardpoints (12 slots). Confirmed as genuinely distinct from existing MMO weapon-class-lock conventions under the Rival Test (§2A).

**Full vehicle roster — 11 platforms (UPDATED V34: air domain 3 → 4):**
| Domain | Platforms |
|---|---|
| Land (3) | Dust-Skimmer, Gut-Ripper, Iron-Jaw |
| Air (4) | Slipstreamer, **Lantern** 🆕, Sky-Sweeper, Iron-Hail |
| Water (3) | Tide-Runner, **The Waterhammer**, Iron-Tide |
| Elite (1) | Thronewalker |

**Weight class is RELATIONAL, not absolute (Founder Lock, Aug 2026):** Light = Slipstreamer scale; Medium = anything between Slipstreamer and Iron-Hail; Heavy = Iron-Hail scale. See §13E.

**Naming note:** "Keel-Cleaver" was proposed and rejected for the medium water vehicle; **The Waterhammer** is the locked replacement.

**9-weapon catalog across 3 damage categories:**
| Category | Weapons |
|---|---|
| Kinetic | The Jigsaw, Bore-Hammer, The Sledgebore |
| Aetheric | Sparkjaw, Coil-Lance, The Furnace Eye |
| Ordnance | Skip-Shot, Barrage Nest, The Grave Caller |

**Crafting materials by category:**
- Kinetic: Scrap Iron, Tempered Alloy, Forged Core
- Aetheric: Coil Wire, Charge Cell, Resonant Shard
- Ordnance: Powder Compound, Warhead Casing, Guidance Core

Each recipe carries Ironmarks cost, an Iron Veleth research gate, and craft time scaling ~3x per tier (10–70 minutes across tiers). **Rare-tier materials (Forged Core, Resonant Shard, Guidance Core) tie to Colossus wreckage drops** (§14). Nine net-new crafting-unlock quests were added this session, bringing the crafting quest ledger from 625 → **634** (§24 quest ledger updated accordingly).

**Platform slot budget / compatibility matrix (locked):**
| Platform Class | Slot Budget |
|---|---|
| Iron Frame (§13B) | 2 slots — **excluded from this 9-weapon system**, uses its own fixed 5-mount roster |
| Light vehicles (Dust-Skimmer, Slipstreamer, Tide-Runner) | 3 slots |
| Medium vehicles (Gut-Ripper, Sky-Sweeper, The Waterhammer) | 5 slots |
| Heavy vehicles (Iron-Jaw, Iron-Hail, Iron-Tide) | 8 slots |
| Thronewalker (elite) | 10 slots |
| Strider turret/missile hardpoints | 12 slots |

**Vehicle unlock progression — CONFIRMED (was open in prior versions):** All vehicles use a quest-plus-research unlock model similar to Battlefield 5's progression system, applying universally across every vehicle class — not Thronewalker-exclusive as earlier drafts implied.

**Coil-Lance on Light platforms:** Technically legal by slot math (fits a 3-slot Light budget) but a deliberately poor build choice by design. **No hard restriction rule was added** — the founder's explicit preference was to let the slot economy self-regulate rather than bolt on an exception rule. (This resolves the "open item" flagged in V21 — the restriction was considered and declined, not left pending.)

**Explicitly deferred to a future dedicated session:** Consumables (ammo types, repair kits, boosts). Water traversal for Striders remains a working assumption, not locked World Canon.

### 13C-1. VEHICLE ASSET NAMING & FOLDER CONVENTION (Founder Lock, August 2026)

**Established via the Slipstreamer build; the template for all 10 platforms in the §13C roster.**

**Folders — split by domain:** `Content/Vehicles/<Domain>/<VehicleName>/`, `<Domain>` ∈ { Air, Land, Water, Elite }

**Rigging — Option A (locked):** separate **static meshes per moving part**, no skeleton, no AnimBP; moving parts rotated via code/Blueprint. Chosen to match the thin-Actor-Component house pattern already used in the Wingworks Combat C++ — the consistent choice, not the easiest one (§38 rule 8).

| Asset type | Pattern | Slipstreamer |
|---|---|---|
| Static mesh | `SM_<VehicleName>_<PartName>` | `SM_Slipstreamer_Hull`, `SM_Slipstreamer_NacelleL`, `SM_Slipstreamer_NacelleR`, `SM_Slipstreamer_TailWheel` |

| Master material | `M_<VehicleName>` | `M_Slipstreamer` |
| Material instance | `MI_<VehicleName>` | `MI_Slipstreamer` |
| Physics asset / collision | `PA_<VehicleName>_<PartName>` | `PA_Slipstreamer_Hull` |

**Design evolution, recorded:** the Slipstreamer began as a fixed-wing glider with folding wings to solve room clearance. It became a **VTOL twin-boom-thruster** design (Avatar aircraft silhouette language, Cogheim Aetheric tech). **Folding wings were eliminated** — VTOL natively solves the clearance constraint that folding wings existed to address. Downstream consequence caught and fixed: the Drifter prop `wing_rigging_bench` became **`nacelle_maintenance_rig`**.

4. Flat-root zip layout → **nested `Public/Private`** to match siblings
5. Missing deploy-script integration line — now documented and applied

Two sessions produced **incompatible** Sky-Sweeper specifications. Both are written up as founder locks. **This Index does not pick one.** Resolve before any Sky-Sweeper art, spec, or crafting work proceeds.

## 13G. SYNDICATE AIR STATION — POST-ALPHA DESIGN PROPOSAL (August 2026)

**Classification: Post-Alpha, Design Proposal. NOT a Founder Lock. No spec, art brief, or module work has started, and none should until Alpha ships.**

A Syndicate-owned mobile airborne asset, larger and more ornate than Iron-Hail, roaming separately from the Strider.

**The Rival Test concern, and the answer that resolved it:** a *second* player-owned mobile settlement would dilute the core pillar — *if every Syndicate has both a Strider and an Air Station, which one is home?* The founder's answer gives it a clean functional identity: **the Air Station IS the Strider's airfield module**, absorbing flying-vessel traffic that would otherwise consume Strider hangar space. Not a second home — a specialised organ. That resolves the pillar conflict.

**Docking concept:** an **umbilical connection housing an elevator** between Air Station and Strider, arriving at a central connection point. While connected, both enter **Running Lights** state (unattackable). This is diegetically consistent — a physical visible connection rather than a menu prompt — and reuses an established safety-state mechanic rather than inventing one.

**Two flagged risks, unresolved by design:**
- 🔴 **Technical scale.** Docking two independently moving reference frames — one airborne, one ground-based, different movement axes and terrain behaviour — is a **harder version** of Strider-to-Strider docking. It needs its own early prototype. Do not assume RFA docking logic simply extends to it.
- 🔴 **Exploit surface.** "Unattackable under any conditions while connected" is a **free invulnerability toggle** during a raid, war, or Colossus event. Needs boundaries — connect/disconnect cooldowns, a vulnerability window during transit, or eligibility rules like those Colossus targeting already requires.

---

## 13H. IRON FRAME SCALE, JUMP & AIR CONTROL — SIX FOUNDER LOCKS (August 2026)

**Status:** All six LOCKED ✓ | **VERIFIED — 674/674 Automation specs passing, zero failures** ✓ | `Rebuild All: 1 succeeded, 0 failed.` ✓
**Session record:** `claude_COGHEIM_IronFrame_Scale_and_Imperial_Units_Aug2026.md` (in project knowledge).

**23 new specs, all green:** 4 Iron Frame chassis-scale, 4 jump / air-control, 15 MAXIOM imperial units.

---

### 🔒 LOCK 1 — Iron Frame chassis height: **8 ft 6 in** (259.08 cm)

**Previously undefined anywhere in the codebase.** A full-tree search found no height, no scale constant, nothing — chassis size existed only as an *implication* of what it could fit through. That is now a real constant in `IronFrame/IronFrameTypes.h`:

```cpp
namespace CogheimIronFrame
{
    inline constexpr float ChassisHeightCm = 259.08f;    // 8 ft 6 in
    inline constexpr float MaxChassisHeightCm = 300.0f;  // Wingworks hangar door
}
```

Bounded by openings **already in the code**, not invented for the occasion:
| Opening | Metric | Imperial | Consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Drifter doorway | 100 × 200 cm | 3 ft 3 in × 6 ft 7 in | **Iron Frame cannot pass** |
| Drifter corridor | 150 cm wide | 4 ft 11 in | Human-scale circulation only |
| Wingworks hangar door | 500 × 300 cm | 16 ft 5 in × 9 ft 10 in | Hard ceiling on chassis size |

---

### 🔒 LOCK 2 — Drifter interiors stay human-scale

**You walk the Drifter as a person. You mount up in the Wingworks hangar and deploy through the hangar door. The Iron Frame is equipment you board, not a costume you wear.** The hangar is the frame's only way in or out. **No interior deck gets widened.**

**Why this is the stronger design:** it keeps interiors intimate and human, which is what makes *"your city is alive and you live inside it"* land. It also gives the exo a real **threshold moment** — suiting up and rolling out — instead of reducing it to a movement mode you're permanently wearing.

**Four passing specs (`Cogheim.IronFrame.Iron Frame chassis scale`) encode this so it cannot erode silently.** Widen a doorway or grow the chassis and the suite fails. *This is a design pillar defended by tests rather than by memory — the pattern worth repeating on every other pillar.*

---

### 🔒 LOCK 3 — `JumpVelocityPerMobilityPoint` = **6.0** (was 2.0)

**⚠ This deliberately overturns the V33 decision.** V33 locked "Option A — fix the test only, locked locomotion untouched," and **parked** the tuning question with a warning that 3.3 cm / 20.4 cm would read as broken. **That parked item is now closed by changing the locked value.** Recorded as a visible overturn, per §38 rule 12.

At 2.0 the frame ran **11.2 mph** but cleared **8 in** at Tier 5 and **1.3 in** untrained — *a worse jumper than the person wearing it.*

Authored curve at 6.0 (100 Mobility). **Apex scales with the square of the proficiency multiplier**, which is what makes Pilotage feel earned rather than linear:
| Tier | Vertical | Hang time | Running-jump gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Untrained | 11.6 in | 0.49 s | 3 ft 3 in |
| Tier 1 | 1 ft 10 in | 0.67 s | 6 ft 1 in |
| Tier 2 | 2 ft 11 in | 0.86 s | 9 ft 10 in |
| Tier 3 | 4 ft 4 in | 1.04 s | 14 ft 6 in |
| Tier 4 | 5 ft 5 in | 1.16 s | 18 ft 2 in |
| **Tier 5** | **6 ft 0 in** | **1.22 s** | **20 ft 1 in** |

Tier 5 clears roughly the chassis' own shoulder — **it vaults onto wreckage and hull ledges; it does not hop over another Iron Frame.** Two passing specs assert the full tier table and tie Tier 5 directly to `ChassisHeightCm`, so the two locks cannot drift apart.

---

### 🔒 LOCK 4 — Committed jump arc: facing turns, heading and speed do not

**A pilot can turn to aim mid-jump. They cannot change where they land.**

Single gate on `ULocomotionComponent`:
```cpp
void SetAirborne(bool bInAirborne);   // pawn mirrors UJumpComponent::IsGrounded()
```

While airborne, `UpdateLocomotion()` returns immediately — no acceleration, no steering, **and no deceleration**. That last part is the subtle one: `Deceleration` models **ground friction**, so letting the normal glide-down branch run would bleed speed in mid-air for no physical reason.

`UpdateFacing()` and `SnapFacingDegrees()` are deliberately **not** gated — the frame can bring weapons to bear mid-arc. This **reuses the existing locked Facing/Heading separation (§13B); no new system was introduced.**

Two passing specs: the committed arc (full throttle + full steering for 20 airborne ticks changes nothing; control returns on landing) and no-friction-in-air (throttle released for a full 1.22 s hang coasts at takeoff speed). **Default is `false`, so every pre-existing locomotion test is unaffected — confirmed, all still pass.**

**Rival Test note:** committed-arc traversal is a real differentiator. MMO movement almost universally grants full air control; committing the arc makes the *decision to jump* the skill expression rather than the mid-air correction, and it pairs with the weak-spot and vehicle-scale combat model rather than fighting it.

---

### 🔒 LOCK 5 — MAXIOM reads out in **IMPERIAL**

Cogheim is post-apocalyptic Earth; the surviving measure is feet, yards, miles. Consistent with the lore layer, which already speaks miles (`LoreScaleDiameterMiles`) while gameplay math runs metric.

**The three-layer rule — do not confuse these:**
| Layer | Unit | Changeable? |
|---|---|---|
| **Engine / simulation** | Centimetres (1 uu = 1 cm) | **No.** Physics, collision, navigation all depend on it |
| **Stored constants** | Metric, named for the unit (`...Cm`, `...Km`) | No — keep the naming discipline |
| **MAXIOM display** | **Imperial** | Yes — this is the locked change |

**Convert at the readout, never in the simulation.**

New in `CogheimMaxiom`: `MaxiomUnitsLibrary.h/.cpp` + `MaxiomUnitsSpec.cpp`, using exact international factors. Display picks unit by magnitude:
| Magnitude | Reads as |
|---|---|
| under 1 ft | `9.4 in` |
| under 100 ft | `6 ft 3 in` (or `6 ft` when flush) |
| under 1 mile | `482 yd` |
| 1 mile and up | `3.2 mi` |

Speed reads `11.2 mph`, signed. **Fail-closed:** negative or non-finite returns `---` and logs a **Warning** — correctly Warning, not Error, per §38 rule 14 (verified in the run: tests pass *and* the warning appears).

**15 passing specs anchored to live constants** — chassis height, doorway, hangar door, corridor, both jump apexes, disc diameter, Grindwalker sensor range, both hull speeds — plus the rounding carry (60.95 cm reads `2 ft`, never `1 ft 12 in`). *Anchoring to live constants rather than literals is §38 rule 15 applied correctly on the first pass.*

**🔒 Standing rule:** every **new** MAXIOM readout uses this library. Existing readouts convert **as they are touched.**

---

### 🔒 LOCK 6 — Jump is an explicitly UNWIRED SEAM until Iron Frames have meshes

`UJumpComponent` computes a height that **nothing consumes.** No collision, no capsule sweep, no ceiling test.

The header carries a blunt `UNWIRED SEAM` block stating that **jump traversal must not be reported as implemented**, and that the owning pawn is responsible for mirroring `IsGrounded()` into `SetAirborne()`.

**Wire it after the art pass**, then re-validate the authored arc against real geometry. Until then: **the vertical figures are sound math; the horizontal gap figures are a design target, not a measured result.**

*This lock is the most valuable one in the set for external communication — it is the difference between "the Iron Frame jumps 6 feet" and "the Iron Frame's jump math is verified and awaits collision integration." Use the second.*

---

### 🔴 Build.cs correction — flat modules need an explicit include path

An initial claim that no `Build.cs` change was needed was **wrong, and is recorded as wrong.** `CogheimDrifter` is a **flat module** — all sources at the module root, no `Public/` folder — so UBT never put its headers on the public include path and the cross-module include failed with **`C1083`**. Fixed with `PublicIncludePaths.Add(ModuleDirectory)`.

**The lesson, generalised:** declaring a module in `PublicDependencyModuleNames` gets you **linkage, not headers.**

⚠ **Still latent in six modules:** `CogheimSRF`, `CogheimComms`, `CogheimWeather`, `CogheimWeaponsCrafting`, `CogheimLogin`, `CogheimWorldMap`. **None are included cross-module today, so none are broken** — but the first one that is will hit exactly this. Logged in §37 as a known trap rather than pre-emptively "fixed."

---

| `IronFrame/LocomotionComponent.h` | `SetAirborne()` / `IsAirborne()` / `bAirborne` |
| `IronFrame/LocomotionComponent.cpp` | Airborne early-return in `UpdateLocomotion` |

### 🎨 Handoff to the art / visibility session

These locks exist partly to **make the art session possible.** What it now has to work with:

- **Iron Frame: 8 ft 6 in standing**, must clear a 9 ft 10 in hangar door and fit a 16 ft 5 in opening. Silhouette is a **piloted industrial chassis — a machine you climb into, not a walking tank.**
- **Drifter interiors are human-scale** — 3 ft 3 in doorways, 4 ft 11 in corridors, decks 15 ft 9 in to 21 ft 4 in. **Design them for people.**
- **Jump reads 6 ft at mastery with a 1.22 s hang** — animation needs a real crouch, launch, committed float, and heavy landing. **The frame cannot steer in the air, so the arc should *look* committed.**
- 🔴 Still **no meshes for Iron Frame, Slipstreamer, or any NPC.**

**Wiring jump to movement is the first task that becomes possible once a chassis mesh exists.** That single dependency now sits under Lock 6, the art track, and the animation brief simultaneously — which makes **the chassis mesh the highest-leverage art asset in the project.**

## 16. MAXIOM & AXIOM SYSTEM V3.0 (Founder Lock, July 2026)

**Status:** Fully specced ✓ | **UE5.8 C++ port COMPLETE ✓ (July 22, 2026)** — corrects the prior "Coded ✓ (C#, 39 tests)" status; module is `CogheimMaxiom`, Python mirror 40/40 passing, delivered as `MAXIOM_AlphaTrack_Carryover_V1.zip` (nested-structure zip, preserves UE `Public/`/`Private/` module layout — deliberately not the usual flat `-j` convention)
→ [Axiom and Maxiom game design specification](https://claude.ai/chat/7dc3ae56-aa9a-43fe-bdff-eacd457df13a)

**Core philosophy (unchanged, reinforced):** No floating menus, no HUD elements that break the world. Everything the player needs lives on the MAXIOM wrist device — the game's sole diegetic UI, equivalent in role to the Pip-Boy.

**Two components:**
- **Axiom** — a player-designed AI companion android aboard every Strider (concourse butler-bot). Same personality core as MAXIOM, different scale/embodiment.
- **MAXIOM (Mobile Axiom)** — the personal wrist device; syncs with Axiom when aboard a Strider.

**8 modules (expanded from 7 — SIGNAL added this session):** Crew Log / World Map / Resources / Build Queue / Comms / GKF Status / Class Telemetry / **SIGNAL (World Radio)**

**SIGNAL — World Radio (Tab 8, standalone module, locked):**
- Two tiers: **Strider Station** (one per Strider, curated by a Signal Chief) and **World Signal** (25 timezone-bound global slots, panel-approved)
- **World Host** is a prestige tier requiring existing Signal Chief status to apply
- Applications judged by a small internal panel (Boom + trusted reviewers) on criteria modeled after the MegaGrants pitch process: experience, channel vision, growth plan, existing following, COGHEIM fit
- Hosts program exclusively from an **Approved Track Library** (Suno-generated + community-contributed tracks that have passed review)
- Advertiser/sponsorship audio pipeline **explicitly deferred** — it touches the ad-free product stance, FTC disclosure obligations, and platform policy; flagged as a business-model decision requiring its own dedicated session, not a UI spec matter

**Drifter Mode module locking (real gap found and fixed this session):** Only 4 of 8 tabs are active during the tutorial. Combat and Strider Status are locked until the player boards a Syndicate Strider. Build Queue is additionally gated behind the first research node.

**Council Crew Log integration:** Built against the confirmed 8-role Council system (Commander, Vaultmaster, IronChief, SignalChief, ForgeChief, TradeMaster, ArchiveWarden, Envoy) and the `Strider.CouncilRoster` shape from prior sessions.

**Known honest gap (not guessed, flagged):** `GetDaysOnStrider` (probation clock) is not yet exposed by the confirmed roster interface — left as a deliberate stub rather than an invented answer.

**C++ architecture (July 22 port):** All 8 modules present as UE classes; systems not yet built (World Map, Resources, Build Queue, Combat) return fail-closed shaped stubs (`bSuccess=false`, empty data) rather than fabricated values. Crew Log wired to a real `UMaxiomCouncilCrewLogAdapter` against the confirmed 8-role Council enum. Strider Status dispatches per class, with Grindwalker GKF telemetry matching the shape of the already-coded system (§6). Signal is a thin pass-through to the SignalSystem built earlier the same session. Two canon errors caught and corrected during the port itself: the Bastion→Blackjack rename hadn't propagated into this module yet, and the Drifter Mode module-locking behavior (below) had never actually been implemented in any prior code pass despite being spec'd.

**Architecture:** `MaxiomSystem.cs` is a pure aggregation layer with narrow reader interfaces per module — wires to real subsystems where they exist (SME, Council, Comms), leaves honest stubs elsewhere rather than fabricating data contracts.

**GKF:** Status readable in real time; GKF hum pitch maps 1:1 to SpinLevel.
**Context-aware field:** Split-panel layout (combat left / navigation right); Field Mode and Glyph Wheel are dedicated spec pages; class variants exist for all 5 Strider classes.

## 16A. PLAYER COMMUNICATIONS SYSTEM (Founder Lock, July 2026)

**Status:** Fully specced ✓ | **All 4 subsystems ported to UE5.8 C++ ✓ — COMPLETE** (corrects V21, which showed only 2 of 4 ported)

**Spec history (3 documents, built in sequence):**
- **V1.0** — the original 6-channel text system
- **Addendum V1.1** — added Direct Comm Links (persistent server-wide DM threads, **2–8 participants**, corrects V21's "1-to-8" phrasing) and Guilded-style Syndicate Voice Decks
- **Addendum V1.2** — locked the Officer role and the full Commander→Officer→Team chain of command per explicit founder direction

**Architecture:** Server-authoritative, UE5.8 C++, dedicated server. All comms surfaces live inside the MAXIOM device UI — no floating windows, consistent with the platform-wide diegetic-only rule. All proximity distance checks run in **Strider Reference Frame local space** (§5B) — a direct dependency on the SRF netcode lock.

**Four subsystems — build package: 4 complete header/implementation pairs + 4 UE Automation Spec files, 52 acceptance checks total:**
| # | System | Function |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **CommsRouter** | Text channel routing across 6 diegetically justified channels: Proximity, Syndicate, Party, Whisper, Black-Maw Station, Distress. Rate limiting, block/mute, profanity filter hooks, Distress cooldown, SRF-aware proximity checks |
| 2 | **TeamManager** | Commander → Officer → Team chain of command. **This is the unified role-lookup service every other comms subsystem consumes** — not a standalone system |
| 3 | **VoiceChannelManager** | Guilded-style Voice Deck — persistent named rooms, role-gated and team-scoped, delegates all access decisions to TeamManager rather than re-implementing permission logic |
| 4 | **DirectCommLinkService** | Persistent 2–8-player DM threads; shares CommsRouter's profile store so block/mute/rate-limit behave identically across every channel type |

**No global chat channel — locked, deliberate.** At 150,000-player server scale, a global channel is unmoderatable and undermines the "each Strider is a real community" premise central to the Rival Test.

**Chain of command (TeamManager, locked):**
- Commanders talk only to Officers; Officers talk to their assigned teams
- Cap of 8 Officers per Syndicate
- Anti-poaching: reassigning crew between teams requires Commander authority
- **Automatic dissolution:** all Officer appointments dissolve when a Commander changes via Mutiny/Succession (§12) — `DissolveAllTeamsOnCommanderChange` is the integration hook into the already-locked political system
- Officer role is comms-management-only — cannot interact with political systems

**Delivery note:** Stale C# reference source was deleted in two cleanup passes as each system's C++ port completed — the output folder now holds only the 3 spec docs and the final C++ files, not legacy C# alongside them (an explicit founder preference, not the studio-wide default of retaining C# as reference).

**Open item:** Black-Maw Station voice rooms — not yet resolved.

Any player can tune their MAXIOM device to any of the **36 total channels** (12 in-Strider blocks + 24 outside-world zone stations, below) from anywhere in the world.

A systemic duplicate-title bug (caused by a templated generation script) was caught and fixed via a full structural rebuild rather than patching individual tracks.

Two contributor-facing documents were produced to support external submissions: a DJ instructions doc (personality notes + example lines for all 24 stations) and a world/mood primer for contributors unfamiliar with gaming or COGHEIM specifically. - **#47 "Credits: Nothing Walking Is Lost"** — reworked as a genuine structural callback to "The Ground Remembers" (mirrors its hybrid folk-metal duet DNA, sub-bass heartbeat motif, whole-step key lift, hard-stop outro)
- **#48 "No Truer Compass"** — love theme for Tiffany and Michael; reworked from an initial pirate-shanty concept to an Irish-Rovers-style folk ballad, slower and warmer
- Dual-purpose: usable standalone (marketing, personal legacy) as well as woven into in-world audio where appropriate

**"Keep It Moving Forward" (personal recovery variant, non-canon):** Shares "Keep Her Crawling"'s structural bones, reoriented toward personal perseverance rather than in-game narrative. Noted this session as potentially viable as a standalone release outside COGHEIM entirely.

**SECTION V — Player-Prompted Dynamic Song Generation (logged, NOT specced here — future major system):**
- Concept: a player describes a mood/moment; a music-generation API auto-generates a full song and adds it to their live experience — no manual instrument/lyric customization, a pure "tell it what you feel" black box
- **Flagged as a genuinely novel MMO feature** — no current MMO offers real-time generative music tied to player sentiment (passes the Rival Test at concept level, §2A)

## 16C. THE THREE UI LAYERS (Founder ruling, 4 September 2026)

The Index carried only the absolutist form of the diegetic rule. **The permission that allows conventional Shell interfaces existed only in the project instructions and was nowhere in the Index.** Every settings, accessibility and legal screen depends on it. It is written in here.

| Layer | Contents | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| **L0 The Shell** | Boot, login, settings, controls, accessibility, account, legal, credits | **Conventional, styled to belong to Cogheim. Permitted.** |
| **L1 The Frame** | MAXIOM: wrist, goggle lens, world map | Diegetic. Real instruments. No floating windows |
| **L2 The World** | War table, Charter Sign, Signatory Engine, lamps | Props carrying information. No screens, holograms or pixels |

---

## 16D. THE OPTICAL FAMILY (Founder ruling, 4 September 2026)

Three real instruments at three scales, one visual language of brass, glass and lamps behind glass.

- **Crew scale:** the war table, a physical lit relief model (§4B)
- **Personal, deep:** the goggle lens, a flip down eyepiece, carries everything that must be READ
- **Personal, quick:** the wrist MAXIOM, a round gauge cluster, carries shapes and direction

**THE SPLIT LAW:** if it needs letters, it is lens. If it is a needle, a ring, a dial or a lamp, it is wrist. **Numerals are permitted on the wrist. Words are not.**

The war table and the handheld brass disc lens are **one system at two scales**. The goggle lens makes it three. **This is what defines MAXIOM's scope**, which had been open since the system was specced. Closes §4C.10 decision 5 and §37 item 12.

---

## 16E. THE CAMERA LADDER (Founder ruling, 4 September 2026)

```
third person (default)
   └─ wrist raised .......... slight pull in, snap, HOLD to keep up, ~0.3s each way
       └─ lens down ......... tight over the shoulder, half view to glass
           └─ world map ..... own instance, world live behind it
```

The ladder **requires no first person camera**. The interface never flips perspective. The lens is permitted in combat at full cost, with no lockout: you lose half your view and everyone around you can see the lens is down.

**⚠ FIRST PERSON AS A PLAYER MODE IS OPEN, NOT RULED OUT.** An earlier draft of the 4 September decisions read "no first person camera is ever built." That was a session overreach, corrected the same day, and was never a founder ruling. Third person is primary and the only supported camera through the Slice and Alpha. Motion sickness runs in both directions and **first person is an accessibility axis, not only a preference.**

**LOCKED:**
> **Every authored interior is built to FIRST PERSON TOLERANCES from now on**, whether or not a first person camera is ever offered. Real ceiling heights, real doorway widths, no geometry that only survives at third person distance, texel density that holds at eye height.

Costs nothing today. Costs a rebuild of every interior in the project if deferred, which is the exact failure the level architecture work exists to prevent. **This belongs in the Strider interior brief and in the Level Architecture whitepaper, not only here.**

---

## 16F. CONTROLS (Founder ruling, 4 September 2026)

**One Core layer that never changes** (move, look, interact, MAXIOM, menu, voice) **plus exactly one Context layer at a time**, across **thirteen contexts**: on foot, Iron Frame, land vehicle, watercraft, aircraft, submarine, turret or gunner, Strider helm, Strider gunnery, command and war table, build mode, dialogue, shell and menus.

**Plus a verb spine:** the same physical key does the analogous thing in every context. Left shift is sprint on foot, boost in a frame, throttle up in a craft, full ahead at the helm.

**Full rebind on every action in every context, plus three or four named presets. Required, not optional.** §26F-7 already commits research to *no rapid or repeated input* and §26E-3 commits assistive cues to Ring exemption. A partial rebind breaks both.

---

## 16G. NAVIGATION (Founder ruling, 4 September 2026)

**The wrist is primary. An on screen marker is available as a player option, recommended OFF by default.**

**Two assist cues ship, one per sense:** a pinned wrist readout anchored to a screen corner, and a **directional audio sonar**.

**⚠ CLASSIFICATION REQUIRED:** the sonar must be an **assistive cue, not diegetic sound**, so §26E-3's Ring exemption applies. Otherwise it goes silent exactly when a player is most disoriented.

**NEW REQUIREMENT ON LEVEL ARCHITECTURE:**
> **Every anchor region must be navigable from its own horizon.** Readable silhouettes at distance, landmarks visible across the region, roads that visibly lead somewhere, Striders you can see coming. **This belongs in the region brief before Coldforge Landing starts.**

---

## 16H. THE BOUNDARY OF THE FICTION (Founder ruling, 4 September 2026)

> **Account creation is not in the game.** Email, password, verification, terms, age gate, payment. Web and EOS.
> **Character creation is the first thing inside the fiction**, after EOS identity, before the server connection, client side, in its own level.
> **The diegetic rule switches on at that line and never switches off again.**

---

## 17. CURRENCY STACK

**Status:** Locked (Monetization Master Spec V1.0) ✓

| Currency | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Loyalty Points | Soft / earned-only | Cannot be purchased |
| Ironmarks | Trade / everyday | Hard cap 999,999 |
| Crownmarks | Premium / real-money | Cosmetics / time-skips / Battle Pass / crew slots ONLY |
| Corethrum | Active energy resource | Powers Colossus super weapon |
| Oil / Metal / Wood / Food | Architecture-only seasonal | Post-Alpha; locked to building systems |

**Iron rule:** No currency converts to any other. Nothing giftable.

**🔴 UNRESOLVED CONTRADICTION, flagged V40 — read §17A before citing this table externally.** This table (Ironmarks 999,999 hard cap; Crownmarks buy cosmetics **and** time-skips, Battle Pass, crew slots) predates the Aug 27, 2026 founder-ruled Economy & Trade Depot Spec V1.3 (§17A), which locks a **stricter** Crown Marks rule — livery only, nothing else, ever, no exceptions for time-skips/Battle Pass/crew slots — and does not mention Loyalty Points, a 999,999 Ironmark cap, or Corethrum at all. §17A explicitly supersedes the currency-separation *principle* (it is the more recent, more detailed, founder-ruled document, and cross-references this Index by name). **What is not yet resolved:** whether Loyalty Points and Corethrum still exist as separate resources alongside Iron Marks/Crown Marks, whether the Crownmarks-buy-time-skips/crew-slots language is retired or was already aspirational and never final, and whether the 999,999 cap survives under a model where Iron Marks are explicitly *not* production-minted. **Requires founder ruling before this table is treated as current** — do not present both this section and §17A as simultaneously authoritative in any external material.

## 17A. ECONOMY & TRADE DEPOT SYSTEM V1.3 (Founder-ruled, August 27, 2026)

**Status:** Founder ruled in session. Structural rulings locked; **all numeric values are starting proposals requiring validation against a build**, not tested figures. Source: `COGHEIM_Economy_TradeDepot_Spec_V1.md` v1.3. Related: §12A, §26B.

**The one-line version, as the source document states it:** *"The Depot sells matter. The Order Book sells labor. The Academy sells knowledge. Crown Marks buy only things that can never be sold. Nothing passes between players except through a clerk who writes it all down."*

**🔒 LOCKED — currency architecture.** **Crown Marks:** real-money, buy livery only, never convert to Iron Marks, no gifting, cannot touch combat/research/territory/crafting/resource/political/progression/survival/intelligence/convenience advantage in any form. **Iron Marks:** earned in-game only, never purchasable, **never minted by player production/holdings/mines/foundries/taxed territory** (a deliberate rejection, made to block off-site selling and industrial bot farming), never transfer wallet-to-wallet — every movement passes through an NPC intermediary and is logged. This is a **structural proof**, not a policy promise, and is the intended one-sentence answer to any pay-to-win question.

**🔒 LOCKED — Iron Mark faucets (the only sources):** quest completion, activity completion, event completion (Colossus/Iron Veil), event drops, and randomly-spawned world stashes (no fixed locations, no farmable table — **requires validation:** a per-player find cap is still needed to stop reroll-farming via relogs).

**Solved (structure locked, figures require validation against a build) — the weekly earn ceiling model:** base rate 70 IM/hr unrested, 1.6× rested multiplier over an 8 hr/week rested allowance identical for every account, 10%/hr compounding daily decay. Resulting spread: a Light player (8 hrs/wk) earns ~812 IM/wk; Heavy (40 hrs/wk) earns ~2,449 IM/wk — a **3.02× spread**, matching the founder's ~3× target. A hypothetical 12-hr/day grinder earns only 4.6× a Light player, making unattended farming a poor investment without any hard daily gate. This ceiling is the foundation every price table in §17A is derived from and **must be computed against a real build before any price ships.**

**🔒 LOCKED — the transfer law (constitutional rule):** *no player may transfer anything to another player directly.* No trade windows, no IM gifting, no mailing goods — every transfer is NPC-mediated, logged, and delivered in person at a Depot cashier window (diegetic, not a floating auction menu). This produces complete transaction telemetry as a free byproduct.

**🔒 LOCKED — the Trade Depot, three layers, rejecting a single global auction house** (which would delete logistics from a moving-city game): **Hold** (every Strider's own Depot, always available), **Regional Board** (visibility into other Depots in-region, shipping delay + surcharge), **Docked Exchange** (connected Striders' Depots pool into one market for the duration of the connection — full inventory, instant delivery, lowest fees). Host Strider collects a **2–3% cut** of Docked Exchange trades — the rule that creates the "market capital" role in §12A. **Alpha berth cap LOCKED at three Striders total** (host + two), pending RFA netcode load-test validation; a labeled, unvalidated Post-Alpha stretch target of five exists for planning only and must never appear in fundraising material as achieved.

**🔒 LOCKED — the governing listing rule:** *you can sell matter, you cannot sell status.* Raw/refined/crafted goods are listable; Iron Marks, Crown Marks, all livery, quest items, research progress, blueprints/schematics, certifications, Syndicate rank, territory claims, and event trophies are never listable. Colossus/Iron Veil **salvage** is listable after a 72-hour maturation window; **trophies** (proof of participation) are account-bound forever. Fully assembled Iron Frames: parts yes on the Depot, whole frames no — routed through the Order Book instead, to keep assembly a player skill rather than an anonymous vendor listing.

**🔒 LOCKED — the Order Book** (commission market, separate from the Depot — the Depot sells what exists, the Order Book sells work that doesn't yet): order placed → escrow closes (materials/IM held by the clerk, never touched by the crafter until delivery) → crafter accepts (certification-gated) → delivery or expiry-with-full-refund. Directed orders (naming one specific crafter) are price-capped against computed value + a master premium, to close the cleanest remaining laundering channel.

**🔒 LOCKED — Academy Depots and the knowledge-goods monopoly:** all eight academies (§3A) have a fixed-price Depot, forming a permanent price anchor and new-player onramp. Exclusive Academy services: **translation/decipherment** of recovered Einwechtëran/Schallûsan text (translatable-canon rule locked: everything except sealed lore — see Pillar 4 labeling discipline, §2A); a **research-consumable family** (Lamp Oil the broad consumable, plus branch specialists — Thinking Salts, Scholar's Draught, Boiler Black, etc.) stacking **additively under a fixed total bonus ceiling**, affecting research **speed only, never outcome**; and account-bound, non-tradeable **certifications/licenses**. The five-tier Lamp Oil ladder (Dregs → Deepwick → Blackseep → Longlight → Glasswick) runs on three axes (Brightness/Burn/Stability); Glasswick is deliberately supply-constrained to salvaged pre-Suture relic oil, tying the top of the research economy to exploration rather than wealth. **Guttering never destroys research progress — only the remaining burn is lost.**

**🔒 LOCKED — livery and Crown Marks:** account-bound cosmetic/identity goods (decals, weapon/vehicle/hull skins, Cogworks room decor, emotes), never listable, split into an **earned lane** (achievement/event/rank, never purchasable) and a **purchased lane** (Crown Marks only, never earnable) that never cross. **"Paint the Town Red"** — a full red Strider livery for Alpha/Beta participants — is **locked never to be reissued in any form.** Reissue policy for other retired ("Struck") livery: minimum two-year embargo, and a reissue ("Pattern") must never share the original's exact name, wear history, or signature motion/light/sound detail.

**🔒 LOCKED — anti-bot/anti-RMT stack (defense in depth, no single layer load-bearing):** no production minting, NPC-mediated/logged transfers only, **anonymous listings** (buyer cannot choose a seller — the highest-value single defense, since it breaks the delivery half of any real-money trade), price bands around computed reference value, listing fees + burned sale tax, and capped/logged directed orders. **A daily account-activity gate was considered and explicitly dropped for Alpha** — it penalizes legitimate players with irregular schedules more than it deters a determined farmer; revisit only if bot activity is actually observed in telemetry.

**Alpha scope classification:** the Iron Mark wallet/ledger, NPC-mediated transfer, the Hold-layer Depot, core anti-RMT listing mechanics, and the Academy Depot with fixed prices are **Alpha-critical**. Crown Marks wallet/livery entitlement and the Regional Board are Alpha-supporting. The Docked Exchange, Order Book, translation service, maker's plates, and the full research-consumable family are **Post-Alpha**, gated on RFA netcode and the crafting system respectively. **Per the V41 directive (§36), this economy work is now authorized to proceed alongside the Drifter/Meridian/RFA/Iron Frame track rather than waiting on it** — the Post-Alpha items here are still gated by real dependencies (RFA netcode, a working crafting system), not by a scope hold.

**Decision log:** all nine open decisions from the Economy spec's V1.0 and all six from the Charters spec's V1.0 were closed by founder ruling Aug 27, 2026 (full tables in the source documents). **Nothing in either document is awaiting founder approval** — remaining blockers are economy math (the earn-ceiling computation against a real build) and RFA netcode load-test data, both engineering/validation work, not design decisions.

## 19A. WEATHER SYSTEM V1.0 (Founder Lock, July 2026)

**Status:** Specced ✓ | Coded ✓ (UE5.8 C++, 15 checks passing) | **COMPILED AND LINKED on Shadow PC ✓ (29 Aug 2026)** — the "pending compile verification" flag carried since V35 is resolved. `UnrealEditor-CogheimWeather.dll` rebuilt clean in `BuildLog_20260829_135154.txt`.

**⚠ One defect found and fixed during that verification (29 Aug 2026):** this module ships a **flat layout** — all seven headers sit in the module root with no `Public/` folder. UBT only auto-exports `<Module>/Public`, so this module was exporting **nothing**. It compiled fine alone (its `.cpp` files sit beside its headers) and no dependent module had ever existed to expose the gap. `CogheimWeatherVFX` (§19B) was the first, and failed with `Cannot open include file: 'WeatherTypes.h'` across five translation units. Fixed by adding `PublicIncludePaths.Add(ModuleDirectory)` to `CogheimWeather.Build.cs` — a focused change that moves no files and touches nothing already compiling. **Note for future modules:** `CogheimSRF` is also flat and will hit the identical failure the first time anything depends on it.

**Context:** Climate zone architecture and the Weather Achievement System (30 achievements, 6 weather conditions, 42 tests — built earlier, see §35) both already existed and referenced weather, but no actual weather *engine* had ever been written. This session pulled the locked design decisions forward and built the real simulation layer that drives them.

**Zone architecture (pre-existing lock, confirmed and formalized):**
- **24 vertical disc zones matching Earth's timezone bands** — each zone anchored to a real-world weather station
- **Weather station-to-zone mapping is proprietary and never shown in-game**
- Regional weather (23 standard zones): a **daily forecast summary** (not a midnight snapshot — a summary avoids the "clear at midnight, storm at noon" misleading-edge-case problem), updates once per day at **UTC midnight**
- **Iron Veil zone updates hourly** — deliberately more volatile, since the Veil is the strategic centerpiece and tactical weather variance matters there in a way it doesn't for standard zones

**Data authority:** Server-authoritative. On data failure, the system **holds last-known state — it never fabricates synthetic weather.**

**Four confirmed consumer systems (weather feeds these, weather doesn't compute their effects):**
1. Iron Veil defense buff
2. Sprawl fuel penalty
3. Battle View storm states
4. Weather Achievement System (pre-existing, 30 achievements / 42 tests)

**Architecture (follows the Iron Frame separation-of-concerns pattern, §13B):** Weather queries zone geography via an interface — it never re-implements zone logic. Station data is injected via a separate provider interface. **Intensity is the single normalized 0–1 value** every downstream consumer keys off of for mechanical scaling — no consumer reads raw weather-condition enums directly.

**Build package:** `WeatherTypes.h` (condition enum + state struct), `ZoneResolverInterface.h`, `WeatherStationProviderInterface.h`, `WeatherSubsystem.h/.cpp` (UGameInstanceSubsystem, two independent timers — daily + hourly), `WeatherTestDoubles.h` (mock resolver/provider for testing), `WeatherSystemSpec.cpp` (UE Automation Spec, 15 checks across 6 describe blocks), Build.cs, README. Delivered as flat-root zip.

**Validation:** Python logic mirror confirmed all 15 checks (zone resolution, daily tick behavior, Veil hourly-tick independence, failure handling, query-interface safety, consumer read consistency) before the C++ was trusted.

**Explicitly deferred — separate future sessions:** Individual consumer hook implementations (Veil buff curve, Sprawl fuel penalty formula, Battle View storm VFX) are each their own dedicated session, not bundled into the weather engine itself. Weather Achievement System C++ port is queued separately (§35).

---

## 19B. WEATHER VFX SYSTEM V1.0 (Built 29 August 2026)

**Status:** Specced ✓ | Coded ✓ (UE5.8 C++, 18 checks) | **COMPILES AND LINKS on Shadow PC ✓** | ⬜ Not loaded in-editor | ⬜ No PIE run | ⬜ Zero Niagara assets authored

**Alpha classification:** **Alpha-supporting.** Environmental presentation, not on the Drifter / Meridian Maritime Academy / RFA Netcode / Iron Frame critical path. It exists because §19A's engine had no way to be *seen*.

**Why a separate module from `CogheimWeather`:** dedicated servers and the weather *simulation* layer have no reason to link Niagara. This module owns **zero gameplay-authoritative state** — every `FWeatherState` it renders is a client-replicated copy. It consumes weather only through `AWeatherReplicationActor`'s replicated property and never touches `WeatherSubsystem`, `WeatherStationProviderInterface`, or `ZoneResolverInterface` directly.

**Build package (11 files):**
- `WeatherVFXProfile.h` — `UDataAsset` holding a `TMap<EWeatherCondition, FWeatherVFXConditionEntry>` (one Niagara System slot per condition) plus fog/blend/wind tuning floats
- `WeatherVFXFunctionLibrary.h/.cpp` — pure static functions: intensity blending, per-condition fog density, change detection, wind scaling. All 18 spec checks target this layer
- `WeatherReplicationActor.h/.cpp` — `AInfo`, `bAlwaysRelevant`, polls the subsystem server-side and replicates `FWeatherState` to clients
- `WeatherVFXComponent.h/.cpp` — client-only; blends intensity and drives Niagara / height fog / wind
- `Tests/WeatherVFXSpec.cpp` — 18 checks, 3 describe blocks, 1:1 port of the validated Python mirror
- `CogheimWeatherVFXModule.cpp` (`IMPLEMENT_MODULE` present — the recurring bug class from CogheimNetcode / SRF+Weather / CogheimDialogueUI did **not** recur here), `Build.cs`, `README.md`

**Validation:** Python logic mirror ran 18/18 before any C++ was trusted, per house practice.

**⚠ Defect found and fixed at compile verification — engine API misuse.** The component originally drove wind with a direct property write:
```cpp
TargetWindComponent->Speed = ...;   // compiles clean, does nothing
```
`Speed` is a public `UPROPERTY`, so this would have **built without error** and silently never worked. Verified against the actual engine header on Shadow PC (`UE_5.8/Engine/Source/Runtime/Engine/Classes/Components/WindDirectionalSourceComponent.h`), which states that the data used to query wind lives on the render thread in an `FWindSourceSceneProxy`, making those properties effectively read-only; mutation must go through the `Set*` functions that queue the render-thread update. **Corrected to `SetSpeed()`.** This would have presented as a content or Niagara bug, not a code bug. `UExponentialHeightFogComponent::SetFogDensity` was verified correct against the same install.

**Open — requires validation or founder input:**
1. **`MonitoredZoneId` on `AWeatherReplicationActor` defaults to 0** and is a placeholder. Which of the 24 zones the Alpha's locked region (Pale Reach) actually sits in has never been established in any session. Same blocker gates the wetlands/alpine vegetation art (`A06`/`A07`) and the `W06` puddle mask priority.
2. **Zero Niagara Systems exist.** Every `ConditionVFX` slot is empty; the module is plumbing with nothing to present. Source textures are delivered (see below) but no `.uasset` has been authored.

**Companion art — delivered, processed, NOT yet imported:**
Seven source textures (`W01`–`W07`) generated against `COGHEIM_WeatherFX_Prompt_Sheet_V1`, validated, and processed 29 Aug 2026: alpha extracted from luminance, `W03` (snow) repacked to a **4×3** SubUV grid and `W05` (storm debris) to **3×3**, colour bias flattened on the neutral effects only. Raw set at `ArtFiles\WeatherFX_Raw\`, processed set at `ArtFiles\WeatherFX_Processed\` with a README carrying the SubImage Size values. `W06` is a greyscale placement mask driving runtime wetness via material parameter — **not** a particle texture, and deliberately not a baked-wet variant of every ground texture (third application of the parameter-driven principle locked in the Cogworks Surfacing Spec).

**Next concrete action:** launch the editor, confirm all 16 modules initialise, then author one Niagara System (Rain) against `W01`+`W02` and wire it into a `UWeatherVFXProfile` — the smallest thing that proves the whole chain end to end.
