## 21. EINWECHTËRAN LANGUAGE & GLYPHS

> ### 🔓 V46 AMENDMENT — THE LANGUAGE IS UNSEALED. FOUNDER RULING, 31 AUGUST 2026.
>
> **The Einwechtëran language is unsealed for the website, for the game, and for the Academy as a learnable player ability. Book V's reveal stays sealed.**
>
> **The line, precisely — everything downstream depends on where it sits:**
>
> | Unsealed — build it, teach it, publish it | Still sealed — Book V's reveal |
> |---|---|
> | The **script and glyph set** — form, stroke order, how it is written | **What Einwechtëran magic actually is** |
> | **Grammar and vocabulary**, enough to be genuinely learnable | **Where it came from** — the Book IV/V origin reveal |
> | **Translation and decipherment** as an Academy service and player skill | **Who the Einwechtëran were**, in the terms Book V uses |
> | **Recovered inscriptions** on ruins, relics, monuments, Strider fabric | The **5th glyph identity** (see below) |
> | The four Maxiom wheel glyphs — **OHR / VELK / SCHAE / GAIM** | The **Hollow Crown Stage 5 glyph** identity |
> | The language's **link to Harmonics as a mechanic** | The **metaphysical explanation** of why that link exists |
>
> **The organizing principle: players learn to READ it, not to understand what it IS.** A player can decipher an inscription, speak a phrase, and use it in Harmonics without the game ever explaining the thing Book V exists to reveal. That is Pillar 4 doing its job — *the novels are the truth, the game is the archaeology.* You can excavate, catalogue and read an artefact for years without knowing what made it.
>
> **🔴 This amends the 29 Aug monument ruling (V44 ruling 2), and the amendment is recorded rather than applied silently.** That ruling was **SHIP UNNAMED** — build the monuments as unexplained pre-Suture relics with no stated origin, because a visibly Einwechtëran network in a shipped Alpha *is* Book V's reveal delivered ahead of the book. Under this decision monuments may now carry **readable inscriptions** that players can translate — but they still carry **no origin, no network purpose, and no makers' identity.** The structures remain unexplained; they are now unexplained **and legible** rather than unexplained and mute. **Arguably a stronger position than the original ruling** — a player who can read a monument and still not know what it is for is in a more interesting place than one who cannot read it at all.
>
> **⚠ Open, requires founder confirmation:** whether any specific inscription may state or imply the network's **purpose**. **Recommendation: no.** Inscriptions should be names, warnings, dedications, measurements and fragments — never explanations.
>
> **⚠ What this creates, and it is not small.** An unsealed language that players actually learn is a **content commitment**: it must be internally consistent, carry enough surface to reward study, and every inscription authored anywhere in the world must be correct in it. **This is a linguistics workload, not a lore paragraph.** A small, rigorous, genuinely learnable core beats a large loose vocabulary that contradicts itself by the third region. Scope it deliberately — see `NAR-10` and `NAR-11` in §0.
>
> **Unblocked by this ruling:** the public language page on cogheim.com · Academy translation and decipherment, which §17A already founder-locked as a knowledge-goods monopoly and which now has real subject matter instead of a placeholder · Einwechtëran as a learnable ability with a progression path · and the **Harmonics grammar**, which can now be designed against a real language rather than an abstraction (§26D).

**Status:** Language **UNSEALED** 31 Aug 2026 ✓ · two specific glyph identities remain sealed ✓

**Maxiom wheel glyphs (locked):** OHR / VELK / SCHAE / GAIM
- SCHAE: first appeared outside Maxiom wheel — scratched in Grindwalker flywheel chamber wall
- VELK: encoded by Mordechai Stridefall; name on every Colossus chest core

**Lyra Cross & Gabriel Cross:**
- Siblings (Lyra older, Gabriel younger); both on same Strider
- Gabriel unaware of Lyra's true nature
- Novel placement: Book III; scene: Gabriel acts recklessly to protect Lyra, she watches like she already knew

**5th glyph:** Scratched into original Statue of Liberty copper in Blackjack Engine Core — **STILL SEALED** (glyph identity pending founder lock; cascades into multiple systems).

**Why this stays sealed while the language does not, and it is not a contradiction.** The 5th glyph and the Hollow Crown Stage 5 glyph are *specific glyphs at specific narrative moments*, not language infrastructure. **A player can read fluent Einwechtëran and still find a glyph they have never seen, in a place that should not have one** — which is a better reveal in an unsealed language than in a sealed one, because the player now has the competence to know it is strange.

## 23A. SYSTEM-BEARING NPC PLACEMENTS — V14 LOCKS

Both systems passed the Rival Test (§2A) at proposal. Spec-before-code applies — these are placement locks with system summaries; full specs are next-session work.

### STRIDER RECOVERY OPERATIONS — Captain Armando Beugois

**Rival Test:** (1) No MMO makes recovery of a city a cooperative/competitive event — ships respawn everywhere else. (2) Only works because 200–300 people live on the thing lying broken on a mountainside. (3) Built as a cross-Syndicate convergence event.

**System summary:**
- A crippled Strider (flywheel dead, hull breached, immobile — NOT destroyed) does not despawn or auto-repair
- It becomes a live recovery site on real terrain: a 48–72 hour open-world event
- **Recovery Contracts** issued by Armando's Wreck Recovery Corps (Iron Veil)
- Rival Syndicates race to salvage / owning Syndicate mounts rescue-and-tow operations / neutral players hire on as recovery crew for Ironmarks
- 200–600 players converging on a single wreck
- Thematic anchor: recovery-as-strength — the founder narrative made mechanical
- Adjacency: Armando's crew know wrecks better than anyone — natural narrative proximity to Colossus wreckage breadcrumbs, never touching sealed Oasis content

### THE SKYBRIDGE — Morgann

**Rival Test:** (1) No MMO has player-defendable, player-raidable scheduled transit — every rival's fast travel is a menu. (2) The Skybridge exists because the cities move and the map has no fixed roads. (3) Escort wings + raid wings + passengers on one flight = a flying social space.

**System summary:**
- The only scheduled NPC-flown air transport on the disc: heavy aeronaut freighters on fixed routes between the 3–5 Black-Maw Stations
- Unaffiliated players ride free (extends Joel's Green Fund logic to mobility); Syndicate players pay Ironmarks
- Routes flown in real time through real weather across contested zones
- **Escort contracts** = paid gameplay for pilot players; **air piracy** = an actual profession
- Morgann personally flies the flagship route and remembers every player who has flown escort for her (SME Personal Thread integration)
- Clean separation from Gary McCloud: he is Grindwalker class-locked combat air; Morgann is class-neutral civil aviation

## 23B. PROFESSOR EVAN HOUSE (Founder canon, 1 September 2026)

**Source:** `claude/COGHEIM_Character_ProfessorEvanHouse_V1_Sep1_2026.md`. **Physical description and role are founder canon.** Everything else in that document is labelled proposal.

**Role:** the principal quest giver for learning Harmonics. Players learn the discipline through his questline.

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| Name | **Professor Evan House** |
| Height / build | **6 ft 3 in**, tall and lanky |
| Hair | Buzz cut |
| Eyewear | Scholar's steampunk spectacles, hinged loupe at the temple |
| Age | Mid thirties to early forties |
| Complexion | **Acne, active and scarred** |
| Presentation | White male, visibly and unmistakably nerdy |
| Carried | A **mandolin**, worn on the back. He plays, and he likes to entertain people |

**SEALED handling: House is not sealed. What he teaches is.** He may appear in art and player-facing material; nothing attached to him states the mechanism, the origin, or the word Einwechtëran before Book V.

**The mandolin is the character detail worth protecting.** The world was struck and the note is still sounding; the man who teaches you to listen carries something that makes a note on purpose. **He never explains it and the game never explains it.** Do not have him say it out loud.

**The acne is a production instruction, not a description.** Character creation locks a library of default blemishes and scars as free base options, deliberately decoupled from any purchasable cosmetic. **House is the NPC who demonstrates that the library exists and is normal.** Written down here so nobody prettifies him in a polish pass. **Generators default to attractive and will remove it unless told twice** — `handsome` and `clear skin` are in the negative prompt for that reason. A pass that comes back with clean skin has failed the brief, not softened it.

**Two production flags:** 6 ft 3 in needs either a scaled rig or a check that the shared humanoid rig carries the proportions without breaking sockets and IK; and **carrying the mandolin is nearly free while performing with it is an authored animation-and-audio asset.** Decide which at Alpha.

**Open, needs ruling (§37):** which school he belongs to (recommendation: an Assayer by institution with a Choir sympathy he will not admit to); **name clearance** — "Professor House" carries an unavoidable television echo and deserves a cheap search before public use; and whether he can appear as a travelling scholar before the Academy exists.

**Ruled by recommendation and recorded:** he is **confirmed game canon, not novel canon** — the Academy has zero references across five books. And **he does not know what Harmonics is.** He teaches the practice at the highest level available to anyone alive and cannot tell you what it is. **He should sound like Christopher, not like a wizard.** That is the whole player-facing register for the discipline.

**Track B**, quest and encounter characters. Portrait card layout is locked: logo top, portrait centre, name and title bottom.

## 24. QUESTLINE ARCHITECTURE V1.0 & THE KAEL SEQUENCE

**Status:** Locked + Acts 0–4 fully specced ✓ | Named NPC Questline Spec V1.1 complete ✓

**Source docs:** `COGHEIM_QUESTLINE_ARCHITECTURE_V1.md` / `COGHEIM_NPC_QUESTLINE_SPEC_V1.md`

**Design bar (reference games):** Valheim (discovery-driven, no quest markers) / New World (faction tension, NPC history) / Fallout 76 (environmental storytelling, player arrives after the story) / Crimson Tides (ship identity, crew loyalty)

**COGHEIM's edge:** The Strider is home. Every questline starts at, returns to, or permanently changes something on the player's Strider or the world's Striders. Quests are chapters, not errands.

**Five locked design principles:**
1. No quest markers for the most important discoveries
2. NPCs have schedules, not spawn points
3. MAXIOM is the quest log — but it lies sometimes (knows what the player knows, not what's true)
4. Rewards are rarely items — rewards are access, relationship, and understanding
5. The novels are the truth. The game is the archaeology.

**Three-tier structure:**
- **Tier 1 — The Main Path:** "The Kael Sequence" — five-act spine following the novel throughline; player is Kael Einwechter
- **Tier 2 — NPC Questlines:** Loyalty chains (Christine, Lyra, Joey, Sara, Tiffany, Rita + intertwined Christine/Jess/Candy/Joel four-chain)
- **Tier 3 — Side Quests:** Procedural + authored; ~5 quests per act density (locked standard)

**Act 1 spine (locked):**
| Quest | Teaches |
|---|---|
| 1.1 | Syndicate UI, Crew Standing, Probation clock |
| 1.2 "First Duty" | Procedural First Duty Generator, class core loop |
| 1.3 | Christine Chain intro (SME-aware) |
| 1.4 | Crew Board, player-generated quest content |
| 1.5 "The Schallûsan Fragment" | Family name glyph artifact; public mythology visibility |
| 1.6 "Pale Reach Dispatch" | Rita Marie's 152-year-old "Found." signal; Act 2 hook |

**Acts 2–4:** Fully specced at 5 quests per act, matching Act 2 density lock. EntityAttentionThread pays off exactly once in **Act 3 Quest 3.4 ("Rade")**, configurable threshold 2-of-3 signals.

**Drifter tutorial roster:** Uses existing named canon NPCs (not invented placeholders); NPCs are placed correctly where they are in the world — not younger versions or alternate timelines.

**Founder-held (NOT specced):** Act 5 "The Full Account" content / Lyra Cross chain resolution / Oasis interior mechanics / Easter Eggs 10–11 full resolution / Extranos content (100% Founder Seal, zero specced).

**COGHEIM Quest System V1.0 — FULLY CODED (July 2026): 605 tests total, zero failures, all four categories complete.** ⚠ Corrects prior versions, which showed only Category 1 (229 tests) as done and Categories 2–4 as "queued" — they were actually built in the same session.

**Category 1 — Side Quest Catalog (229 tests):**
| Module | Purpose | Tests |
|---|---|---|
| SideQuestCatalog.cs | Discovery state, Iron Veleth gating, expiry, player records | 68 |
| ColossusWarStoriesChains.cs | 26 authored quests across three witness chains | 30 |
| ZoneDiscoverySystem.cs | 8 disc zones, trigger evaluation, density QA validator | 53 |
| StriderWorldQuestGenerator.cs | Crew requests (3-day cycle), Strider history, inter-Strider, recovery reconstruction | 45 |
| TextureQuestRegistry.cs | 12 archetypes, coverage validator, singleton enforcement (The Reunion) | 33 |

**Category 2 — Strider-Specific NPC Quest System:** `StriderNPCGenerator` — deterministic per-Strider invented-NPC generation using FNV-1a hashing (same Strider always generates the same non-canon-tier NPCs, no randomness drift on reload) + `StriderNPCQuestFamilies` — 8 archetype templates. Populates every boarded Strider with people, not furniture, without touching the 33 locked named-NPC roster.

**Category 3 — Crafting Quest System:** `CraftingRecipeRegistry` — exactly **600 recipes** (5 material categories × 5 tiers × 24 recipes per tier = 120 per category). Categories: Ironwork, Vitals, Fuel, Timber, Aetheric. 8 crafting families per tier (Weapon, Armor, Component, Consumable, Architecture, Vehicle, Tool, Inscription). `CraftingDiscoveryArcs` implements **the Weston Method** — a 3-stage discovery gate (find notes → test batch → correct failure) applied across 5 categories × 5 tiers = 25 discovery arcs. Recipes unlock by *tier*, not individually — completing a tier's discovery arc unlocks all recipes in that tier. Cross-references Iron Veleth trees directly (e.g. a Juggernaut player's Hull Plating recipe draws from both the Iron Pillar and Aetheric Engineering disciplines simultaneously). **This 600-recipe crafting system is distinct from and additional to the 634-quest crafting-unlock ledger in §13C** — the ledger tracks quest count, this registry tracks the actual recipes those quests unlock.

**Category 4 — World/Daily Quest System:** `DailyWeeklyQuestScheduler` — timezone-safe UTC reset boundaries (a real date-math bug was caught here: an initial assumption that 5-day gaps always stay within one weekly cycle was wrong regardless of starting day; fixed and covered with both positive and negative test cases). `FactionReputationSystem` — 5-tier permanent standing.

**Three design innovations locked in code:**
1. **Permanently missable quests** — e.g. Soot-Bridge's Reassignment expires if the server's world moves on; The Reunion fires once per server. Not content gaps — features.
2. **Iron Veleth visibility tiers** — the same physical location (e.g. Sunderveil Corridor) yields different content to a player with Colossus Research T3 vs. none. The world exists; the player earns the eyes to see it.
3. **Coverage validation as QA tooling** — ZoneDensityValidator flags any zone below 80% of target quest count before it ships; TextureQuestCoverageValidator requires all 12 texture archetypes covered before any build passes content check.

**Delivered as:** `COGHEIM_QUESTS_V1.zip` — all four categories, C# + NUnit, Python-verified. ⚙️ Pending UE5.8 C++ port (all four categories, not just Category 1).

## 24A. KRIS BRICKMAN — BIOGRAPHY & QUEST CHAIN (Founder Lock, July 17, 2026)

**Status:** Biography locked ✓ | Quest chain skeleton locked ✓ | Estimated 30–40% of a codeable spec — full objective/dialogue/reward authoring still needed

**Biography (locked):** Master Mechanic stationed at The Garage. Tagline: *"I don't just shape metal, I forge legacies."* Born human; underwent deliberate military android conversion during the Colossus Rise War (triggered when the first Colossus rose from the ground and more followed); served four years. Reads mid-30s permanently; retains his human soul. Recovering Grindspirit user, currently dry, treats sobriety as ongoing discipline. Thrill-seeker beneath a quiet exterior; collects pre-Suture-Day horror films, shared selectively as a trust signal. His conversion tech is **not** GAIA-9 lineage — the Colossi he fought were GAIA-9 units, making him and Armando Beugois (GAIA-9) tech-distinct despite both being androids.
**Flagged lore thread (needs its own future session):** the Kris/Colossus/GAIA-9 tech relationship.

**Three-tier quest chain (skeleton locked):**
- **Tier 1 — 7 baseline quests:** first meeting through full conversion reveal; objectives alternate between Garage beats and outside salvage/exploration runs
- **Tier 2 — 9 vehicle-unlock quests:** one per platform (Dust-Skimmer, Gut-Ripper, Iron-Jaw, Slipstreamer, Sky-Sweeper, Iron-Hail, Tide-Runner, The Waterhammer, Iron-Tide), each Garage briefing → outside objective → return-and-build; some objectives are parts salvage, others are horror-film reels traded for access. The Iron Frame has its own separate single quest, one lineage path among several.
- **Tier 3 — Thronewalker capstone:** 6-stage endgame track, ~7–9 hours at the founder's playstyle, additional to the 7+9 chain. Gated by four interlocking differentiator systems: SME-driven personalized dialogue (unique final unlock conversation per player, keyed to which films were shared and what the player did), build-history-encoded cosmetics (the player's own Colossus-wreckage kills bake into the Thronewalker's visual state), a Syndicate-witnessed public unlock ceremony (not solo), and a 9-reel horror-film stash gate (distinct locations from the vehicle chain, fixed narrative unlock order).

**Placeholder numbers:** 5 Colossus-wreckage salvage hauls for Stage 3 (Thronewalker). The 9 film-reel locations are unresolved pending a dedicated Strider-proximity/travel mechanics session — reachability depends on where the player's Syndicate Strider is positioned in the world, a mechanic not yet specced.

This session's tiered structure (7 + 9 + Thronewalker capstone) implies a higher count. ## 25. SYNDICATE MEMORY ENGINE (SME)

**Status:** Fully coded ✓ — **218 passing tests confirmed** (COGHEIM SME V1, July 2026 session)

Unified system built from three approved multiplayer innovations: **shared NPC memory**, **server-wide quest evolution**, and **player-authored consequence**.

**Five modules (built in dependency order):**
| Module | Function |
|---|---|
| Strider Ledger | Server-wide shared event state per Strider |
| Personal Thread | Per-player, per-NPC trust/relationship state |
| Reroute Resolver | Checks Ledger before loading a Personal Thread quest node |
| Witness Mark Ledger | Player-to-player discoverable consequence |
| First Duty Generator (FDG) | Procedural quest text from live Syndicate state |

**FDG priority tiers (highest → lowest):** SuccessionWindow → RecentAttack → OfficerRequest → ResearchBottleneck → ResourceDeficit. Reads live Syndicate state, binds real variable data into templates, delivered diegetically by NPC — no popups. Rules-based, no ML dependency, deterministic unit tests per tier.

**Content wired through SME:** The four intertwined NPC chains (Christine, Jess, Candy, Joel) built simultaneously with SME integration.

## 26. IRON VELETH SYSTEM

**Status:** Named + Fully Architected + Locked ✓ (July 2026)

**Etymology:** Iron = the world's bones. Veleth = the receiving end of the question. (Formerly "The Veleth System" — renamed "Iron Veleth System," permanent.)

**Core innovation: Skill = Visibility.** Higher skill doesn't just increase numbers — it makes more of the world legible to the player.

**Structure:**
| Component | Nodes |
|---|---|
| 5 Pillar Trees (one per Strider class) | 1,440 |
| Universal Academy (8 disciplines) | 240 |
| Applied Synthesis capstone | 20 |
| **TOTAL** | **1,700 nodes** |

**Pillar Tree assignments:**
| Pillar | Class |
|---|---|
| Iron | Juggernaut |
| Wind | Grindwalker |
| Current | Vector |
| Ash | Scavenger |
| Crossing | Blackjack |

**Every node has 3 outputs:**
1. **Capability unlock** — mechanical function
2. **World Response** — how NPCs and the world treat the player differently
3. **Quest Visibility** — quests that were invisible now become findable

**Applied Synthesis:** Unlocks at Academy Tier 3 in 4+ disciplines — cross-discipline mastery capstone. 20 nodes × 8 quests each = 160 quests.

**Quest generation from Iron Veleth:** ~5,760 authored/template quests at launch (2,160 authored unlock + 3,600+ template).

**Connection:** Feeds the Aetheric Academy (Candy Mind-Stone, Director) — the research tree's visual branching structure IS the Oasis symbol (Hint 6).

## 26A. CHARACTER PROGRESSION FRAMEWORK (Founder Lock, July 2026)

**Status:** Model locked ✓ — Alpha scope defined, full system deliberately deferred

**Model selected: Model C — Strider-Heritage-Tied Role Specialization**, chosen over two rejected alternatives:
- Traditional level-plus-class tree — **rejected**, fails the Rival Test (§2A), too close to existing MMO convention
- Skill-based horizontal advancement — **rejected**, too legible-UI-hostile for the locked MAXIOM diegetic-only interface (§16)

**Alpha scope (deliberately minimal — no player-facing class system):**
- Gear + a hidden Combat Rating + Grindwalker heritage flavor only
- **No XP levels, no skill points, no class picker**
- MAXIOM displays health scan-pulse readout and equipped gear only — nothing more

**Design rationale:** Class-as-system is deferred until a second Strider class enters production — heritage comparisons are only testable once there's more than one heritage to compare against.

**Two systems locked as deliberately separate, both deferred beyond Alpha:**
1. **Personal progression (future):** a portable Role system — combat / engineering / logistics flavors, follows the player
2. **Iron Veleth Pillar Trees (§26):** ship-owned Strider-tech infrastructure that persists with the Strider through Mutiny/Succession events (§12) — belongs to the vessel, not the pilot

**Explicitly out of scope for Alpha, confirmed Post-Alpha:** Full 1,700-node Iron Veleth tree, multi-class ability kits, the Role list definition (scoped to its own future dedicated session), any XP/leveling curve.

## 26B. SYNDICATE RESEARCH SYSTEM — "THE FOUNDRY LEDGER" (Founder Lock, July 14, 2026)

**Status:** Fully specced ✓ | **UE5.8 C++ port COMPLETE ✓ (July 22, 2026)** — corrects the prior "port not started" status; `COGHEIM_SyndicateResearch_AlphaBuild_V2.zip`, 11 C++ files, 22 Automation Spec checks across 7 describe blocks. Built on Opus 4.8 per the multi-system-interdependent routing rule. **All code uncompiled pending Shadow PC build.**

**⚠ Found during full-history audit — this entire system was missing from all prior Index versions.**

**Core design constraint:** Team-driven research that runs *parallel* to personal Iron Veleth progression (§26), aboard the player's home Strider via the Aetheric Academy — **no travel required for either track.**

**Research output spans three types:** Strider-specific equipment/upgrades, Syndicate-wide perks, ability unlocks. Contributions require **both** resource donations and task completion — resources alone don't advance research. Categories follow a hybrid model: 4 universal categories + 1–2 class-exclusive bonus lines per Strider class.

**The Foundry Ledger dual-track mechanic (the actual named system):**
| Track | Behavior |
|---|---|
| **Command Line** | Leadership-queued, singular, immediate — the Council directs one priority project |
| **Crew Initiative** | Crew-posted, Momentum-gated, auto-igniting, capped at **3 parallel slots** |

**15% Shared Momentum overflow pool** connects the two tracks — contributions that would otherwise be wasted (e.g. Crew Initiative already at its 3-slot cap) spill into the other track instead, so player effort is never lost regardless of where it's contributed. This mechanic emerged from the founder describing a desired behavior he couldn't yet name; Claude formalized and named it during the session.

**Slot-cap bug caught and fixed during build:** an early version let projects bypass the 3-slot Crew Initiative cap via a momentum auto-flip edge case. Fixed by moving ignition control exclusively to the Ledger through an explicit `Ignite()` gate — no project can self-start outside that single choke point.

**Five Academy Research Officers, one per Strider class (locked, named):**
| Officer | Class |
|---|---|
| Director Adelaide Iron-Vow | Blackjack |
| Foreman Bruno Steel-Tally | Juggernaut |
| Chief Wren Salvage-Wit | Scavenger |
| Archivist Suri Calm-Line | Vector |
| Chief Deke Fast-Reckon | Grindwalker |

**Christine Chronos explicitly excluded** from this roster — she remains singular to her own sealed-lore location (Iron Veil) and is not a Research Officer.

**C++ port fixes (July 22 — both were ship-blocking, both now resolved):**
- Two live bugs caught in the C# prototype during the port: a dead `ReconcileIgnitions()` call that nothing ever triggered (a capped project would wait forever even after a slot freed), and an overflow routing flaw where Command Line contributions spilled as Momentum onto already-ignited Crew Initiatives — dead weight, not real progress
- ✅ **Open Item #2 resolved** — pre-ignition donations now bank toward both Momentum and real project cost simultaneously; front-loaded crews get an instant-complete payoff
- ✅ **Open Item #3 resolved (caller identity, the ship-blocker)** — split into two components: `UCogheimSyndicateResearchComponent` on the Strider governance actor (no client-callable RPCs) plus a new per-player `UCogheimSyndicateResearchRequestComponent` on `APlayerState` that resolves real identity through the owning connection
- **Four items still open, non-blocking:** overflow-as-progress founder lock, research node catalog, balance pass, Iron Veil acceleration curve

**Deliverables:** `COGHEIM_Syndicate_Research_System_V1.md` spec, `COGHEIM_SyndicateResearch_AlphaBuild_V2.zip` (C++, current), C# prototype marked implementation-stale.
→ [Syndicate and strider collaborative research](https://claude.ai/chat/dc950c16-ef96-4e5b-b9e1-cc9b5a3a0ea1)

## 26C. CHARACTER CREATION SYSTEM V1 (Design Specification, August 29, 2026)

**Status:** Design proposal; founder direction locked on items marked below. **C++ delivered but not compiled or tested in the live Cogheim project** — the authoring session was not linked to the project. Source: `COGHEIM_CharacterCreation_Spec_V1.md`. Alpha-critical for the base material's blank tattoo layer only (see below); the rest of the creator UI can ship on its own timeline.

**Player fantasy:** "You are not picking a costume off a rack. You are meeting yourself for the first time in the world of Cogheim."

**Rival Test:** FFXIV/WoW/ESO/New World/Albion/EVE all use preset-driven creators — pick a race/body preset, push sliders inside it. None of them decouple gender presentation from face/body sliders, and none offer photo-seeded starting likeness as a core creation step. Cogheim's structural advantage: full independence of gender presentation, skin tone, and facial structure (no bundled presets), plus default blemish/scar options that treat the body as having a history. Advantage type: primarily immersion and accessibility, with persistence as a secondary benefit via the tattoo-parlor hook.

**🔒 LOCKED, direction confirmed by Michael — gender presentation and sliders.** Three gender-presentation options (masculine/feminine/neutral) select only a starting body-proportion curve and voice-line bank; every face, body, and skin-tone slider stays fully independent and reachable regardless of which is chosen.

**🔒 LOCKED, direction confirmed — skin tone and facial structure.** Skin tone (color + undertone) and facial-structure sliders (jaw, nose, cheekbones, eyes, lips, ears, hair texture) are each independent controls, never bundled into a race/ethnicity preset, never gated by skin tone. Deliberate design choice, not only technical: avoids reducing identity to a dropdown.

**🔒 LOCKED, direction confirmed — blemishes, scars, and the tattoo layer.** Blemishes/scars are ordinary creator options (no story attached). **Tattoos are deliberately excluded from the creator** — every character is issued a blank, fully transparent tattoo render-target layer at creation, filled only in-world at a tattoo-parlor NPC aboard a Strider (first discoverable during the Drifter walkthrough), matching the diegetic-first pillar. **This is why the base character material must carry the blank layer from day one — the reason the creator itself is Alpha-critical even though the parlor content that fills it can ship later.**

**⚠ Requires validation — the photo-to-slider pipeline (§9 of the source spec).** What is real and documented: Unreal's skeletal-mesh morph-target system and dynamic-material runtime parameters (both used in the delivered C++); facial landmark detection is mature, widely available technology. **What is not a documented, player-facing runtime feature today: MetaHuman Creator and Mesh-to-MetaHuman are development-time authoring tools, not a shipped in-game SDK for live photo capture in a multiplayer session.** Treat any claim that MetaHuman itself does this out of the box as false pending verified official documentation. Recommended path: build an independent runtime layer (landmark detection → slider values → existing morph-target/material-parameter system), so the "wow" moment stays under Cogheim's own control. **Immediate next action, not yet run: a two-week technical spike** answering exactly one question — can a photo produce a believable slider seed on a test rig. Do not cite photo-based character creation as delivered or prototyped in any fundraising material until that spike has run and Michael has seen the result. **Fallback if infeasible:** ship the slider-based creator with manual default values for the Alpha milestone; treat photo seeding as a fast-follow.

**Failure and recovery:** if photo capture fails, times out, has no camera, or the detection service is unreachable, the system falls back silently to default midpoint values — character creation must never be blockable by a third-party service outage.

**C++ delivered (unverified in project):** `CogheimCharacterAppearance.h` (data struct/enums), `CogheimCharacterCustomizationComponent.h/.cpp` (applies slider values to skeletal-mesh morph targets + a dynamic material instance, exposes the tattoo-layer render-target write call). Written against standard `USkinnedMeshComponent`/`UMaterialInstanceDynamic`/`UKismetRenderingLibrary` API, not against any MetaHuman-specific API, so it works whether the underlying mesh ends up custom-parametric or MetaHuman. **Next step once linked to the project:** inspect the existing character base class / Mannequin placeholder (§36 Phase 0) and adapt these classes to compose with or inherit from whatever is already there, rather than replacing it.

**Risks:** technical (photo pipeline unproven; MetaHuman licensing/runtime rig depth needs verification against current Epic docs; morph-target count needs a performance-budget pass); production (touches every system that renders a player body — Iron Frame seating, animation retargeting, the tattoo parlor, NPC dialogue cameras — real scope-creep risk if "wow factor" pulls in deferred ideas like animal-hybrid heads); **moderation, requires professional review** (user-submitted photos as multiplayer character input is a real risk surface — impersonation, inappropriate uploads — needs legal/platform-policy review before any playtester outside the founder, plus a photo-retention data policy); financial — none directly, the system is Zero Pay-to-Win by construction.

**Open decisions requiring Michael's approval:** whether the seed photo is retained after slider derivation or discarded immediately; whether character re-creation later is free, cooldown-gated, or livery-gated; whether applied tattoos are permanent or removable at in-game cost; confirmation of the §9 technical spike before any public/investor claim that photo-based creation is proven.

**Deferred, phase 3, hook only:** a `voiceProfileID` field is reserved on the character record for a baseline NPC-dialogue voice-bank system; the "dialogue spoken back in your own recorded voice" stretch idea is explicitly not started and not specced pending its own dedicated design/risk review (moderation and live-service cost).

## 26D. EINWECHTËRAN RESONANCE — "HARMONICS" (Design proposal, 29 Aug 2026)

> ### 🔓 V46 AMENDMENT — HARMONICS IS FULLY UNPARKED. FOUNDER RULING, 31 AUGUST 2026.
>
> **Harmonics moves to active production — design and build both.** This **overturns the 30 August ruling**, one day old, which parked Harmonics behind a named trigger: *until the Drifter, RFA and Iron Frames are in a state you would show an investor.* **That trigger has not been met.** The overturn is deliberate and is recorded here per §38 std. #12.
>
> **The reason is sound and worth stating:** the language unsealing (§21) and Harmonics are the same body of work. *"Like science but magic"* is one design problem, and splitting it across a parked system and an active one would mean designing the language twice.
>
> **⚠ The cost, recorded once and then not re-argued.** Harmonics was parked for exactly one reason: it was classed **Alpha-supporting conditional on costing the four Alpha priorities zero weeks**, and it could not honestly meet that condition. That has not changed. It now competes for the same weeks as **STR-07** (walk the Drifter under way — the vertical-slice gate), **LVL-18** (the moving Level Instance prototype) and **NET-12** (M2 concurrency and load, the only thing that converts every population figure in this document from a target into evidence).
>
> **Recommended mitigation — requires founder approval: design-heavy, engine-light until the vertical slice closes.** Language design, grammar, Academy progression, Harmonics rules and the website page cost the slice nothing — they are writing and design hours, not engine hours. **Engine implementation is the part that competes.** Holding only that piece until STR-07 is green preserves the founder's original intent at essentially zero cost to the new direction. Tracked as the `SYS-25` / `SYS-26` split in §0. **Scheduled review at the vertical-slice gate:** if Harmonics engine work has displaced slice work by then, that is the signal to re-park it.
>
> **The publish-nothing ruling needs a narrower restatement.** The 30 Aug ruling was *publish nothing* — no anonymous round, no branded brief, no NDA panel — reasoned on the system being genuinely unlike anything shipping, and an NDA not surviving human nature. **That still holds for the Harmonics *mechanics*. It does not extend to the *language*, which the founder has now explicitly directed onto the website.** Recommended line, requires founder approval — **publishable:** the language, its glyphs, its script, and that it is learnable in-game. **Not publishable:** Ring, the audio-degradation design, the grammar's mechanical effects, and the ten open decisions below.
>
> **⚠ One consequence must not be lost in the change.** The whitepaper made a **deaf or hard-of-hearing panelist mandatory**, because Ring is an audio-degradation mechanic. That is an **accessibility-quality requirement, not a secrecy one.** It survived the publish-nothing ruling as an open problem, it survives this one, it still needs a non-public route — and unparking Harmonics makes it **more** urgent, not less.
>
> **The ten §24 Harmonics decisions are now on the critical path rather than parked behind a trigger.** The load-bearing one is unchanged and is sharper now, not softer: **whether Book V's treatment of Einwechtëran magic must be written before the game locks the Harmonics grammar** — because the game is now going to teach the language publicly while the book that explains it is unpublished.

**CANON STATUS: DESIGN PROPOSAL.** The whitepaper's own words: *"Nothing in it is confirmed canon, founder approved, implemented, coded, compiled or tested. No code exists. No prototype exists."* Source: `COGHEIM_Einwechteran_Resonance_Whitepaper_V1.md`, 25 sections, plus a playtest recruitment kit and a publishable consultation brief.

**This section exists because V44 said magic was "recorded, not designed, and nothing assumes it." Both halves are false.** The whitepaper quotes that exact note and answers *"This is that session. This document designs it."*

### The four founder rulings, 29 Aug 2026

| Ruling | Value |
|---|---|
| Physical nature | **Substrate resonance. Not supernatural.** The disc substrate is an engineered medium that answers correctly shaped frequency |
| Power location | **Split tier.** Personal tier is knowledge and perception only, never force. Strider tier is where world shaping happens and it requires a crew |
| Player input method | **All three** — research pass, simulated critique panel, real recruitment kit |
| Publication order | **Design fully, ship unnamed** — matching the monument posture in §3C. The word Einwechtëran and the mechanism never appear in player-facing material before Book V |

### The grammar

**6 Carriers** — Stone, Iron, Water, Air, Filament, Void. **8 Figures** — Sound, Recall, Count, Name (receptive, 1–4); Hold, Part, Draw, Quiet (projective, 5–8). **48 combinations = 24 personal + 24 crew, exactly half solitary and half social by construction.**

**LOCKED in-document:** Figures 1–4 **cannot** write world state — *"Not 'should not'. Cannot."* Figures 5–8 require **three occupied stations minimum**, matching the primary Strider weapon rule and Pillar 3. A station may be crewed by any player aboard, not only Tuners.

**Vocabulary:** Einwechtëran Resonance → **Harmonics**; practitioner → **Tuner**; personal act → **a Listening**; crew act → **a Sounding**; instruction object → **Cadence Plate**; personal instrument → **Tuning Frame**; crew instrument → **Resonance Bench**; personal cost → **Ring**; permanent public cost → **Residue**.

**Ring** is real audio degradation for real minutes, stacking, server-owned, decaying through disconnect. **Residue** is a permanent SME write per Sounding coordinate that never decays, recording **Syndicate and Strider attribution, never account identity and never real-time position.**

### Why this is not a new pillar

It adds **no new progression track** and **no Tuner class**, consistent with the Model C ruling that there is no player-facing class system in Alpha. It attaches to what already exists: personal knowledge to **Iron Veleth §26's Einwechtëran Studies branch** (already authored), team research to the **Foundry Ledger §26B**, consumables to **Scholar's Draught**, already defined in the Trade Depot spec. The whitepaper's claim: *"You are not adding a new pillar. You are finishing one you already started and already coded a branch for."*

**It also resolves the monument attunement mechanic (§3C):** the monuments are Einwechtëran, and attunement is a Listening rather than a proximity trigger.

**Hard exclusions, §11:** no damage, no healing, no teleportation beyond the monument network, no resurrection, summoning, transmutation, illusion or mind control, no combat buffs, no fast travel, no stealth or invisibility. **Zero pay-to-win, §9,** including an explicit refusal of purchasable translation speed.

### Alpha classification

Prototype 1 is **Alpha-supporting, conditional** — *"proceeds only if it costs the four Alpha priorities zero weeks."* Prototype 2 is Post-Alpha. The full 48-combination grammar and the plate economy are Full production. Per-server plate seeding is a Research experiment. **Anything publicly naming Einwechtëran is blocked until Book V.** The whitepaper's own recommendation: *"build nothing until the Drifter, RFA and Iron Frames are in a state you would show an investor."*

### External exposure — requires founder attention

A **publishable consultation brief exists**, written to be posted publicly under the Cogheim / Limitless Game Makers LLC name, and a recruitment kit for a standing panel of 8–12 people under NDA. **Nothing has been posted, sent or run, and no responses exist.** The brief contains a written zero-pay-to-win commitment and a reciprocity promise to respondents. **No NDA text, no compensation terms, no dates and no legal review are present in either document.** Anything published under the company name is a founder decision.

**One caution to carry forward:** the whitepaper's §12 critique panel is **four simulated personas**. Both documents state it must never be represented as player feedback and never appear in a pitch deck or investor document as player validation.

### Open — ten founder decisions in §24

The load-bearing one, flagged in-document as most important: **must Book V's treatment of Einwechtëran magic be written before the game locks the grammar?** *"If Book V says something different from §5, one of them has to move, and it should not be the novel."* Also unresolved and explicitly not guessable: Residue cell grid size, attribution set cardinality, and retention window — *"They must come from a simulation before any implementation begins."*

## 26E. HARMONICS — THE METAPHYSICS LAYER, THE CANON RECONCILIATION, AND RING (1 September 2026)

**Sources:** `claude/COGHEIM_Harmonics_Metaphysics_Layer_V1_Sep1_2026.md` (16 rulings), `claude/COGHEIM_Harmonics_CanonStatement_Reconciliation_V1_Sep1_2026.md`, `claude/COGHEIM_Harmonics_Ring_Design_V1_Sep1_2026.md`, `claude/COGHEIM_Harmonics_BookV_Submission_Brief_V1_Sep1_2026.md`. **Amends and extends §26D.**

> **SEALED, INTERNAL. The publish boundary, restated because it is easy to get wrong now that the language is public:** the Einwechtëran **language** is public and **the note** is public. **The mechanism is not.** Ring, Residue, the grammar, Carriers, Figures, Cadence Plates, the bench, the three stations, the chutes, the dial, addresses, the Manifest, the schools, the Threefold, the Registers — none of it goes on the website, in a deck, or in any player-facing string before Book V. **Player-facing, the discipline is Harmonics and its origin is unexplained.**

### 26E-1. THE SIXTEEN RULINGS OF 1 SEPTEMBER

1. **The Threefold.** Every grain of the disc has three inseparable properties: **Body** (what it is made of), **Motion** (what it is doing), **Witness** (that it registers what happens to it). Harmonics addresses a grain by one of its three faces.
2. **The aspects are universal, not substrate-only.** Ordinary matter has them; the Einwechtëran engineered **access**. **This is the load-bearing item** — the Filament carrier, reading a hull plate, the Registers and all forensic play hang off it.
3. **The four Registers** — Dull, Set, Kept, Waking. Witness comes at four grades of fidelity, and **nobody living can make Waking matter.** This is the only reason Cadence Plates cannot be crafted and a forged plate fails a Name check.
4. **Witness is written by events, never by persons.** Kept in code regardless, since fiction is not a security boundary.
5. **Residue is the third aspect, not a penalty.** You cannot change Body or Motion without Witness recording it, so **magic cannot be used privately.**
6. **Ring is desynchronisation, not fatigue** — *superseded the same day by the Ring design, see 26E-3.*
7. **Novel-first publication.** Where the game and Book V disagree, the novel wins.
8. **The chutes are composed with the account model:** physical brass tubes into the disc, **shorter inside than out**, a 10–20 second ride joining zones hundreds of km apart. Every other thing about the machine is ordinary engineering.
9. **Glyph addresses are sentences** — an address describes a place by what it is, not as an abstract code. **You cannot dial what you cannot read.**
10. **Reading replaces the walk-there attunement gate.** **⚠ This supersedes Level Architecture V2 §7.3 gate two.**
11. **Three schools plus the Registrars.** The Assayers say the ground is a machine; the Choir say it is willing; the Abstainers say it is a debt. **The Registrars have no position and say so as doctrine**, because a record that takes a side stops being neutral.
12. **Affiliation has zero mechanical effect.** The argument is content, not a faction bonus.
13. **The Manifest** — every chute head keeps a permanent record of crossings: direction, rough time, and which Syndicate at high translation tier. **Never a person, never a live position.**
14. **Arrival Ring** — a fixed light dose on climbing out of a chute, applied to Tuners and non-Tuners alike.
15. **Sutured chutes are cut.**
16. **The language carries at least two grammars** — one for what to do (a Cadence Plate) and one for where (an address). Whether there are more is an unsettled in-world argument.

**Ruling 17 was made and then overturned the same day. See 26E-2.**

### 26E-2. 🔴 THE CANON RECONCILIATION — THE NOVEL RULED AND THE GAME FOLDED

**RULING 17 IS OVERTURNED. The underside of the disc is HELD.**

- **Not to be built, art-directed, modelled, scripted or written.** When it is eventually unheld it is **light, not structure** — a glow at depth with implied shapes that never resolve, a lighting setup rather than a level.
- **🛑 DO NOT RUN the v4 panel 6 prompt** in the Crossing storyboard §10.3, or the v4 single-page prompt in §10.4. Both ask for resolved structure, which is now out of canon.
- The existing plate's pale, unresolved port is **closer to correct than the correction would have been.** Keep the plate; treat panel 6 as provisional. The caption work stands.

**🔴 TERMINOLOGY CORRECTION: the ancient builders are the SAEL-VORI, not the Schallûsan.** The Schallûsan are the later line. **A project-wide sweep is required and is BLOCKED pending the §4.2b ruling** — whether the ancient script is properly called Einwechtëran at all. Sweeping before that ruling risks doing it twice.

**Three hard collisions still needing founder rulings:**

| # | Collision | What is at stake |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Filament is a conduit, not a Carrier** | The **48-combination grammar** is at risk. This is the largest mechanical exposure on the list |
| 2 | **GAIA is still building access points** vs "nobody makes Waking matter" | Two canon statements that cannot both stand as written |
| 3 | **The Waking Register needs redefinition as ANCHORED** | Proposed fix for collision 2 |
| 4 | **§4.2a — "a handful" of anchors vs 24 monuments** | A numeric contradiction inside existing canon |

### 26E-3. RING — DESIGNED WITH ITS ACCESSIBILITY ROUTE, NOT BEFORE IT

**Six founder rulings, 1 September. This supersedes Harmonics whitepaper §7.1 and §14.1 in full.**

**The problem nobody had named, and it was not only an accessibility problem.** As originally written Ring was an audio degradation — so **a player not using sound paid nothing.** A deaf player, or a hearing player who muted the game, was strictly better at Harmonics. **That is a balance inversion, and no amount of parallel feedback channels fixes it**, because parallel channels add feel to the players who already had feel. **The cost itself had to live somewhere every player has.**

| # | Ruling | |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The cause is THE NOTE.** A Listening is getting close enough to hear it; Ring is that you are still hearing it after you stop. **Masking, never damage.** Supersedes both the fatigue model and the desynchronisation model of ruling 6 | |
| 2 | **The real cost is server-computed coarsening of returns** — fewer Sound entries, less certain Recall, wider Count error bars, shallower Name attribution. **Sense-independent. This is the ruling that removes the inversion** | |
| 3 | **MAXIOM loses precision across the board while Rung**, not only for Harmonics. Ranges round off, bearings widen, ally status lags, needles wander. **A readout is a readout regardless of which sense reads it** — so a Rung player is a liability in a fight for everyone equally | |
| 4 | **NO THRESHOLDS, LOCKED.** Continuous degradation, no steps, no tiers, no breakpoints. Displayed as a needle, never a number. **This closes the whitepaper §13 Ring exploit by construction — if no number is magic, there is nothing to sit on** | |
| 5 | **A Sounding lightly Rings everyone at a station.** All three were close to the note. Gives crews a reason to rotate seats | |
| 6 | **Ring never touches assistive output.** Architectural, not a setting, and true from the first prototype | |

**Consequence of ruling 4, restated because it is the kind of thing reopened by accident:** any future proposal that introduces a tier, a breakpoint or a state change at a Ring value **reopens the §13 exploit and should be refused on those grounds.**

**Consequence of ruling 5, flagged for the bench spec:** a crew that Sounds repeatedly degrades its own next Sounding. **That is a natural rate limit on Residue writes arriving from the cost model rather than from a rate limiter. The whitepaper §13 rate limiter may now be redundant and should be re-examined.**

**The three expressive channels — audible, visible, haptic — carry feel and never information.** The MAXIOM readout is authoritative and visible. A player with all three off loses atmosphere and nothing else. **That is what makes turning one off a comfort setting rather than a competitive decision.**

**🔴 The failure case that would have shipped:** a blind player uses audio as their primary channel, and a naive Ring would degrade the accessibility layer itself — the more they played the discipline, the less able they would be to play the game. **Hard rule: diegetic sound is maskable; screen reader output, assistive cues and menu narration are exempt at every Ring level. The haptic channel must carry Ring's full feel alone.**

**In-world precedent already exists and should be used rather than invented: Ren notates. Christopher reads with his hands.** A Tuner working from a written trace or by touch is **a school of practice in this world, not an accommodation.**

### 26E-4. THE BOOK V SUBMISSION BRIEF

**Thirteen items are on the bill to Book V, ordered by what the game loses if the novel refuses.** **If the novel takes only one thing, take item 2** — the aspects being universal. Everything expensive hangs off it.

**Four questions only the novel can answer**, and the game has written no material assuming any answer: did the Suture make the substrate Waking or reveal that it always was · was the Suture itself a Sounding · is there a fourth aspect · what is beneath the disc.

**One existing canon hook that needs a deliberate answer:** established canon puts Einwechtëran glyphs on **the GAIA channels** — the one canon object whose name sounds like a tube network. **Are the chutes the GAIA channels, deliberately not, or unrelated?** The novel should say it rather than the game discovering the collision in a level review.

**What the game commits to in return, already in force:** ship unnamed · no consultation, no anonymous round, no NDA panel · the reveal stays the book's · **nothing is built** · a canon change-log entry for anything the novel changes, so no deviation is ever silent.

**The cost of getting this wrong today is one session.** That is why the brief exists now rather than after the grammar is in code.

### 26E-5. CLASSIFICATION

**Post-Alpha, in full.** The priority remains **the Drifter, Meridian Academy, RFA netcode and Iron Frames.** Everything in this section is design and writing, which per the V47 design-heavy / engine-light split costs the vertical slice nothing. **`SYS-26`, the engine half, is what competes** and it is not authorised.

---

## 26F. THE RESEARCH FLOOR & THE ACT OF RESEARCHING (1 September 2026)

**Sources:** `claude/COGHEIM_Academy_Research_Floor_V1_Sep1_2026.md`, `claude/COGHEIM_Research_Act_RivalTest_V1_Sep1_2026.md`. **Personal research only.** Syndicate research (the Foundry Ledger, §26B, dockets and specialties) is untouched.

> ### 🔴 AUTHORED BLIND. READ THIS BEFORE ENTERING A SINGLE NODE INTO THE BUILD.
> **§26 records 1,430 structural Iron Veleth nodes as already built, with real authored content for Einwechtëran Studies and Colossus Research. That content library was not open when these 260 nodes were written.** Node names will collide and some effects will duplicate. **Where they disagree, the built content wins unless a founder ruling says otherwise.**
> **The reconciliation pass outranks every other action on this system.** Authoring on top of a system you have not read is how a project loses work.

### 26F-1. THE FLOOR

A circular hall in the Academy. Brass and dark stone inlay, **the branching pattern is the Oasis symbol at architectural scale** — already canon. You stand in the middle and your MAXIOM lights the discipline you are working in beneath the floor: **earned nodes warm, available nodes dim, everything beyond dark.** No menu, no overlay, no floating text.

**The social layer is free and it is the best part.** The lit pattern is visible to anyone else in the hall, so **standing on the floor shows people what you have studied.** A scholar recognises another scholar across a room without a word. That makes the floor a meeting place rather than a menu, and it costs one shader parameter. **Reading another player's floor is a courtesy, never an entitlement** — a setting hides it, and hiding it is normal rather than suspicious.

**Committing a node:** you carry materials to the rim and place them in a drawer. **The floor is a machine.** Brass inlay lights along the line from the centre outward, one segment at a time, travelling — **not a bar, a route.** The plate rises, splits, takes the material. On completion the node lights permanently and the light spreads one step further out. **And other people in the hall see it happen.**

**The Commonplace** — a physical notebook the character carries. At the bench, under lamp light, **your own handwriting appears on the page** as the operation finishes. It is already canon practice (Ren notates; Christopher writes down what he finds), it is the offline artefact, and it is what a screenshot is of.

**The MAXIOM star-field view is the PORTABLE view, not the primary one.** The real floor is at the Academy, and that asymmetry is the reason to go there.

**What it must never look like:** no floating node cards, no tooltips in space, no percentage, **no progress bar anywhere including at the bench, and no skip button ever, in any form, including a free one.**

### 26F-2. THE CURRENCY — PAGES

**Pages, kept in the Commonplace. Proposed, pending a check against whatever Iron Veleth already uses.**

**Why not dockets.** Dockets are capped at two per player per day and already fought over by officers (§12B-5). **Adding a third claimant to that cap makes personal progression compete with the Syndicate's, which is the wrong fight to design.**

**The earning rule, inherited from the locked docket rule because it works:** **each discipline earns pages from its own activity only.** Walking and surveying earn Wayfinding; fighting earns Gunnery; repair earns Field Engineering; Listening earns Resonance Practice; translating earns Einwechtëran Studies. **You cannot grind one activity to fund the whole floor.**

**No page is ever purchasable, giftable, tradeable or accelerable with Crownmarks.** Research consumables affect throughput and never outcome, unchanged.

### 26F-3. THE EIGHT DISCIPLINES AND THE NODE COUNT

| # | Discipline | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Constitution | Proposed |
| 2 | Field Engineering | Proposed |
| 3 | Gunnery | Proposed |
| 4 | Wayfinding | Proposed |
| 5 | **Colossus Research** | **CONFIRMED** |
| 6 | **Einwechtëran Studies** | **CONFIRMED** |
| 7 | Resonance Practice | Proposed |
| 8 | Parley | Proposed |

**30 nodes per discipline** — Tier 1: 10 at 6 pages · Tier 2: 12 at 14 · Tier 3: 8 at 30 — **summing to the locked 240.** Plus **Applied Synthesis: 20 nodes at 60 pages**, requiring Tier 3 in four or more disciplines, 8 quests each. **Launch total: 4,944 pages across 260 nodes.**

**Pacing lands the founder target:** a heavy player is very nearly through the launch set in about a month; a light player has a season of it. **Turn costs to slow people. Never turn node count down** — the floor's whole appeal is that it is dense and small-stepped.

### 26F-4. THE SEVEN DESIGN LAWS

1. **Small.** A numeric node moves one number by 1–2%. Power comes from having many and from combination.
2. **Additive, never multiplicative.** Three nodes touching one number sum; they never compound.
3. **At least half of every discipline is visibility or capability, not a number.** *Skill equals Visibility* enforced as a ratio rather than a slogan, and **the main defence against power creep.**
4. **No node grants a Cadence Plate, an address, or a Figure.** Those are found. **Research improves how well you use what you found; it never gives you what to find.**
5. **No node makes another player weaker.**
6. **No class.** No XP levels, no points from a pool, no picker. **A floor pattern is a history of what you did, not a build you chose.**
7. **Personal research improves the PERSON; Syndicate research unlocks MODULES.** Never the same number twice.

**What must never grow: the size of any single bonus.** A Tier 4 node is still 1–2%. **The floor gets wider, never steeper.**

### 26F-5. 🟡 THE CANON TENSION, AND THE RECONCILIATION

**Founder direction:** Harmonics plays a role in attacking efficiency and military strategy.
**Whitepaper §11, locked in writing:** no damage, no healing, no combat buffs. *"A Tuner does not make the crew hit harder."*

> **Reconciliation: Harmonics never adds damage. It tells you where to aim.**

What Harmonics contributes to a fight is **information** — which plate is thin, how many bodies are behind that bulkhead, whether the ground will hold, how old that repair is. **A crew that knows where the weak seam is puts fewer shells into a strong one. Nothing hits harder; fewer shots are wasted.** Same result, §11 intact, and a far more distinctive thing to own than a damage percentage. The Gunnery nodes **Seam Shooting** and **Plate Judgement** are this made concrete: one lets you act on information a Tuner supplied, the other gives a non-Tuner a rough version of the same read.

**Requires a founder ruling to confirm.** If a literal damage bonus was intended, **§11 has to be reopened deliberately and visibly**, and the recommendation is against it.

### 26F-6. THE ACT — TWO FOUNDER LOCKS, 1 SEPTEMBER 2026

**A node is a single, short, hands-on act, performed once, that is the thing the node is about.** Not a minigame, not a puzzle with a fail state. **An operation** in the trade sense: two to four minutes, under a lamp, with the materials in front of you. **You cannot fail it and it never has a score.** Then you know it, forever, and the node lights.

**🔒 LOCK ONE. NO SKIP, PAID OR FREE, IN ANY FORM.** No rush button, no purchasable acceleration, no free bypass, no auto-complete, no consumable that finishes a node.
*Grounds for refusing any future proposal:* this is the exact surface ESO, EVE and Warframe monetise, and whitepaper §9 already refuses selling it. **A free skip is worse than a paid one, because it makes the operation optional and an optional operation is dead content nobody builds well.**

**🔒 LOCK TWO. TWELVE OPERATION TYPES. FIFTEEN IS THE HARD CEILING.**
Fit · Strip and Set · Read · Sort · Measure · Pace · Listen · Drill · Site · Compare · Assemble · Negotiate.
**Content varies per node. The type library does not.** 260 bespoke operations is 260 systems, unaffordable, and would be built badly. **If the library pushes past fifteen, that is the signal the approach has failed**, and the correct response is the fallback below, not more types.
*Recorded as adopted by implication:* operation types exist only inside the operation model, so **locking lock two adopts the operation model itself.**

**The material is the clock. No node has a timer.** Some materials are near; some exist only in a region the Strider must travel to, which is a course decision the crew has to argue for; some come only from an event, a wreck or a depth. **A Tier 3 node might be a week away because its material is a week away — the same pacing outcome as a seven-day timer, arrived at through play rather than through absence.**
**Materials are consumed on completion, never on commit.** A disconnect mid-operation loses nothing. **Architecture rule, not a courtesy.**

**Two lanes, and this answers the one real argument for timers.** Personal (the Floor) is **active** — nothing accrues while you are away. Syndicate (the Foundry Ledger) **accumulates** on other people's dockets while you sleep. **So the answer to "I want to log in and find I have progressed" is: you did, aboard your Syndicate.**

**Fallback, available at any point including after launch:** the original gather-deliver-timer model. **It costs nothing but distinctiveness** and does not disturb the node tree, the pages economy or the floor. *If that fallback is ever taken, Starfield's per-category slot — one node in progress per discipline — becomes a good pacing lever. It has no purchase under the operation model.*

**Counter-metric, watched hardest: the rate at which players ask for a skip button.** If it rises, **shorten the operations. Never sell the skip, and never add a free one.**

### 26F-7. ACCESSIBILITY — GATING, NOT OPTIONAL

Same standard as Ring. **No operation requires reflexes, reaction time or precise timing · none requires fine sensory discrimination in any channel · none has a fail state** (a confused player gets more help, never a failure) · every operation has a described completion path usable without sight, and Listen and Measure must both be completable without hearing · no rapid or repeated input · **length capped at a few minutes, walk away and come back with nothing lost.**

**Notation is the accessibility node and it sits deliberately at Tier 1.** Reading the trace rather than the sound is a school of practice in this world and should be available on day one.

### 26F-8. HARD RULES CARRIED FROM ELSEWHERE

- **🔴 No node in Colossus Research — and no node anywhere on the floor — may alter Colossus eligibility or targeting.** *Reading the rules is content. Changing them is not, at any tier, ever.* Eligibility Reading exists to make a frightening system legible, which is the whole point of a system that is also fair.
- **Every Ring effect is continuous.** A node that created a threshold would reopen the §13 exploit (26E-3 ruling 4).
- **Anchor Recognition teaches WHAT, never WHY.** A player learns there are two kinds of anchor long before anybody tells them one is ancient.
- **🟡 "The Arrangement" as a node name needs a canon check** — the Canon Statement uses the phrase for the Sael-Vori arrangement of the door's glyphs, and it may be too close to sealed material.

**Address Construction is the payoff of ruling 9 and the best node on the floor.** Because an address is a sentence rather than a code, a strong reader can reason toward a route and find out they were wrong by arriving somewhere else. **No other game has a fast-travel system you can think your way into.**

### 26F-9. CLASSIFICATION

**Post-Alpha.** §26A puts personal progression explicitly out of Alpha scope and that stands. **Design costs the Alpha nothing; this authorises no engineering.** The twelve-type library is a real engineering line when it is eventually scheduled and **should be scheduled honestly rather than absorbed into a content budget.**

**Production pipeline (locked — wkhtmltopdf stack):**
- python-docx paragraph extraction → type classification (body / tech / act_header / bold / italic / bold_italic)
- HTML/CSS generation (14pt Georgia, 1.7 line spacing, amber left-border tech blocks, hyphens:none, page-break-inside:avoid)
- wkhtmltopdf → prose PDF
- img2pdf → full-bleed 6×9 act illustration pages (1800×2700px JPEG at 90% quality)
- pypdf merge (replace blank placeholder pages with illustration pages)
- reportlab/pypdf → page number overlays

**Critical pipeline lessons:**
- 14pt font size threshold reliably separates scene numbers (13pt) from act titles (20–24pt)
- Tech block border-only lines: filter with `^[═╔╗╚╝─━\s]*$` regex before content extraction
- `p.paragraph_format.alignment` must compare against `WD_ALIGN_PARAGRAPH.CENTER` enum (not string "CENTER")
- Page number overlays via reportlab/pypdf more reliable than wkhtmltopdf footer flags
- XeLaTeX incompatible with Books I/II visual system — always use wkhtmltopdf for COGHEIM books
- DOCX files do not persist to Google Drive unless explicitly saved there

**Post-V12 canon locks (Books I–IV):**
- "Einwechter" replaced with "Einwechtëran" throughout all prose, tech blocks, front matter, and status lines across all published books
- **Tiffany carries the Sael-Vori lineage** ("the receiving end of the question")

- The Oasis requires both lineages present simultaneously
- Book IV ends: GAIA-9 speaks "Two names" at the threshold

- Book III: acts expanded with new sub-scenes; pipeline rebuilt for font accuracy; scrambled act ordering fixed

Fix: zero the CSS body margin, let wkhtmltopdf carry all margins (18mm left / 20mm right / 20mm top / 18mm bottom). Verified via pdfplumber at exactly 18.0mm left / 20.1mm right / 114.3mm text column — matching Books II, III, and IV. This is now the standing pipeline rule for all five books.

## 33A. COGWORKS MODULAR KIT ASSET PIPELINE

**Status:** All 20 structural pieces (S01–S20) built, per-part verified, kit-level tiling verified, and exported to FBX with authored collision. **Not yet imported into Unreal** — that confirmation is the next Shadow PC step. Sources: `COGHEIM_Cogworks_Asset_Pipeline_Aug2026.md` (26 Aug), `COGHEIM_Cogworks_Surfacing_Spec_V1.md` (27 Aug), `COGHEIM_Cogworks_Kit_Generator_Handoff.md` (28 Aug), `COGHEIM_Cogworks_FBX_Pipeline_Aug2026.md` (28 Aug). Related: §35G, §35H.

### Why structural pieces are parametric Blender, not generative 3D — locked decision, validated by test

Four pipelines were tested on the same wall panel (S01); only parametric Blender succeeded for a modular kit piece:

| Approach | Result |
|---|---|
| Tripo text-to-3D | Failed — hardware protrudes past the silhouette, wrong aspect ratio, tile subdivides into a grid, invents a frame border |
| Flat orthographic elevation → Tripo | Failed — no depth cue, so it extruded the footprint into a **column**, not a panel |
| Three-quarter concept render → Tripo | Worked, but only as a **bake source** (916 faces, slight residual taper) |
| **Parametric Blender** | **Exact dimensions, exact origins, 12 tris, regenerable** — because a modular kit is a constraint problem, not a generation problem |

**Props (P01–P22) are unaffected and stay on Tripo text-to-3D**, which is where generative 3D genuinely wins — a prop *is* an object. Decal sheets stay on image generation.

### Poly budgets — recommendation, requires founder approval before it becomes a hard gate

Structural tile 12–200 tris, structural trim 100–600, door/window frame 300–1,000, small/medium/hero props 300–20,000. **A solid wall panel is a box: 12 triangles.** Detail lives entirely in a baked normal map. Budgets matter more here than in a typical game because of triple multiplication: instancing (one panel repeated thousands of times per Strider), scale (~200 Striders per server, 500 targeted), and an ever-growing player-building catalogue that inherits whatever budget is set now. **Nanite solves raster cost, not disk/memory/package-size/build-time cost, and does not make bad topology good** — Tripo's dense output has no edge flow and no LOD chain, so it is a bake source only, never shipped geometry.

### Surfacing — the skin/decal split (locked decision)

Solves the "same wall recognized on every deck" problem cheaply: variation is layered on top of a small mesh set via four layers, ordered by uniqueness-per-cost — per-instance material variation (near-zero runtime cost) → decal layer (patches/stains/repairs by rule) → palette by deck function (parameters only) → geometric variants (3 wall meshes, not 20). **A SKIN is what a room *is*** (authored, parameter-driven, not texture-swapped — a bespoke texture set costs full memory every time a parameter set costs a handful of floats). **A DECAL is what a room has *been through*** (earned or rule-scattered).

**🔒 LOCKED — the neutral-bake rule:** weathering must never be baked into the albedo. The generative pipeline captures *structure* (rivets, seams, tread → normal/AO/curvature/cavity), not *weather* — rust/soot/paint apply afterward as material layers driven by the curvature/cavity masks. Consequence: the original Aug 26 S01 test panel's albedo is discarded and rebuilt; all structural concept prompts revised 27 Aug to request minimally weathered renders.

**🔒 LOCKED — the Crownmark livery boundary, pillar-critical:** a Strider livery (house colors, trim, decals with no gameplay meaning) is a legitimate cosmetic category. **Prohibited without exception:** any skin that alters or *appears to* alter armor/durability/speed/capacity, reveals otherwise-unavailable information, or accelerates any process — and **a skin must never be named to imply function** ("reinforced," "hardened," "armoured" liveries are banned outright), because implied capability breaks Zero Pay-to-Win even when the underlying numbers are clean. Consistent with §17A's livery rules.

**Design proposal, not canon — memory-driven surfacing:** surfaces record what happened to them (a Colossus-damaged panel keeps its patch permanently, tied to the Syndicate Memory Engine, §25). Rival Test: no competitor's world weathers in response to events; the advantage is persistence and consequence. Requires a bounded design (fixed decal slots per surface, capped record with oldest-non-significant eviction, no free-text/unmoderated content on permanent surfaces) before it is more than an idea. **Open founder decisions:** the six proposed deck palettes; livery as a formal Crownmark category and its naming rule; whether this enters the Alpha prototype list; "livery" vs. "skin" as the player-facing term.

### The kit generator, harness, and assembly test

`cogworks_kit_gen.py` (parametric Blender, headless via `pip install bpy`) produces a LOW (shipped) and HIGH (bake source) mesh per part. **All 20 parts implemented and verified** by a per-part harness (tri budget, LOW/HIGH footprint agreement) — which caught real errors, including a `cylinder()` primitive rotation bug where `bpy`'s headless build doesn't refresh `ob.dimensions` until a rotation is baked in.

**What the per-part harness structurally cannot catch, and did miss:** a real bug where floor-family parts (S07/S08/S09/S11/S18) were silently inheriting the wall-family 2cm overlap spacing, because the harness only ever compares a part against its own HIGH twin, never against a neighboring instance. Found and fixed 28 Aug by `cogworks_assembly_test.py`, a new kit-level test that assembles sample LOW parts (wall runs, a hung door, floor tiling, a stair/railing pairing) and checks seam overlap, doorway clearance, and flat-lie orientation — all checks now pass, floor seam gap 0.000cm.

**S15 (Lift_Collar)** resolved 28 Aug — its spec was already written in `COGHEIM_Modular_Kit_Prompt_Sheet_V1.md`, found by a targeted `project_search`, not rewritten from scratch. Crossed-lattice safety gate built as real silhouette geometry (504 LOW tris) via a new `bar()` primitive.

### Path to real Drifter assembly — no longer decision-blocked

The grey-box assembly test proves the kit tiles correctly in isolation; placing it into the actual Drifter interior needs the re-parameterized blockout (§35G), and as of 28 Aug the mechanical path is fully specified, just not yet applied. **Separately tabled, not yet reconciled:** `CogheimAcademy` already has a working procedural placement system for Meridian Maritime Academy (31-piece kit, 137-rule layout, 2,216 instances, compiles and links, blockout-walkable) whose 31-piece list has not been located in project docs — worth checking whether S01–S20 already covers Academy pieces before building a second kit from scratch.

## 33B. THE SPECIALTY BADGE SET — APPROVED CONCEPT (1 September 2026) & ART-DIRECTION CORRECTIONS

**Source:** `claude/COGHEIM_Specialty_Badge_Spec_APPROVED_Sep1_2026.md`. Board version V3.

**Status: approved as concept.** All eleven passed the **64-pixel 1-bit silhouette test** across three iterations — individually identifiable, **no two sharing a shape or an object.**
**Outstanding: a flat two-colour vector redraw** for the lamps, the Signatory Engine booth selectors and hull markings, then one more silhouette test on those. These are painterly concept paintings, not production icons.

> **⚠ The approved board image is NOT in project knowledge.** The upload was refused — the project is near its storage cap. **`COGHEIM_Specialty_Badge_Board_APPROVED_v3_Sep1_2026.png` must be added manually via claude.ai.** The written spec is authoritative and stands without it.

Each badge is a cast brass and iron medallion: raised riveted outer ring, recessed enamel field, one engraved symbol, brass nameplate in period industrial serif.

| # | Specialty | Symbol | Enamel |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Ordnance | Crossed artillery barrels over a mortar shell | Deep ember red |
| 2 | Ironclad | Curved riveted hull armour plate, angled, one impact dent | Cold gunmetal blue |
| 3 | Marque | Dripping wax seal with trailing ribbon | Deep burgundy |
| 4 | Rail & Route | Winged spoked wheel over a rail junction | Dark bronze |
| 5 | Counting House | Balance scale above a stack of coins | Deep green |
| 6 | The Exchange | Banded strongbox with a brass merchant's stamp | Ochre |
| 7 | Broadsheet | A single printing press, screw head-on | Ivory |
| 8 | The Forge | Hammer resting across an anvil | Glowing orange |
| 9 | The Seam | Single pick driven into a rock face with an ore vein | Verdigris-copper |
| 10 | The Larder | Wheat sheaf beside a lidded cook pot | Warm amber |
| 11 | The Almanac | Brass sextant alone, arc downward | Cold cyan |

**Six design rules that produced this set — hold them in any redraw.** Only Ordnance uses the crossed-X composition (three X badges were indistinguishable at lamp size) · **no two badges share an object** (the Exchange lost its book and keys; the Almanac stands as a sextant alone) · one object per badge where possible (Broadsheet dropped its speaking horn and became the strongest silhouette in the set) · **no heraldry** (Ironclad was a medieval shield in v1, the only knights-and-castles element on the board) · every enamel field saturated and distinct (the Seam was charcoal and vanished into the dark panel) · **every symbol nameable in pure black and white at 64 pixels.**

### 33B-1. LAMP RULES — SYSTEM RULES, NOT ART NOTES

The Charter Sign in a Strider command centre carries **five lamp housings.**

- **A lit lamp burns in ITS OWN enamel colour**, never uniform amber.
- **🔴 An unlit lamp is COMPLETELY EMPTY** — dead grey glass, no symbol, nothing etched, nothing dimmed. Dust and a faint reflection only. **An unearned slot has no specialty assigned to it yet.** A dimmed symbol would imply the Syndicate holds five specialties with two switched off, when the truth is it holds three and has two empty seats. **It is also what makes the fourth lamp lighting up mean something — you cannot earn a lamp that was already showing you what it would be.**
- **Lamps fill left to right.** A **filling** state exists for the next slot: a slow partial glow as mastery research completes.
- **Alarm:** all five pulse red during a mutiny reading. **Succession:** slow amber pulse while the Commander is absent past 14 days.

**Diegetic constraint, restated:** no screens, no holograms, no pixels, no glowing projections. **Every light in the Cogheim UI is a real lamp behind real glass.** Chart tables are printed paper held down by brass weights and **lit from above — light falls on them, it never comes out of them.**

### 33B-2. 🔴 CORRECTION TO THE STANDING "NO GLOW" NEGATIVE

**The blanket "no glow, anywhere" line used in the Harmonics figure prompts was wrong as written and contradicted §33's locked art direction**, which requires **dual competing light sources, warm amber and gold interior against cold cyan and teal** — and names the cold side as instrument glow. **A cyan-lit instrument on a bench is on brand and wanted.**

**The rule, corrected, and it replaces the blanket line in every future prompt from this project:**

- **Instruments, lamps, gauges and machinery MAY glow**, and the cold side of the two-light rule usually requires that something does.
- **Harmonics itself NEVER glows.** No glowing runes, no energy, no aura around a practitioner, no lit Residue, no visible field. **The discipline is invisible and the machinery is not.**

### 33B-3. 🔴 THE FAINTNESS LESSON — REUSABLE, PAID FOR ONCE

A panel asked for something distant and half-glimpsed and described it as *"barely resolved, more suggested than drawn."* **The model heard *draw almost nothing*, which is a reasonable reading and the wrong result** — the panel came back reading as glare and scratches on glass.

> **The correct instruction is the inverse: draw it completely, then wash it back. HIGH DETAIL, LOW CONTRAST — never low detail. Faintness is a value problem, not a detail problem.**

**Anywhere in this project where something must read as distant, hazy or half-glimpsed, specify the contrast and never the level of detail.**

### 33B-4. TWO DOCUMENTED GENERATOR DRIFTS, AND THE FIX

**"Painterly" prompts drift to oil painting. "Photoreal" prompts drift to real photographs.** The fix that works is **explicit AAA game engine render-target language:** *high end AAA game engine character art, UE5 cinematic quality, physically based materials, subsurface skin scattering, real time render look.*

**Locked background rule:** softly blurred shallow depth of field on an **in-world location appropriate to the character's post.** **Never a flat dark studio backdrop, which reads too grimdark.** A three-by-two backdrop reference grid exists; **attach the relevant panel as an image reference rather than describing it.**

**Two style families, and they are not interchangeable.** Internal technical plates are **ink and wash**, nineteenth-century engineering-volume style, on aged paper ground with a margin — **never a dark background**, because the plates are tipped into a dark page and the contrast is the design. Character portraits and hall scenes are **AAA engine renders.**
