## 35A. ALPHA TRACK DEPLOY PIPELINE — `Deploy-CogheimAlpha.ps1`

A single consolidated PowerShell script (successor to the per-system `Deploy-CogheimNetcode.ps1` pattern) placing all Alpha modules into a fresh UE5.8 project in one pass. **Built by inspecting every zip's actual contents, not their descriptions** — which is how it came to encode five real landmines that would each have broken the build:

1. Missing `IMPLEMENT_MODULE` entry points in **SRF** and **Weather**
2. `CogheimLogin`'s `INTEGRATE_ME` files, which would have compiled as a **second competing GameMode** — diverted to `_ManualIntegration\`
3. A **malformed brace-expansion directory** in the Dialogue zip — skipped
4. Two **nested-zip structures** (WorldMap, MAXIOM) needing unwrapping
5. Missing `Build.cs` for six modules (Comms, WeaponsCrafting, SyndicateResearch, Drifter, SRF, Weather) — authored

The script also redirects non-code files to `_ModuleDocs\`, runs a post-placement sanity check confirming each module has both a `Build.cs` and an entry point, and prints carry-forward flags at completion.

**🔴 Encoding lesson, logged:** a **UTF-8 BOM plus an em dash** caused a parse failure under Windows PowerShell 5.1 on Shadow PC. **All PowerShell scripts must be ASCII-only, with a BOM.** No typographic dashes in script bodies.

This is recorded here rather than quietly overwritten, because the original wrong diagnosis was published in a handoff document and could otherwise be acted on. See the new §38 rule 14.

**Overall read:** the runtime code behaves correctly in Groups 2 and 3; the tests assert Python exception semantics against the C++ fail-closed-and-log convention. Group 1 is bookkeeping against canon locked hours earlier.

### 🔒 FOUNDER DECISION (Aug 2026) — SENSORS DO NOT SCALE WITH THE DISC

The disc was re-locked 600km → 2,500km (**×4.16667**). Sensor ranges had only received a 2× bump ("Blend 2x bump, Jul 2026"), so relative discoverability fell roughly 2×.

**Founder's call: keep the 2× sensor values. The world got bigger; sensors did not.**

Consequences now asserted in spec, computed from the live 738-location registry:
| Consequence | Value |
|---|---|
| Densest Grindwalker-range cluster | fell **7 → 5** locations, and **moved** from The Cinder Foundry (Ironvast abandoned city) to an **Ironvast river braid** (RIV14/19/24/27/40) |
| Grindwalker parked at Coldforge Landing sees | **3** locations |
| Blackjack at the same spot sees | **6** |
| On foot | **1** |
| The SaltFork (RIV23) from Coldforge Landing | **28.3 km** — outside Grindwalker range, **inside** Blackjack range |

**Design read:** exploration is meaningfully scarcer than pre-rescale. This *strengthens* the 4X exploration pillar and gives Scavenger-class scouting a genuine job — sensor range becomes a real class differentiator rather than a stat line. **⚠ REQUIRES VALIDATION — this has not been playtested.** Scarcer exploration is a hypothesis about improved play, not a measured result, and it should not be presented as a proven design win in any external material.

---

### 🔒 FOUNDER DECISION (Aug 2026) — IRON FRAME JUMP WAS A TEST BUG, NOT A LOCOMOTION CHANGE

The failing check configured an Untrained frame (×0.40) then advanced it 0.1s:

| Tier | Jump velocity | Apex | Hang time |
|---|---|---|---|
| Untrained (×0.40) | 80 cm/s | **3.3 cm** | 0.163 s |
| Tier 5 (×1.00) | 200 cm/s | **20.4 cm** | 0.408 s |

A single 0.1s Euler step is **61% of the Untrained hop**, so the frame had already landed before the airborne assertion ran. The test set Tier 5 on `LocomotionComponent` but not on `JumpComponent`.

**Founder's call: Option A — fix the test only. Locked locomotion untouched.**

🔴 **OPEN ITEM — REQUIRES FOUNDER APPROVAL (parked, not closed):** `JumpVelocityPerMobilityPoint = 2.f` yields a **3.3 cm** hop untrained and **20.4 cm** at Tier 5. For a pilotable industrial exoskeleton this is likely far below intent — clearing a barricade or a Drifter deck lip needs roughly **15–20 cm/s per Mobility point** (a 1–2 m jump). **A playtester will read a frame that cannot clear a deck lip as broken, even though the code is correct.** This is a design question deserving its own tuning pass and prototype, not a bug. **Decide before Alpha.**

## 35C. DRIFTER BLOCKOUT — IN-EDITOR SPAWN & THE Z-DOWN FINDING (August 2026)

**Status (V40):** 🟢 **The Roll-180° vs. negate-Z decision below is CLOSED — founder-locked to negate-Z, 28 Aug 2026.** See new §35G for the lock record and the full mechanical change spec (not yet applied). The rest of this section — the original finding, the in-editor spawn status, the no-parent-Actor gap — is retained below as the record of how the decision was reached, and remains otherwise unchanged: still no `REPORT:` block, blockout still not walked.

**Status (V38):** 🟡 **In progress, per founder confirmation Aug 24 — not complete, no `REPORT:` block confirmed yet.** Do not read this as walked until that block is pasted in and verified. Everything below (V34 status, the Z-down finding, the Roll-180° recommendation) still stands as the last verified state.

**Status:** 🟡 **STILL NOT RUN as of V34.** A dedicated execution session (Aug 18) planned the full run order but ended before execution — it closed requesting `cogheim_spawn_drifter_blockout.py` and `DrifterBlockoutGenerator.h` to verify the `SpawnBlockoutInWorld` signature and `DrifterOrigin` parameter before running anything. **No `REPORT:` block exists. The blockout has never been walked.**

**🆕 Architectural gap surfaced in that session (real, and easy to miss):** `SpawnBlockoutInWorld` spawns actors **directly into the editor world**, so the geometry bakes as **loose level actors with no parent Actor**. That means **no host Actor to attach an SRF frame to.** Fine for walking a static blockout; blocking the moment the Drifter needs to *move* with a player inside it. Correctly out of scope for M3 — **but do not let a working blockout create the impression that seam is closed.**

**⚠ Also noted:** step 7 places a **PlayerStart**, and a PlayerStart *is content*. It is cheap to move, so it isn't a hard stop — but the `Roll = 180°` vs. negate-Z ruling should still land before props or nav go in.

Script on the Shadow PC at `C:\CogheimAlpha\CogheimAlpha\cogheim_spawn_drifter_blockout.py`.

This is the first attempt to actually **walk** the Drifter blockout in-editor rather than assert it in a spec.

### 🔴 THE FINDING — the blockout is built Z-DOWN

`DrifterDeckGeometry::SpineZ(1) == 0`, and `SpineZ` **grows with deck number**. Inside each deck, `GenerateZoneBoxes` places the **floor** at `Z0 + H - T/2` and the **ceiling** at `Z0 + T/2` (lines 166/170; the same pattern appears in corridors at 233/234, and the window header/sill math agrees).

**So local +Z points toward the keel.** Deck 1 is the top deck at local Z 0; the hull runs *down* to local Z 2760. That is internally consistent and matches canon — **but Unreal's +Z is up.** Spawned at identity, the ship arrives upside down, every room's floor sits above its ceiling, and the character falls through the world.

**Tonight's fix (no C++ change):** pass `Roll = 180°` via the existing `DrifterOrigin` parameter, mapping local +Z to world −Z. Deck 1 lands at world Z 0; Deck 6 hangs at roughly Z −2760; floors end up underfoot. The script does this.

### ⚠ ARCHITECTURE ITEM — REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION, BEFORE CONTENT IS PLACED

`Roll = 180°` is a **display fix layered over a coordinate-convention mismatch**, and this project has a system that cares: **SRF/RFA resolves authority, relevancy, and quantization in frame-local space.** A permanently rolled Drifter actor means every child actor, nav mesh, physics query, spawn transform, and future SRF frame transform inherits a flipped basis.

| | Roll 180° at spawn | Negate Z at generation |
|---|---|---|
| Tonight | Works, zero C++ | Small C++ change in `GenerateZoneBoxes` + `SpineZ` |
| Nav / physics | Inherits rolled basis | Clean |
| SRF / RFA | Frame-local math sits on a rolled actor | Local +Z = world +Z, matches engine |
| Canon ("Deck 1 = top") | Preserved in data | Preserved in data, flipped at the geometry boundary |
| Cost | Deferred, and rises once content is placed | ~1 session, now |

**Recommendation:** run tonight with `Roll = 180°` and prove the blockout walks — do not block on this. But **decide before anything is placed in that level** (props, PlayerStarts, nav, spawn points). Rework cost only goes up after placement.

### Setup sequence (Shadow PC)
1. **Third Person content pack** — Tools → Add Feature or Content Pack → Blueprint Feature → Third Person. Supplies `BP_ThirdPersonCharacter` and `Content/Input/` (`IMC_Default`, `IA_Move`, `IA_Look`, `IA_Jump`). **This makes `phase2_setup.py` redundant.**
2. **New Level → Basic** (not Empty — Empty has no lighting). **Delete the `Floor` actor** (it sits at Z 0 and fights Deck 1). Keep DirectionalLight, SkyLight, SkyAtmosphere, ExponentialHeightFog. Save as `Content/Maps/L_Drifter_Blockout`. Set Editor Startup Map, Game Default Map, and Default GameMode (`BP_ThirdPersonGameMode`) in Project Settings — this also clears the stale `/Engine/Maps/Templates/OpenWorld` default currently in `DefaultEngine.ini`.
3. **Enable Python Editor Script Plugin**, restart, then run the script from the Output Log's Python console.
4. **Save the level** — actors spawn into the editor world, so saving bakes them in. After this the blockout is **level content, not a re-runnable script**.
5. **PlayerStart at roughly Z −450**, inside a Deck 1 zone clear of walls (Deck 1 spans world Z 0 down to −550, floor slab ≈ Z −540).

### Acceptance gate
The script prints `REPORT:` lines. **The one that matters is `actors still at scale 1,1,1 = 0`.** Anything other than zero means the mobility bug fired and the geometry is garbage — **stop, do not walk it.**

### Known hazards
- **Python method arity** — `SpawnBlockoutInWorld` has `meta=(WorldContext=...)`; the Python glue sometimes hides that parameter. The script tries both signatures and reports which worked.
- **Doorways 100 × 200 cm, corridors 150 cm wide** vs. a Mannequin capsule of 34 cm radius / 88 cm half-height. It fits. `/Engine/BasicShapes/Cube` uses simple box collision, so a doorway that *looks* blocked means overlapping jamb boxes, not undersized geometry.
- **Deck 1's Z 0 ceiling slab** occupies exactly where the Basic level's default floor sat — hence step 2.

### 📌 Project-file change to record
Enabling the **Python Editor Script Plugin** modifies the `.uproject` plugin list, which currently holds only `ModelingToolsEditorMode` and `ReplicationGraph`. Logged here alongside the ReplicationGraph enable so the plugin set stays traceable.

## 35D. `CogheimDialogueUI` EDITOR-INIT BLOCKER — ROOT CAUSE & FIX (August 2026)

**Status: CLOSED, verified on Shadow PC August 25, 2026.** The first time this project reached a running editor with all 15 modules initialized.

### The symptom that misled two sessions
The project built and linked clean — `Rebuild All: 1 succeeded, 0 failed` — and all 15 module DLLs *loaded* at editor startup, `UnrealEditor-CogheimDialogueUI.dll` included. Then:

> The game module 'CogheimDialogueUI' could not be successfully initialized after it was loaded.

followed by `EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION reading address 0xffffffffffffffff` with a stack containing **no `CogheimDialogueUI` frames at all** — six `UnrealEditor-CoreUObject.dll` frames, eight `UnrealEditor.exe` frames, `KERNEL32.DLL`.

That stack is why two prior hypotheses (a Blueprint signature mismatch, then stale generated headers) were both wrong. **The log timing broke it open:** the error dialog closed at `22:56:16`, `EngineExit()` was called in the same second, `CloseDisregardForGC` ran at `22:56:17`, and the access violation landed at `22:56:19`. The crash was never `CogheimDialogueUI` initialising — it was the engine tearing itself down three seconds later, in a shutdown the failed init had triggered.

### Root cause
`CogheimDialogueUI` had **no module class**. The module contained exactly four files — `CogheimDialogueWidget.h/.cpp` and `CogheimChoicePlateWidget.h/.cpp` — and no `IMPLEMENT_MODULE` anywhere. `FModuleManager` loads the DLL, calls `GetProcAddress` for `InitializeModule`, gets null, and reports precisely that message.

**A missing entry point is invisible to both the compiler and the linker.** Nothing references `InitializeModule` at build time; it is looked up by name at runtime. This is why a green `Rebuild All` was never evidence the module worked, and why the defect survived a full clean rebuild of the module's `Intermediate\Build\...\Inc\CogheimDialogueUI\` headers.

### The fix
Two new files, matching the `CogheimLogin` / `CogheimAcademy` house pattern (header in `Public\`, implementation in `Private\`, empty `Startup`/`Shutdown` — this is a presentation-only module):

- `Source\CogheimDialogueUI\Public\CogheimDialogueUIModule.h` — `FCogheimDialogueUIModule : public IModuleInterface`
- `Source\CogheimDialogueUI\Private\CogheimDialogueUIModule.cpp` — `IMPLEMENT_MODULE(FCogheimDialogueUIModule, CogheimDialogueUI)`

**Purely additive. No existing file was modified** — the `InCaptionText` rename was left untouched, and the `.uproject` needed no change (`CogheimDialogueUI` was already registered with `LoadingPhase: "Default"`, identical to all 14 siblings, which ruled out load-phase as a cause).

### Verified result (measured, not projected)
| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `UnrealEditor-CogheimDialogueUI.dll` rebuilt | 152,576 → **153,088 bytes** |
| Cogheim modules loaded | **15 / 15** |
| “could not be successfully initialized” | **absent** |
| `EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION` / Critical error | **none** in 327 KB of log |
| Engine init | `Engine is initialized` — **21.95 s** |
| Map | `L_Drifter_Blockout`, 281 actors, All Saved |

The shutdown access violation disappeared along with it, settling the one open question from the diagnosis: it was downstream of the failed init, not an independent exit-path bug.

### What this does NOT prove
- **674/674 is unchanged and was not re-run.** The Automation suite was not executed this pass. An editor that launches is not a spec result and must never be reported as one.
- **The dialogue widgets have still never run.** No Widget Blueprint parented to `UCogheimDialogueWidget` exists, so `BindToDialogueComponent`, the caption timer, `SelectPlateByIndex`, and the choice-plate spawn path have never executed. Module load is proven; widget behaviour is not.

### The systemic finding
This is the **fourth** missing-entry-point incident: `CogheimNetcode`, then `SRF` and `Weather` (§35A landmine #1), now `CogheimDialogueUI`. The project already had two defenses and **both were bypassed rather than defeated**:

1. §38 std. #8 already carried the standing corollary "`IMPLEMENT_MODULE` always explicit."
2. `Deploy-CogheimAlpha.ps1` already runs a post-placement sanity check confirming each module has both a `Build.cs` and an entry point.

Per V37, `CogheimDialogueUI` was written directly into `Source/CogheimDialogueUI/` and hand-registered in the `.uproject`. It never went through the deploy script, so the check never ran on it. Writing the rule down a second time would not have helped — **the gap is that the only automated guard covers exactly one placement path.** Recorded as §38 std. #23.

### Audit note
Both files were re-verified after writing: byte-level readback from disk, non-ASCII and line-ending conformance against sibling files, an `IMPLEMENT_MODULE` census across all 15 modules, `PCHUsage` and dependency comparison against `CogheimLogin.Build.cs`, and confirmation that `CogheimDialogueUI` is a leaf module (no other `Build.cs` depends on it, nothing outside it includes its headers) so the change could not cascade.

**Status: content chain complete and verified in-editor. One Project Setting outstanding.**

Until this session §16C existed as design, code and a locked PSD — and **nothing else**. No textures, no material, no widget, no boot-flow assets. That is now closed.

### The chain, end to end

```
L_TitleScreen  ──(per-level GameMode Override)─▶  BP_CogheimTitleScreenGameMode
                                                        │ PlayerControllerClass
                                                        ▼

                                                        │ LoginWidgetClass

                                              └─ needle pivot (0.4970, 0.4793)
```

Every link was verified by reading the saved asset back, not by assuming the write succeeded.

### 1. Art export — 16 PNGs from the locked PSD

Exported by direct layer-raster extraction (`psd-tools`) from `Cogheim-Login-Sequence-Layered-v2-Identity-Fixed-1.psd`, **SHA-256 re-verified as `66e0e738…66c0c` at export time**. Not a flattened composite.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| File count vs export spec §2.2 | **16 / 16** |
| PSD structure vs lock | 7 groups, 38 layers, per-panel counts exact |
| Canvas | 1672×941 on every file, no resampling |
| Backgrounds | alpha 255–255 (opaque plates) |
| Glow / Steam / Needle | alpha 0–255 (real per-pixel transparency) |
| **Magenta sentinel** | **PASS — zero `#FF00FF` pixels in any file** |

Backgrounds came from `11 • ANIMATION CLEAN BASE`, **not** `10 • BACKGROUND`, so steam is not baked into the plate. Deliberately excluded: `00 • APPROVED ORIGINAL`, `10 • BACKGROUND`, both magenta previews, the magenta needle.

The magenta check matters because the export spec warns about it twice and the failure is silent — magenta layers have no alpha and composite wrong in UE. It is now **proven** clean rather than trusted.

Committed to `_ModuleDocs\CogheimLogin\Art\PNG-Login-1672x941\` with a manifest carrying every file's SHA-256, plus a UE import README. Folder layout matches the existing `CogheimDialogueUI\Art\` house pattern.

### 2. Textures and material

All 16 imported to `/Game/UI/Login/Textures` at export spec §2.6: Texture Group **UI**, Compression **UserInterface2D**, **no mips**, sRGB on. Verified 16/16 by reading the settings back off each asset.

> **Engine quirk, logged so nobody "fixes" it:** `UserInterface2D (RGBA)` is `TC_EDITOR_ICON` in the Python enum. That name is a long-standing UE wart, not a mistake.

`M_Login_Glow` built as a **UI-domain, translucent** material: `EmissiveMask` (TextureSampleParameter2D) × `Intensity` (scalar) → Final Color, and **alpha × Intensity → Opacity**.

> **The opacity wire is an addition, not in the spec.** Without it the glow plate renders as an opaque rectangle over the panel art. Recorded because it is a real design decision made during implementation, not a transcription of §2.3.

Both parameter names confirmed present on the saved material — `UCogheimLoginWidget` drives them **by name**, so a rename breaks the widget silently.

### 3. WBP_CogheimLogin

Parent `UCogheimLoginWidget`. Canvas Panel with `BackgroundImage`, `GlowImage`, `SteamImage`, `NeedleImage` (that draw order — needle above steam), all anchored full-stretch with zero offsets. `AccessibleErrorText` optional per Founder Lock #7.

`GlowBaseMaterial` = `M_Login_Glow`. **Do not hand-assign a Dynamic Material Instance to `GlowImage`'s brush** — `NativeConstruct` creates the MID from `GlowBaseMaterial` and calls `SetBrushFromMaterial` itself. Hand-assigning is wasted work and masks a null `GlowBaseMaterial` (no glow, no error).

| Identity | 02_Identity | 02_Identity | — | 0.0 | 1.00 |

Searching for a Blueprint to set `LoginWidgetClass` on returned only ThirdPerson template leftovers. **There was no PlayerController Blueprint, no GameMode Blueprint, and no title map.** V38 §35 records the boot/title flow as "authored, NOT compiled"; it compiles now, but it had never been instantiated as content, and that gap was invisible until something tried to use it.

### 5. Maps & Modes — what was found, and what was deliberately NOT changed

Read from `Config/DefaultEngine.ini`:

```ini
GameDefaultMap        = /Game/Maps/L_Drifter_Blockout
GlobalDefaultGameMode = .../BP_ThirdPersonGameMode_C
EditorStartupMap      = /Game/Maps/L_Drifter_Blockout
```

**Correcting an assumption made earlier the same day:** the *maps* were already pointed at the Drifter blockout, not the ThirdPerson template. Only the **GameMode** is still template.

- `GlobalDefaultGameMode` **left alone on purpose.** It is what gives `L_Drifter_Blockout` a playable character. Pointing it at the title GameMode would break blockout testing — hence the **per-level override** on `L_TitleScreen` instead.
- `EditorStartupMap` should **stay** `L_Drifter_Blockout` so the editor keeps opening where work happens.
- ⚠ **`GameDefaultMap` is still `L_Drifter_Blockout` — OPEN.** Set it to `L_TitleScreen` when a packaged build should boot into the title flow. Python could not persist `GameMapsSettings`, and hand-editing the ini behind a running editor risks a silent overwrite on exit. Change it in **Project Settings → Maps & Modes**, which makes the editor write the ini itself.

### 6. CogheimPromptPlateWidget — integrated, two defects fixed

From the Aug 25 title-screen handoff. Placed in `Source/CogheimAlpha/` and compiled.

1. `COGHEIMTITLESCREEN_API` → `COGHEIMALPHA_API`. **Confirmed on Shadow PC, not guessed:** there is no `CogheimTitleScreen` module; the title-screen code already lives in `CogheimAlpha`. That also means the handoff's premise is already satisfied — the V37 `C1083` was fixed before this session, because `CogheimAlpha.Build.cs` already lists `CogheimLogin`.
2. **`meta=(OptionalBindWidget)` → `meta=(BindWidgetOptional)` on five properties.** `OptionalBindWidget` **is not a real UMG meta specifier**. UMG matches `BindWidget` and `BindWidgetOptional` only. As authored those five art layers would have compiled clean and stayed permanently null — no error, ever. The house pattern is right in `CogheimDialogueWidget.h`.

The handoff also asks for `Slate`/`SlateCore` in the owning module. **Not needed** — `UMG` is already there and `CogheimBootScreenWidget` compiles in that module without them. Not added; unnecessary dependency.

### 7. Technical findings worth keeping

- **UMG widget trees are not reachable from Python in UE 5.8.** `dir(unreal.WidgetBlueprint)` filtered for tree/root/widget returns `[]`. Anchors, slots and Render Transform must be set in the Designer by hand. Confirmed by introspection, not inferred from a failure.
- **UE Python strips the type prefix**: `ELoginUIState` → `unreal.LoginUIState`, `FLoginStateVisual` → `unreal.LoginStateVisual`, `ACogheimTitleScreenPlayerController` → `unreal.CogheimTitleScreenPlayerController`.
- **`EditDefaultsOnly` struct members cannot be set via `set_editor_property` on a struct instance** ("cannot be edited on instances"). Use the keyword constructor — initialization is not subject to that check. CDO properties are fine either way.
- Blueprint parent class is **`get_blueprint_parent_class()`**. Not `parent_class`, not `get_super_class`.

## 35F. BOOT FLOW — TITLE/LOGIN PROTOTYPE VERIFIED IN PIE (27 Aug 2026)

**Status:** Working prototype, verified in Play-In-Editor on `L_TitleScreen`. **Not packaged, not tested outside the editor.** Alpha-critical. Source: `COGHEIM_BootFlow_Session_Aug27_2026.md`.

**What now works, verified in PIE:** `LGM ident (10s)` → `COGHEIM ident (15s)` → looping title plate + prompt → `WBP_CogheimLogin`. Space/Enter/gamepad-A advances the title plate; Escape skips idents. The `Content/Movies/` path assumption (files there are auto-copied into a packaged build; a `FileMediaSource` pointing outside `Content` plays in-editor and silently fails packaged) is reasoned but **unproven in a cooked build.**

**New C++ — `UCogheimVideoBootScreenWidget`** (`Source/CogheimAlpha/`): a boot-flow stage that *is* a full-screen video, replacing three hand-wired Blueprint video stages (identical full-screen-Image-bound-to-MediaTexture wiring, invisible to code review, verifiable only by dependency audit). Failure paths are deliberate — every failure still broadcasts `OnScreenReady` and logs the reason, so a missing asset costs one video, never a soft-locked boot.

**Findings worth keeping:** a `MediaPlayer` routes no audio on its own — needs a separate `UMediaSoundComponent`; a `MediaTexture` retains its last decoded frame across PIE sessions/editor restarts unless `AutoClear` is enabled (was producing a flash of the completed logo between idents); a C++-constructed `UTextBlock` needs an explicit font or renders nothing; `SetBrushFromTexture` takes `UTexture2D*` — a `UMediaTexture` (derives from `UTexture`, not `UTexture2D`) needs `SetResourceObject` with an explicit `ImageSize` instead.

**A process failure recorded rather than hidden:** two PIE runs were reported as verified when they weren't. `set_editor_property` on a Blueprint's CDO writes in memory only — `compile_blueprint()` is what commits it to disk, and a script had advised skipping the compile on a mistaken half-memory that compiling would discard the write. **The compounding error:** the verification step read the CDO back in the same session, which reads the same in-memory object just written — it passes whether or not anything persisted. **That is not verification.** New standard, §38 #25: a script that writes to an asset may not verify itself by reading its own write back; it must read something the write had to pass through (package dependencies on disk, the Details panel, or runtime behavior in PIE).

**Open items:** title loop video is muddy (source needs a better master, ideally 8K, not a settings fix); ident duration runs longer than expected, not yet diagnosed; brass prompt-cartouche needs a founder decision on WBP vs. native composition (recommend WBP — reusable for Login/MAXIOM); `GameDefaultMap` still points at `L_Drifter_Blockout` (packaged builds won't boot into the title flow until changed — see §35E); the whole flow is editor-only, packaged-build behavior unverified.

## 35G. DRIFTER Z-DOWN FOUNDER LOCK & HULL RE-PARAMETERIZATION SPEC (28 Aug 2026)

**Status:** Founder lock recorded + a complete, ready-to-apply mechanical change spec. **Not yet applied to `DrifterBlockoutGenerator.cpp`.** Source: `COGHEIM_Drifter_NegateZ_and_Reparam_Spec_Aug2026.md`. Closes the §35C open decision, carried as 🔴 in §36/§37 since V33.

**🔒 FOUNDER LOCK, Michael Einwechter, 28 Aug 2026 (Cowork session):** the Drifter's Z-down convention is fixed by **negating Z at the geometry-emission boundary**, not by `Roll = 180°` at spawn (the interim workaround §35C used to prove the blockout walks). **Technical convention lock only — no story canon affected; "Deck 1 = top" remains true in all deck data.** Rationale: SRF/RFA resolves authority, relevancy, and quantization in frame-local space, and a permanently rolled root actor makes every future frame-local computation (netcode, nav, physics, docking, child spawns) compose with a flipped basis forever. Negate-Z costs roughly one session now; Roll-180° costs nothing now and more every day after content is placed — and the blockout is generated/disposable, which is exactly what the pipeline is for.

**The mechanism:** a single `EmitZ()` helper (`return -LocalZDown`) applied exactly once at every site that computes a box-center Z for spawning — internal deck data stays top-down/canon; only geometry emission flips sign. Downstream, in the same session: remove `Roll = 180°` from `cogheim_spawn_drifter_blockout.py`; update any automation spec asserting Z-down relationships to the emitted convention (a spec that starts failing after this change is doing its job); apply the same flip in the Python mirror; reposition `PlayerStart` for the new orientation.

**Also unblocked — Hull Lock re-parameterization (Aug 24 §8):** the uniform 2400×1100cm plate is retired in favor of **six per-deck usable-interior footprints** (Deck 1 Wake-Up Bay 2090×1730cm through Deck 4 Cargo Hold 4000×2850cm, the largest), a **variable-height `SpineZ`** running sum replacing the uniform stride (bottom lands at 3490cm vs. the 3500cm hull — the same 10cm margin the Hull Lock already verified), a through-deck **lift shaft** at one fixed X/Y position on all six decks (the Cogworks S15 Lift_Collar dresses each landing), and a **stairwell zone** for the canon back-hull switchback staircase, also position-locked across decks. A `ValidateDoorwaysArePassable` check (long-flagged as missing — §37) is added at the same time, as the cheapest moment to do it.

**Acceptance criteria for the session that applies this (not yet run):** compiles with all Drifter automation specs updated to emitted coordinates (no weakened asserts); blockout spawned at **identity rotation**, no Roll anywhere, walked in-editor with a pasted `REPORT:` block (§35C's rule stands — not walked until that block is pasted and verified); six visibly distinct deck footprints matching the Hull Lock taper; lift shaft continuous through all six decks; `SpineZ` overflow guard (3490 ≤ 3500) in place; Python mirror parity re-run.

**Unchanged status, do not let this imply otherwise:** the no-parent-Actor gap remains open (`SpawnBlockoutInWorld` still bakes loose level actors with no host Actor for an SRF frame — fine for a static walkthrough, still the open seam for a *moving* Drifter, §35C). Cogworks kit placement into the blockout waits on this re-parameterization being applied.

## 35H. COGWORKS .BLEND → FBX → UNREAL PIPELINE (28 Aug 2026)

**Status:** Built and verified in-container (binary-level FBX parsing). **Final confirmation needs one Unreal import on the Shadow PC — not yet done.** Source: `COGHEIM_Cogworks_FBX_Pipeline_Aug2026.md`.

**Pipeline:** `cogworks_kit_gen.py` → `out/*.blend` (40 files, source, stays local) → `cogworks_fbx_export.py` → `fbx/*.fbx` (20 files, **LOW meshes only** — HIGH meshes are bake sources and never enter the Unreal project) → `fbx_verify.py` (binary FBX parser, proves correctness independent of any Blender re-import round-trip, which can't detect an absolute-unit error).

**Three things the export step adds that the `.blend` source doesn't have:**

1. **UCX collision, authored, not generated.** The Hull Lock already specifies `Generate Missing Collision` OFF, and Unreal's auto-box collision would make a doorway impassable and a window solid. Each LOW builder's own convex-box list *is* the collision, re-run un-joined and renamed `UCX_<meshname>_NN` — free and exact. **Two deliberate overrides:** S15 Lift_Collar collapses its ~40-bar lattice gate to kerb+head+one solid slab (**requires founder confirmation** — this assumes the safety gate is always closed; if it's ever meant to open, its collision belongs on a separate movable actor); S12 Railing uses one slab rather than per-baluster hulls.
2. **Floor-family 90° orientation baked at export**, not left to per-instance in-engine rotation — a whole class of placement bug removed at the source.
3. **Units — where this nearly went wrong.** The first export config silently produced the classic 100× Blender→Unreal scale bug (`UnitScaleFactor 100.0` with vertices already in metres — ambiguous). Caught by `fbx_verify.py` reading the binary file directly, not by a Blender re-import (which applies its own inverse conversion and can't see the absolute error). Corrected to `apply_scale_options='FBX_SCALE_NONE'` + `apply_unit_scale=True` + `global_scale=1.0`, matching the exact configuration already verified for the delivered Drifter hull FBX.

**Verified (all 20 files):** `UnitScaleFactor == 1.0`; uniform node scale (100,100,100); per-mesh render dimensions match the export manifest and the 20-part harness (measuring pooled-vertex dimensions without per-object translation gave five false failures before this was fixed); exactly one `SM_` render mesh per file; every `UCX_` hull name matches its render mesh; object counts match the manifest. **Not verified, and cannot be from a container:** the actual Unreal import — scale, orientation, and collision pickup should all be confirmed on the Shadow PC as the first real test.

**Still open:** the bake pass (HIGH → normal/AO/curvature onto LOW) is unwritten; lightmap UVs (a second UV channel) needed only if the project uses baked lighting anywhere — skippable under Lumen. These are Alpha blockout assets; nothing here is a final art pass.

## 35I. LANDSCAPE IMPORT — WORLD PARTITION TILING RULE (28–29 Aug 2026)

**Status:** Solved, verified by measurement. UE 5.8. Source: `COGHEIM_Landscape_Import_WorldPartition_Rule.md`, `COGHEIM_Meridian_Region_Session_Aug2026.md`.

**The rule, cost about one night to find:** a World Partition landscape must be **no wider than one region** (default 16 components), or the heightmap **tiles** — re-applied into every region, producing a repeating quilt. Region-width formula: `landscape verts = (section quads) × (sections per component) × 16 + 1`; for Section Size 255 / 1×1 sections, that number is **4081**.

**How the Meridian Region import failed and was diagnosed:** three separate configuration attempts (8129 verts/98.425 scale/16 components-per-region... at 32×32 total components — i.e., **two regions wide**; then 4033/198.413 two more ways) all repeated at a **constant 2000m**, regardless of resolution or file format (PNG vs. raw `.r16`) — a period constant in *metres*, not pixels, which can only happen if the tiled unit is the region, not the data. Ruled out by direct experiment, not guessing: the source file (re-verified 8129², matching the generator exactly), the PNG codec (raw `.r16` failed identically), the resolution, and World Partition Grid Size (2 and 8 proxies failed identically) were each individually eliminated. Corroborated by the disc heightmap importing perfectly into the **non-partitioned** `L_Drifter_Blockout`. The tool that actually cracked it was `measure_landscape_grid.py` — comparing proxy bounds against the source heightmap turned "it looks wrong" into "the period is exactly 2000m" in two attempts.

**Working import, re-done clean:** 4081×4081px heightmap, Section Size 255×255, 1×1 sections/component, 16×16 components (exactly one region), scale **196.078/196.078/400** (`8000m ÷ 4080 quads × 100` — do not round), location (0, 0, −3400). Verified post-import: 8000×8000m extent, height −60..569m (source −60.1..568.8), all five landscape layers bound, 2×2 proxy grid matching the source cell.

**Axis mapping — resolved.** §3A's Meridian session left this open (does the heightmap column map to world X or Y), with only a screenshot-based lean toward "swapped." **Now resolved by the same measurement pass: Unreal X follows the image column, Unreal Y follows the image row — no swap.** `world = (region_coord_m − 4000) × 100`. The Wingwater easter-egg butterfly basin lands at world (−3612, +662) — matching candidate "A," not the session's own tentative lean toward "B."

**Traps recorded for next time:** `Overall Resolution` is a derived field that silently recalculates when Section Size/Sections-Per-Component/Components change — always confirm it equals the heightmap's pixel dimensions immediately before importing; use `Fit To Data` rather than dictating numbers by hand; **World Partition Region Size 64 crashes the editor outright** (ran to 100%, created nothing, took the editor down — on restart, Skip Restore, the autosaves are the broken state); Grid Size clamps to a minimum of 1; deleting a WP landscape takes ~3 minutes and looks frozen; proxies unload constantly while building — untick **Enable Streaming** during work, re-enable before shipping.

**Unresolved, flagged rather than guessed at — §8A land:ocean ruling.** The Meridian disc weightmaps measure **87.45% land by dominant layer / 83.28% by weight-average** — against this Index's own §3A-locked figure of **81.3% land**. A prior handoff document's claim of "verified exactly 75.0%" does not hold against either measurement. Three different numbers now exist across three sources for the same locked canon figure. **Founder ruling required** before any of the three is treated as current; do not average them.

## 35J. LEVEL ARCHITECTURE — MANIFEST EXECUTED IN-ENGINE (29 Aug 2026)

> ### 🟢 V46 AMENDMENT — THE DRIFTER LEVEL FORK IS CLOSED. THERE IS ONE DRIFTER LEVEL.
>
> **Founder-locked 30 Aug 2026: `L_Drifter_Blockout` is deleted. `L_Drifter_V5` is the only Drifter level.** Verified from `Terrain\drifter_blockout_retired.txt` and the editor log — `.umap` present at 34.8 MB, **removed, 34.8 MB reclaimed, asset registry rescanned, FINAL on disk: False, in registry: False.**
>
> **Order of operations, all completed before the delete:** `Config\DefaultEngine.ini` — `GameDefaultMap` and `EditorStartupMap` both repointed to `/Game/Maps/L_Drifter_V5.L_Drifter_V5` (repoint **confirmed working**, the editor opened on it) · `cogheim_titlescreen_map.py` `RETURN_TO` repointed (a live value, not a comment) · `cogheim_create_levels.py` quarantine entry removed and the on-disk map inventory corrected · `cogheim_rename_levels.py` `MOVES` entry removed, docstring reduced from three moves to two · `cogheim_maps_and_modes.py` comments corrected · **asset registry referencer check: 0 referencers.** Nothing in C++ ever referenced the map.
>
> **What this supersedes in the sections below and in V45's open list:** "One Drifter level — **Two**" is **resolved, one** · the Level Manifest is back to **three** levels; the `L_Drifter_Blockout` row is void · *"deck extraction should almost certainly source from V5"* is **answered — V5 is the only source, there is nothing else to extract from** · *"which Drifter level is the deck-extraction source"* is **unblocked** · the level reorganisation is **two** renames/moves, not three — the Drifter entry was deleted rather than quarantined.
>
> **§38 std. #26 is unaffected and its evidence is unchanged** — the 2,800× file-size gap it cites was measured before deletion and remains the correct illustration. Worth noting the gap held to the end: **34.8 MB for 282 actors against 1.7 MB for 1,070.**
>
> #### 🔴 Why `delete_asset()` could not be trusted — new standard
>
> `EditorAssetLibrary.delete_asset()` returned **`True` twice** while the asset stayed in the registry and the `.umap` stayed on disk. `unload_packages()` also reported success and a forced `collect_garbage()` ran. **None of it moved the file.** The actual cause, found by probing rather than guessing again:
>
> ```
> PermissionError [WinError 32]: the process cannot access the file
> because it is being used by another process
> ```
>
> The running editor held an **OS file handle** on the package from having opened that map earlier in the session. **Releasing the `UPackage` does not release the handle, and no Python API can drop it. The handle dies only with the editor process.**
>
> **New §38 standard #30: a delete is proven by the file being gone, never by the return value of the call that deleted it.** Same class as `BUILD AND LOG VERIFICATION` — `delete_asset() == True` is *"it should have deleted"*, not a result. Check `os.path.exists` **and** `does_asset_exist` afterwards, and if either is still true, **say so.**
>
> **The working sequence, for the next asset that will not delete:** repoint every reference → verify 0 referencers → **restart the editor so it never opens the doomed asset** → delete on the fresh session.
>
> #### Still open from this closure
>
> - **`GameDefaultMap` → `L_TitleScreen`.** It is currently `L_Drifter_V5` **deliberately**, so nothing pointed at a deleted map. `cogheim_maps_and_modes.py` documents `L_TitleScreen` as the intended shipping boot map and **the script has still never been run.** One line, whenever the title flow is ready. **Requires founder approval.**
> - **`L_Drifter_V5` sits at `/Game/Maps/` root**, while `/Game/Maps/Striders/L_Strider_Drifter` already exists as a placeholder. V5 probably belongs there under the folder convention. **Requires founder approval** — it is a rename plus an ini repoint, not a free move.

**Status: structure created and independently verified on disk. Content gate not yet passed on any level.**

**The problem this closes.** Three levels existed and each answered one technical question. None was built inside a level *system*, so there was no rule saying where content lives, and content was accumulating in whichever map happened to be open. The measured evidence, read off disk before any change:

```
Content/Maps/L_Drifter_Blockout.umap    36,471,238 bytes   unpartitioned, 281 actors
Content/Maps/L_Meridian_Region.umap         13,074 bytes   World Partition + OFPA
Content/Maps/L_TitleScreen.umap              8,718 bytes
```

**A 2,800× file-size gap between two maps in the same project.** The Drifter blockout is 36 MB because its content is authored straight into the map. Meridian is 13 KB because World Partition and one-file-per-actor put its actors in `__ExternalActors__`. That gap *is* the architecture argument, expressed as a number.

### The governing rule

**A persistent level is a container, never a canvas.** A level may hold only: landscape actors and proxies, data layer definitions, Level Instance / Packed Level Actor references, a level bounds volume, its own sky/atmosphere/fog/post-process, and streaming-source or gameplay volumes. Anything else is content and belongs in a Level Instance. A loose static mesh in a persistent level is a defect, not a shortcut.

### The six-tier world model

| Tier | Name | UE level? | Authored or generated |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | The Disc Record — 738 locations in fractional coords, already `world_map_locations.json` / `CogheimLocationData.cpp` | No | Data only |
| 1 | Disc Terrain — streamed around **any** registered streaming source, ~95% of the disc | One host, `L_Disc_Runtime` | Generated at runtime |
| 2 | Anchor Regions — 8×8km World Partition levels, **one per fixed anchor** | Yes, one per anchor | Authored, PCG-assisted |
| 3 | Mobile Anchors, the Striders — instanced per live hull, local frame per SRF/RFA | Yes, one per class | Authored |
| 4 | Interiors and POI | No, Level Instances only | Authored |
| 5 | Frontend | Yes, standalone | Authored |

**The player is the streaming source. The Strider is a composite one.** Streaming radius and prefetch lead scale with velocity. World Partition streaming decides what is *loaded*; it never decides what is *replicated*. Gameplay relevancy is RFA.

World state the Syndicate Memory Engine or the Iron Veil changes **switches a runtime data layer or drives a component — it never requires a second copy of a map.** Colossus and Iron Veil events are therefore data-layer states on existing regions, not maps, which is why no event levels were created.

### The readiness gate — no content enters a level until all six pass

(a) path and name match the manifest · (b) WP state correct, region measures 8000×8000 m with 0 map-check errors · (c) level bounds volume exists · (d) data layers present and correctly typed · (e) sky/atmosphere/fog/post-process owned by this level · (f) **zero loose content actors**.

Meridian's gate (b) is already passed: 8.00 × 8.00 × 0.56 km, 0 map-check errors, verified after the tiling fix.

### 🔴 V45 CORRECTION — THE MANIFEST IS INCOMPLETE. A SECOND DRIFTER LEVEL EXISTS.

Level Manifest V1 was written against three levels. **There are four.** A parallel session built `L_Drifter_V5`:

| Level | Actors | PlayerStart | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| `L_Drifter_Blockout` | 273 | (650, 300, −430) | Untextured blockout, all validators pass, world Z −3310..0 |
| `L_Drifter_V5` | **1,070** (1,074 total) | (0, 0, −410) | **Fully textured and lit.** 32/32 textures, `M_Drifter` master + 10/10 `MI_Drifter_*`, zero unmaterialed actors, DirectionalLight / SkyAtmosphere / SkyLight / fog / cloud / PostProcessVolume, exposure locked at EV100 −3.5 |

**Both report world Z extent −3310..0**, so both are right-side up and the negate-Z convention is consistent across them.

**The unresolved question this creates, and it is a founder decision:** the Level Manifest says extract six deck Level Instances from `L_Drifter_Blockout`. **`L_Drifter_V5` is the better-looking, more advanced level and it is the one a player would walk.** Deck extraction should almost certainly source from V5, not the blockout — but the C++ generator still returns the old single-row layout, so **the code and the levels disagree**, deliberately and knowingly. Until the generator is ported to V5, extracting from either one bakes in a divergence.

**Related and open:** Deck 1 overflows its Hull-Lock envelope by **160 cm** (2250 needed against 2090 available, with Wingworks founder-locked at 8 × 6 m), and the single-row deck layout wastes **65–82% of each deck's depth**, reading as a narrow tower rather than a ship. Both need a founder ruling before deck extraction is worth doing.

**Recommendation: do not extract decks until the V5-versus-blockout source question is ruled.** This supersedes the Level Manifest's "highest value item" sequencing, because extracting from the wrong source is exactly the rebuild the whole architecture exists to prevent.

**Governing documents:** `COGHEIM_Level_Architecture_Whitepaper_V2.md` (supersedes V1, which is withdrawn) and `COGHEIM_Level_Manifest_V1.md` (**amended by this section**).

## 35K. UE 5.8 ENGINE KNOWLEDGE — AUGUST 2026 RESEARCH PASS

**Status: RESEARCH. No plugin enabled, nothing compiled, nothing tested in the Cogheim project.** Every consequence below is a recommendation or an open question. Full sourced detail in `COGHEIM_UE58_Engine_Knowledge_Aug2026.md`.

Epic's August 2026 learning roundup and the City Sample PCG release were read in full, including the Networked Physics Fundamentals tutorial, the Mesh Terrain documentation set, the Unreal MCP server guide, the Game Animation Sample 5.8 notes, the Accumulation DOF deep dive, the MetaSound granular tutorial, and the four City Sample PCG documentation pages.

### The four findings that matter to Cogheim

**1. Iris vs Replication Graph — an architectural fork in front of the RFA work.** Epic's networked-physics `UNetworkPhysicsComponent` has two data flows, chosen by which `CreateDataHistory` overload is called. The **Iris flow** (`FNetworkPhysicsPayload` + `TNetworkPhysicsInputState_Internal/_External`) is recommended. The **legacy flow** (`FNetworkPhysicsData` with traits) is "for RepGraph… new work should use the Iris-flow," and Epic states there will be **no official documentation** for it. `SRFReplicationNode` is a Replication Graph node. **🔴 V45 CORRECTION TO THE FRAMING ABOVE.** That "legacy flow" wording comes from Epic's *Networked Physics* tutorial and describes `UNetworkPhysicsComponent`'s `CreateDataHistory` data flow. **It is not about Replication Graph as an interest-management system.** Epic's *replication* documentation does not deprecate Replication Graph; it says *"Iris does not support replication graph… the network driver can only use one or the other"*, and Iris is **Experimental and not the default in 5.8**. Cogheim has no `UNetworkPhysicsComponent`, so it was never on the legacy path at all. V43 raised this as an architectural risk on wrong grounds. **CLOSED V44 by founder ruling, and now closed on correct grounds too: Cogheim does not run server-authoritative networked physics.** Iron Frames use the existing custom C++ locomotion model, Striders are kinematic movers, ragdolls are cosmetic and client-side only. **The Replication Graph stays; RFA and SRF are unaffected.** Reversible if a system ever genuinely requires Chaos networked physics — nothing does today. The rest of this section is retained as reference, not as a pending decision.

**2. 🔴 V45: THIS FINDING WAS OVERSTATED AND IS NOW LARGELY BUILT. Epic's own example of the problem networked physics exists to solve is a player jumping onto a moving platform** — the platform in the interpolated timeline, the player in the forward-predicted one, landing in different places on client and server. **That is Cogheim with 300 players aboard.** SRF (§5B) solved the *coordinate* half by moving interior characters into a stationary local frame. The **timeline** half is unaddressed in §5B and §5C as written and warrants its own section in the RFA spec.

> **V45 correction.** Overstated. SRF's local-frame design **structurally prevents** the moving-platform artifact for intra-hull movement — sliding on the deck or landing in the wrong place *cannot occur* — which is a stronger result than §5B ever claimed. The problem relocates to a small enumerable set of frame-boundary events: board, disembark, Strider-to-Strider transfer, firing outward, and objects leaving the hull. **Increment 3 built and verified those** as `USRFLibrary` (`HullPos3AtFrame`, `BoardAtFrame`, `DisembarkAtFrame`, `TransferAtFrame`) plus frame-stamped component wrappers, with the unstamped `BoardStrider` / `DisembarkStrider` pair **removed rather than deprecated alongside**, so the easy wrong call cannot be written. Second correction: without networked physics, Epic's Forward-Predicted / Server / Interpolated vocabulary does not apply. Cogheim's equivalent is the classic replication timing split. The *shape* of the problem and the SRF analysis hold; the *mechanism* is classic replication, not Chaos timelines.

**3. Physics Replication LOD is structurally parallel to RFA relevancy, and Epic shipped it.** Client-side, per-object selection of replication mode *and timeline* based on distance to a focal particle: Resimulation in the predicted timeline close in, Predictive Interpolation in the interpolated timeline far out, graded transition between. Experimental, off by default (`bEnablePhysicsReplicationLOD` / `p.ReplicationLOD.*`). Read it before hand-building more of the relevancy graph. Its complement, **Bubble Resimulation** (`p.Chaos.BubbleResim.Enable`), cuts the cost of each resim instead of the number of them.

**4. Mesh Terrain plus PCG is Epic's current world-building pipeline, not two unrelated experiments.** Mesh Terrain is not a heightfield — overhangs, tunnels, caves, sheer cliffs, and **variable resolution across one terrain**. Cogheim's registry holds 40 caves, 46 Strider ruins, 6 named abandoned ruins, 7 silos and 10 trenches, none of which a heightmap can express as terrain. The City Sample PCG demo runs *on* Mesh Terrain, and the PCG toolset ships a dedicated `Skill_PCGMeshPartition` for authoring graphs that write to it correctly. **Recommendation: do not adopt for Alpha — §35I's heightmap pipeline cost a night to earn and Meridian is verified clean — but schedule a real review at Beta planning rather than leaving it on a vague watch list.** If Mesh Terrain matures it does not amend the level architecture, it replaces a third of it.

### Epic's networked-physics law, adopted into the project instructions

Think in physics frames, never wall-clock. **Resimulation does not scale** — cost grows with RTT and resimulating body count, bounded by rewind history depth. Never read or write across Game Thread and Physics Thread; marshal through AsyncInput/AsyncOutput. Keep networked Input and State minimal but complete. **Design out instant actions** — prefer a commit window networked by `ServerFrame`, which is exactly the shape Cogheim's 3-player firing crew and Colossus dual-pilot co-sign already have. Keep logic deterministic. Anything affecting the sim that is not an Input or State goes through the Action system. **If a body's behaviour is a pure function of `ServerFrame` it needs no networking at all** — worth checking against Strider hull motion, which may be a deterministic function of a plotted course and the frame number, and therefore far cheaper to replicate than currently assumed. All interacting bodies share one replication mode.

Key defaults: Resim error thresholds 10 cm / 4° / 5 cm/s / 2°/s. Max Supported Latency Prediction 1000 ms, and note the trap — the rewind history is a `TCircularBuffer` that **rounds up to the next power of two**, so lowering it from 1000 ms to 600 ms at 30 Hz still allocates 32 frames. Debug with the Chaos Visual Debugger and `p.Net.DebugDraw.ShowRepMode 1`, `np2.Resim.DrawDebug 1`, `p.RenderInterp.DebugDraw 1`.

### City Sample PCG — the largest content cost in the project has a tool now

Epic rebuilt the City Sample with **no Houdini**: an entire 1 km city from **18 interdependent PCG graphs** run in a fixed order, fully parametric and infinitely regenerable, on procedurally shaped Mesh Terrain. Author-driven splines shape terrain, city boundary, arteries, highways and high-rise zones. Buildings are selected by **style code** with tooltips describing look, type and construction era — which is precisely the mechanism an "accumulated, repaired, generations-deep" steampunk-Gothic city needs, since that is a style-mix problem best expressed as a rule. **Shape Grammar Definitions** arrange modular meshes along splines by grammar string, which is the consuming system the Cogworks kit generator (§33A) already produces the right input for.

**Two traps that become project rules.** PCG actors are **unpartitioned by default and incompatible with streaming** until every graph is explicitly partitioned — Partition Grid Size defaults to 128 m, collision generation for arbitrary building shapes is a **separate manual checkbox**, and a finished-looking unpartitioned PCG region simply will not stream. And graph chains have hard ordering dependencies: encode the order in graph names and document the rebuild cascade, because changing an early graph invalidates everything after it.

### Also logged

**Game Animation Sample 5.8** — the **Physics Control Component** (drive a physics asset through linear/angular motors) and the powered-ragdoll `SandboxCharacter_Mover_Ragdoll` are the most direct fit yet for **Iron Frame exoskeletons**, and **Pose Search Interaction Assets** (multi-character animation with motion-warp handling) map onto two locked cooperation systems: the 3-player weapon crew and the Colossus dual-pilot requirement. **Accumulation DOF** — a trailer/marketing tool (§34), correct DOF through translucency, parallax, fur and grass, at a render cost of `NumSamples × CA Bands × Temporal Samples`. **MetaSound adaptive granular synthesis** — a strong fit for a Strider's mechanical voice changing with speed, load, damage and Overdrive (§16B). **Fortnite's 68% shader reduction talk** — unwatched, but directly relevant to a modular kit game with a livery system, which is exactly the shape that produces permutation bloat; watch it before the master material set is locked.

**Unreal MCP server (experimental, 5.8).** Hosts an MCP server inside the editor process (`ModelContextProtocol.StartServer`, default port 8000), with an All Toolsets plugin exposing Editor, GAS, Niagara and Physics tools, and per-client config generation. It would let a session read live editor and level state. **It does not replace the build-log rules** — reading a live editor does not prove a build succeeded — and the tutorial's `--dangerously-skip-permissions` flag is a real security decision, not a setup step to copy. Worth a dedicated evaluation session; whether a cloud session can reach it through the device bridge is **unverified**.

## 35L. DRIFTER V5 C++ PORT — THE CODE/LEVEL FORK IS CLOSED (30 Aug 2026)

**Status: compiled and linked, and the spec suite ran. Not verified in PIE.**

`UDrifterBlockoutGenerator::GenerateAllBoxes()` now returns the V5 layout, so `SpawnBlockoutInWorld` and every other caller build **the ship that exists.** With `L_Drifter_Blockout` deleted, the code had become the *only* thing describing the superseded single-row layout — the disagreement was no longer between two levels but between the code and the one level that remained.

**Result, read from `Saved\Logs\CogheimAlpha.log`, automation run 30 Aug 15:30:41 — suite total 804 Success / 4 Fail.** `Cogheim.Drifter.Systems.TestDrifterBlockoutV5`: **12 tests, 11 passed, 1 failed.**

**The load-bearing result is `test_per_deck_box_counts_match_the_generator` passing:** the C++ port independently produces **94 / 150 / 200 / 218 / 208 / 200 = 1,070** boxes, matching `drifter_blockout_v5.py` deck for deck and matching the 1,070 StaticMeshActors then in `L_Drifter_V5`. *(All of these counts were subsequently superseded by the envelope rewrite — see §11A.E. The port's correctness is what this entry records.)*

**What changed.** New: `Source/CogheimDrifter/DrifterBlockoutV5.h/.cpp` — `UDrifterBlockoutV5`, mirroring `drifter_blockout_v5.py` **1:1 by name** per house convention. It **generates** the layout; it does not read the JSON. Changed: `GenerateAllBoxes()` delegates to `UDrifterBlockoutV5::GenerateAll()` — **that one line is the fix.** Deliberately unchanged: `GenerateZoneBoxes`, `GenerateCorridorBoxes`, `GenerateLiftSafetyBoxes` still describe the single-row model and are **commented as retained by decision rather than neglect**, until they are ported or retired. **🔴 Porting or retiring the eight legacy validators requires founder approval.**

**The seam that made this a focused change:** only **two** validators consume `GenerateAllBoxes()` and both are model-agnostic; the other eight call the per-zone functions directly and are untouched. **Zero pre-existing spec tests needed rewriting.**

**Python semantics mirrored deliberately, each commented at the site:** `StableSort` for largest-zone-first ordering (Python's `sorted` is stable, so equal-area zones keep declaration order) · **exact-equality** dedup on candidate offsets (Python builds them in a `set`; near-equality dedup would silently drop a distinct candidate and move a room) · lexicographic `(-edge, -spread)` comparison replicating Python's tuple compare.

### 35L.1 The failing test was wrong and the port was exact — a new standard

```
Expected 'Lowest centre' to be -3301.000000, but it was -3301.129883
```

`drifter_blockout_v5.py` prints its Z extent with `"%.0f"`, so it **reports** `−3301`. The real value in its own JSON is **`−3301.13`**. That printed figure went into the assertion as if it were the value, and **a correct port failed against it by 0.13 cm.**

> **A number formatted for a human to read is not a number to assert against.** Same family as `delete_asset() == True` and "it should compile" — a display artefact mistaken for a result. (§38 std. #31.)

Fixed to `−3301.13f` with a `0.01f` tolerance; two decimals is stable because `MakeBox` rounds every emitted coordinate to 2 dp, exactly as the Python does.

### 35L.2 🔴 The other three failures were a self-inflicted regression, and the first account of it was wrong

The initial claim was that three failures pre-dated the port and belonged to the RFA session. **The pre-port automation CSV settles it: all three passed before the port.**

**Root cause.** `DrifterSystemsSpec.cpp` was **staged into the session workspace early**, three fixes landed on the Shadow PC afterwards, and the stale copy was then edited and committed **whole**, reverting three green tests to their pre-negate-Z form. The symptoms then diagnosed as "stale sign expectations" were real — they had just been reintroduced.

**The tell that was read past:** the passing test is named `test_floor_sits_below_ceiling_in_world_z_terms`. The failing one is `..._in_spine_z_terms` — **a different, older test.** A test that changes name is not the same test.

**What actually protects against this, in order:** ① **re-stage immediately before editing a shared file** — a staged copy is a point-in-time snapshot and this one was hours old; ② **pass `expectedMtimeMs` on every commit to a shared file** — it would have refused the write outright; ③ a renamed test is a different test.

**⚠ Both the Drifter and RFA sessions edit `DrifterSystemsSpec.cpp`. Re-stage before editing and pass `expectedMtimeMs`, from either session.**

Repaired against figures measured before the regression (`Terrain\drifter_spec_verify.txt`) and committed under an mtime guard.

## 35M. EXTERNAL ART BUILDER QA — THE GATE THAT WAS WRONG THREE TIMES (30–31 Aug 2026)

Five deliveries from an external asset builder produced a genuinely reusable process result, and the most useful part of it is a sequence of **our own specification failures**, recorded so the pattern is not repeated on the Grindwalker.

### 35M.1 The standing protocol

1. **Parse the shipped files directly. Read their QA sheet LAST**, and never make it the basis of a figure. This stays permanent even now that their reporting has become accurate.
2. **A checksum proves the bytes travelled intact. It does not prove they were ever complete.** v1 shipped two PNGs with **no terminal IEND chunk**, and `MANIFEST.sha256` verified **50 of 50 OK** because it hashed the truncated bytes, while `PACKAGE_VALIDATION.json` reported `"result": "PASS"` without opening an image. **Every package gets an image-decode gate.**
3. **A self-reported diff is worthless without verifying the baseline.** When v5 shipped a `QA_Baseline/V4/` folder, **their baseline was checked byte-for-byte against our own v4 copy before anything computed from it was trusted.**
4. **Verify the reachability claim with an independent BFS**, not by reading their number.
5. **A measurement taken from a corrupt file is not a measurement.** v1's Brass ORM was read as "correct at 0–219" — **from a truncated file force-loaded past its missing IEND**, and reported as a clean result. It was never a valid read.

### 35M.2 🔴 The metallic-mask gate, wrong three times in three different costumes

**The problem being specified:** metallic near 1 deletes the diffuse term, so patched, painted and oxidised surfaces at a constant 0.78–0.90 read as raw metal and go black in shadow. **This is the same physical error that cost a full session on the Drifter interior.** Paint, rust, patina and oxide are **dielectrics**; only bare unpainted metal is metallic 1.

| Gate | As written | How it was satisfied without being met |
|---|---|---|
| **v2** | `min < 30` somewhere in the ORM blue channel | **One dark pixel.** A minimum measures one sample, not a distribution. It also named only two of four maps, excluding Brass on the strength of the corrupt-file reading above |
| **v3** | Histogram distributed: no 32-wide bucket over 70%, ≥5% below 0.1, ≥5% above 0.8 | **Uniform noise.** Iron and Brass came back with **identical distributions to three decimal places across all eight buckets** — the same template stamped onto two different materials. Brass correlated with its own albedo at **r = −0.108**: patina where the albedo has none, bare metal where the albedo shows verdigris |
| **v4** | The above **plus Pearson r ≥ 0.35** | **The 5%-below-0.1 floor was tuned on one material and applied to all.** Brass railings genuinely *are* mostly bare metal, so a physically correct brass mask fails a rule that manufactures patina that is not there |

**The pattern named plainly: three times, a *summary statistic* of the mask was measured instead of its *relationship to the thing it masks*.**

> **🔒 The corrected rule, and the one the Grindwalker prompt now carries.** *Metallic is a valid mask when it is **correlated**, not merely distributed. Require **|Pearson r| ≥ 0.35** between the metallic channel and basecolor luminance, computed at 256 × 256, **and** no single 32-wide histogram bucket above **70%**. **There is no fixed dielectric floor.** Timber and Canvas are exempt as correctly all-dielectric.* Reference distributions: Iron v3 **+0.907**, TreadPlate **+0.63**, Timber **+0.63**, Brass v5 **−0.492** (inverse and correct — bright verdigris reads dielectric, darker bare brass reads metal).

**This also belongs in `surf/process.py` as a permanent assertion, not only in a builder brief.**

### 35M.3 Two script lessons from the same work

**A guard band fitted to a snapshot is not a guard.** `build_drifter_shell_collision.py`'s Z band fired on v3 because `zmin` **legitimately** moved −2038.8 → −2208.8 when the boarding ramp was deployed to the ground. The band had been fitted to v2's numbers. **Rederived from physics instead — nothing may sit below the model's ground plane at −2454.8 cm** — and verified against both v2 and v3 data.

**Annotate the constant that has already been misread once.** `import_drifter_shell.py` now annotates the hull constants so the **deployed ramp is never mistaken for beam.**

**Legend:** ✅ Done | 🟡 In Progress | ⬜ Not Started | 🔒 Founder Lock Required | **🔗** = has a source chat, linked for reference | **⚙️** = flagged for C# → UE5.8 C++ port | 🆕 = gap not on the original systems-only checklist

**🔒 V41 — how to read the phases below, post-directive.** Michael's Aug 29, 2026 directive (banner, §1) moves the target from "validate Alpha, then decide" to "build the full game now." The Phase 0/1/2/3 split below stays as-is — it's still the right way to sequence dependencies (you can't crew Strider Combat before Strider Combat is coded; you can't load-test docking before RFA Pass 2b exists) — but it is **no longer a gate that holds Phase 2/3 work until Phase 1 is formally "done."** Work on any phase's systems is authorized now; the phase number is prioritization guidance, not a fence. **What doesn't change:** classify every new or resumed system as Alpha-critical / Alpha-supporting / Post-Alpha / Full-production / Live-service expansion / Research experiment / Postpone-or-reject per the project's own system-design requirements, and keep proving the still-unvalidated core risks (M2 concurrency/load, §1; RFA Pass 2b+, §5C; multi-Strider docking, §17A §7.2) early rather than last, since Phase 2/3 systems (full Strider Combat crewing, the Docked Exchange, multi-class interiors) all sit on top of them.

### PHASE 1 — ALPHA (Playable Vertical Slice) — HIGHEST PRIORITY, NO LONGER THE ONLY ACTIVE PHASE (V41)

### 📋 §37B. DEFECTS TO CORRECT — DOCUMENTATION ERRORS, NOT DECISIONS

Found 4 September 2026. Those that could be corrected mechanically have been, in place, and are marked. The rest need a judgment.

1. **The ledger count does not close.** §24 lists **32** names against a locked **33**. Penny Truex and Rade Voss-Iron have both been argued as the missing 33rd on the same arithmetic. **Both cannot be right.**
2. **Rade Voss-Iron is the payoff of Act 3 Quest 3.4**, the single place the EntityAttentionThread pays off. **This materially favours LIVING** and was not known when he was proposed as the missing slot. **Casting him anywhere would destroy authored content.**
3. **Maria, Danielle and Kim have no §23 rows** despite being on the locked Drifter roster and carrying 11 quests each.
4. **Petra Voss-Iron's role cell is blank.**
5. **Four of the six historical figures are unnamed**, against 35 allocated quests.
6. **Kris Brickman's quest count is unreconciled.** The ledger says 11; the tiered chain implies more.
7. **Oasis Hint 6 deck number** is given as **8** in one place and **9** in another, and one of those says **"Bastion"**, which is retired.
8. **Tiffany contradiction.** §22 says "Not a tutorial NPC — a person" while §11A casts her as the tutorial's roaming first contact.
9. **Three Drifter roster names flagged post-Drifter** — Maria, Danielle, Kim — contradicting the locked timeline rule. **Resolve from the novels, not by convenience.**
10. ✅ **Nine Greeter names retire to §32**, keeping Teddy Quickmatch. Recorded at §12B-7. "Adelaide" appears in two parallel civic rosters with different surnames; retiring the Greeter set leaves **Adelaide Iron-Vow** untouched.
11. ✅ **The Grindwalker Iron Table crew list was stale** where it named Gina as First Officer. **Corrected in place at §4B**; that station now needs a new occupant. Danielle at Navigation, Joel at Tactical, Astin at Sensor, Louis at Comms are unaffected.
12. ✅ **`OPS-11` evidence was false.** `Deploy-CogheimAlpha.ps1` does not exist. **Corrected in place at §0.** The JSON was corrected on 4 September.
13. ⚠ **The project instructions are stale** where they say "MASTER INDEX V47 IS THE ONLY SURVIVING VERSION." **This cannot be fixed from inside this document.** V48 existed, this document supersedes it, and V46 still awaits purge. **The instructions need a founder edit.**

**🔴 NEW, V43 — REQUIRES FOUNDER APPROVAL, HIGHEST PRIORITY OF THIS PASS:** amend the **§5C shared-root scaling paragraph**, which claims the shared world octree "only ever holds a few thousand hulls — never 150k characters." Off-hull play is a supported playstyle (traversal ruling 1, §3C) and was already locked design in three places, so the root will hold characters. True for Alpha, not true at target scale, and **the sentence has appeared in fundraising-adjacent material.**

### 🔴 OPEN, V47 — created by the founder decisions of 31 August 2026

**Canon and content**

1. ✅ **CLOSED 4 Sep 2026. May a monument inscription state or imply the network's purpose? NO.** Never state or imply purpose. Names, warnings, dedications, measurements and fragments only. No origin, no network purpose, no makers' identity, at any site, ever. Governs every inscription authored from here on (§21, §37A).
2. ✅ **CLOSED 4 Sep 2026. Einwechtëran scope: ~40 glyphs, ~150 words**, consistent grammar, fluency in a few hours. Not a cipher, and not a large loose vocabulary. Scopes `NAR-10`, `NAR-11`, `SYS-24`, `OPS-17` (§21, §37A).
3. **The narrowed publish line — recommended, not approved.** Publishable: the language, its glyphs, its script, that it is learnable in-game. Not publishable: Harmonics mechanics, Ring, the audio-degradation design, the grammar's mechanical effects (§26D).
4. **The 5th glyph and Hollow Crown Stage 5 glyph identities remain SEALED.** Unchanged by the unsealing, and deliberately so (§21).
5. **The ten §26D Harmonics decisions are now on the critical path** rather than parked behind a trigger. The load-bearing one is sharper, not softer: **whether Book V's treatment must be written before the game locks the Harmonics grammar** — because the game will now teach the language publicly while the book that explains it is unpublished.

**Sequencing**

6. **Design-heavy / engine-light for Harmonics until the vertical slice closes** — recommended, not approved. Review at the slice gate (§26D, `SYS-25` / `SYS-26`).

It is an accessibility-quality requirement, **not a secrecy one**, it still needs a non-public route, and unparking Harmonics makes it **more** urgent, not less.

**Business**

4. **The disc's origin convention is an inference** — texel (1008, 1008) as centre with a 1250 km radius. Consistent with the carve script, **not written down anywhere. Requires validation before it ships in a sampler.**
5. **🔴 `rfa_hull_conformance_mirror.py` asserts the retired beam.** Constants 420/100/220 → **330/90/150**; checks G15 (2.26× → 2.88×), G16 (the beam sum) and G17 (±160 m → **±120 m**) all carry old literals. **Not a decision — an execution item that must happen before Stage A goes to C++**, together with re-deriving the roll limits. §3E-3.
6. **🔴 Two disc sample-spacing conventions are in circulation** — `2,500,000 / 2016 = 1240.079` in the mirror versus `2,500,000 / 2017 = 1239.4646` from the carve script. **0.6 m per texel, and it propagates through every disc↔region conversion. Settle one convention before anything is baked.** §3E-3.

### 37A-1. CHARACTERS PER ACCOUNT — OVERTURNED WITHIN THE SAME DAY

> ### OVERTURNED 4 SEPTEMBER 2026 — THREE CHARACTERS PER ACCOUNT
> **Overturns:** `COGHEIM_Founder_Decisions_Sep4_2026.md` C2, which locked **one per account** at approximately 17:53.
> **Replaced by:** `COGHEIM_CharacterCreation_Workflow_Spec_V3.md` §4, which locked **three per account** at approximately 13:07. **V3 wins.**
> **Why:** V3 moves Syndicate binding, the Account Hold shared storage, per account docket caps and alt visibility to the **account** level, which defends the political design without removing player variety.
> **The cause is recorded because it is a process failure and not just a number:** neither session knew the other existed, and the collision was on no open list. See standard #41.

### 37A-2. THE SCHALLÛSAN SWEEP — REQUIRED, AND NOT MECHANICAL

**Sael-Vori** are the builders, three thousand years ago, in the western corridor. **Schallûsan** is the **later line** from the same region. Every Cogheim document currently uses Schallûsan for the ancient builders. That is wrong.

§4.2b is answered and this is what unblocks the sweep: **the word Einwechtëran names the ancient language AND what the lineages become, and why those share a name is the Book V reveal.** The game keeps using it without ever explaining it, which is what it already does. No game side change is needed.

**Welded and immovable: 220** appears eighteen times across Books IV and V and the generational math depends on it. **Never adjust, round, or restate as 250.**

| Target | Finding |
|---|---|
| **This Index** | **13 occurrences of "Schallûsan" remain.** §3 twice, §11 "Schallus Rotunda", §17A, §20, §22 twice, §23 Christine's title, §26E-2. Was not on the §4.2 target list and is added here |
| `canon_lock_v1.json` | Named as a target but **contains neither word.** The correct action is an ADDITION, not a replacement |
| "the universe canon note" | Named as a target. **Not a document in project knowledge under that name** |
| Inscription corpus | **Zero authored.** `NAR-10` and `NAR-11` both `not_started` |
| Shipped terrain | **One** placeholder glyph in `meridian_finish_v2.py`. Swap `STROKES` and `RING`. **Do not re-carve Meridian** |

> **⚠ THE SWEEP IS DELIBERATELY NOT APPLIED IN THIS PASS, AND THAT IS THE RULING.** Each occurrence needs a judgment about whether it means the ancient builders or the later line, and a blanket replace would get some of them wrong. **A naive find and replace also destroys two real people tributes:** "Schallus" without the circumflex is a surname on 9 lines, **Rita Marie Schallus** and **John Eric Schallus**. Match on **"Schallûsan" with the circumflex only**, and hand check every hit. This is a founder led pass, not a script.
