## 38. SESSION WORKFLOW STANDARDS

**Kaelic Can operates under these rules every session:**

> ### ➕ ADDED 4 AND 5 SEPTEMBER 2026
>
> **Numbering note.** The change set drafted these as #30 to #32. Those numbers were already taken by the standards below. They are recorded here as **#40 to #43** so nothing is overwritten.
>
> **#40. THE SAME DAY RE-READ.** **Before locking any decision in session**, call `project_info`, sort by creation time, and read anything created today. A ruling made in conversation carries the same re-read obligation as an Index pass. **A same day document is the most likely thing to contradict you and the least likely thing you have read.** *Written because it was broken on the day it was written: the one per account and three per account locks were set four hours apart by two sessions that never saw each other.*
>
> **#41. ONE SUBJECT, ONE OWNER.** Every subject belongs to exactly one session, per `COGHEIM_Session_Architecture_V1_Sep4_2026.md`. **If two sessions both believe a subject is theirs, that is a defect to report, not a thing to work around.**
>
> **#42. EVERY SESSION ENDS WITH A HANDOFF.** No session closes without stating what it decided, what it built with evidence, what is still open and who owns it, what the next session must read, and any contradiction it found and did not resolve. **A session that ends without a handoff has produced nothing durable.**
>
> **#43. ENGINE LAW GOVERNS FACTS, NEVER AMBITION.** (appended to the Engine Law at founder direction, 5 September 2026)
>
> > **This law governs facts, never ambition.** It forbids inventing a symbol that does not exist. It does not forbid, and must never be used to discourage, building a system Unreal was not designed for. Cogheim's netcode already does exactly that. **Bending a system requires knowing it exactly, because you cannot subvert an API you had to imagine.**
>
> **Why it was added, recorded because the reasoning generalises.** The founder read the Engine Law as a constraint on pioneering. It is not: it constrains fabrication. A plausible sounding API is not a bold engineering choice, it is a sentence that reads perfectly, compiles in the reader's head and fails on the machine an hour later. Nothing in the law touches what Cogheim may be. The moving city, the three instrument optical family, the skating exoskeleton, the removal of the on screen navigation marker and the single competing slot budget were all designed under it. **But the failure mode the founder identified is real.** A session can hide behind the law, decline to propose an approach because it has not seen it in a tutorial, and call that discipline. The clause above exists to make that reading impossible.
>
> **Authorship, recorded for the trail:** the Engine Law was drafted by a session on 29 August 2026 at the founder's request and installed by the founder into the project instructions. This clause was directed by the founder on 5 September 2026.

> ### ➕ ADDED IN V48
>
> **#29A. THE NEWER-DOCUMENT SWEEP IS A GATE, NOT AN INTENTION.** Std. #29 has now failed three times — V43, V44 and V47 were each written without reading every document newer than the Index being superseded. **The standard is fine; executing it under time pressure is what fails.** So it becomes mechanical: **before writing a new Index, call `project_info`, sort by creation time, list every document newer than the version being superseded and every document whose name contains "Correction", and write that list into the new Index's front matter as documents read. An Index that cannot name its own read list has not done the sweep.**
>
> **#29B. A CLAIM ABOUT A FILE'S EXISTENCE IS VERIFIED, NEVER ASSERTED.** V47 opened by stating every prior Index had been purged. V46 was still there. **A delete is proven by the file being gone.** Any statement in an Index about what has been deleted, uploaded, deployed or purged is checked against a listing in the same session that writes it, or it is written as an intention with the word "should".
>
> **#29C. A DOCUMENT TITLED "LOCKED" LOCKS ONLY WHAT IT SAYS IT LOCKS.** Both 2 September silhouette documents are founder-approved as *shape* and carry numbers that are derived, proposed, or contradicted by other approved documents. **Read the status column, not the filename.** Where a locked document's numbers descend from a retired figure, every number in it is suspect until re-derived (§4D-4).
>
> **#39. NOT EVERY FILE GOES INTO PROJECT KNOWLEDGE. THE KNOWLEDGE BASE IS A BUDGET, NOT A DRIVE.** (added V48, at founder direction, 3 September 2026)
>
> Project knowledge has a hard cap of roughly **2.0 MB** and it is close to full. It has already refused two uploads: the approved specialty badge board (§33B) and the Crossing storyboard plate. **There is no known way to expand it.** So storage is now a design constraint on how this project keeps records, and it is governed by the rule below rather than by whoever happens to be writing that day.
>
> **What belongs in project knowledge, and nothing else does:**
>
> | Keep | Why |
> |---|---|
> | **The current Master Index, one copy only** | The continuity mechanism. Every superseded version is deleted once its successor is confirmed good |
> | **Founder decision documents** | The record of what was ruled and when. Individually tiny |
> | **Session and handoff documents** | With no earlier Index to diff against, these are the only evidence trail (std. #38) |
> | **`cogheim_production_status.json` and `render_production_status.py`** | §0's source. Small, and §37 std. #37 forbids hand-editing the rendered output |
> | **The terrain generator scripts** | Architecture ruling D7: they **are** the world (§3F-1). Text, and small |
> | **`COGHEIM_canon_lock_v1.json`** | Machine-readable canon |
>
> **What does NOT belong in project knowledge:**
>
> Generated images, boards, plates and renders · concept art of any kind · superseded Master Indexes · superseded drafts of a document that has a current version · prompt packs whose content is already folded into an Index section · anything reproducible from a script and a seed · anything a session produced as scratch on the way to a deliverable.
>
> **Where those go instead: the Google Drive `Cogheim` folder**, which exists, is writable, and is outside the cap.
>
> **Three working rules that follow from this:**
>
> 1. **🔴 Write the Index to Drive on every version, before purging the previous one from project knowledge.** Std. #38 already requires a copy outside project knowledge and it has still not been done. **The purge and the backup are one action in one sitting, never two actions on two days.** A purge performed without the backup in hand is how eight months of canon disappears.
> 2. **A document folded into the Index does not need to stay a separate file.** Once a session document's content is inside an Index section and cited by name, the file is a record rather than a reference. Records can live in Drive. **The test is whether a future session would need to open it: if the Index carries the substance, it would not.**
> 3. **When an upload is refused, say so in the document that needed it and name the file.** §33B does this for the badge board. **A missing asset that nobody recorded as missing is indistinguishable from an asset nobody made.**
>
> **What must never be purged to save space:** the founder decision trail, the session documents, the status JSON and its renderer, and the generator scripts. **The indexes and the images are the expensive files. The records are not**, and deleting records to make room for an image is a trade this project should never make.



1. **Verify canon first** → check project knowledge and prior session memory before proposing anything
2. **Rival Test every spec** → all three §2A questions answered in the spec header before Boom reviews; me-too features flagged, never presented as innovations
3. **Spec before code** → full written spec locked by Boom before any code is written (UE5.8 C++ per §2B)
4. **One major system per session block** → then code sprint across all locked specs
5. **Master Index handoff** → updated Master Index produced at end of each session — **in practice, this happens roughly daily**, so the Master Index is the primary continuity mechanism between sessions, not chat memory
6. **Trust but verify "complete" markers** (added July 13, 2026, after the Iron Frame incident; extended July 14 after the Iron Table incident) → a prior session's "port complete" or "fully coded" status in this Index is a claim, not a guarantee. Before building on top of a marked-complete system, confirm the actual source exists in the current session's accessible files. The Iron Frame system was marked complete in V21 but the source didn't actually exist in that session's sandbox — it required a full rebuild, not a port. Conversely, **before declaring something new canon, search broadly enough to catch prior work that project knowledge alone may have missed** — the Iron Table was incorrectly treated as brand-new when it was actually the already-built War Table from a July 11 session not covered by the project knowledge search at the time. The founder caught this by sharing a direct conversation link. Both directions of this error cost real time; the fix in both cases is the same: search harder before assuming either completeness or absence.
7. **Merge from the actual source file, not a summary of it** (added July 14, 2026, after the checklist merge correction) → when the person references an existing document (a checklist, a spec, a prior deliverable) that lives in another chat or an upload, treat a conversation summary of that document as a lead to verify, not as the content itself. The V25 checklist merge was built from session summaries and silently dropped real detail — specific Phase 0 sub-items, an important design-reconciliation note, and four unresolved Open Founder Decisions — even though the summary read as complete. The fix: when the actual source file is available (uploaded, or fetchable), read it directly before merging or reporting on it.

8. **"Simpler isn't always better"** (added August 2026, after the Wingworks session) → never recommend an approach because it is easiest or most familiar. **Verify against the existing house pattern first** — read the actual deploy script, the actual sibling `Build.cs`, the actual zip structure. In the Wingworks session five first-pass assumptions were wrong and all were caught only by inspecting real patch files. Standing corollaries: `FDefaultModuleImpl` not `FDefaultGameModuleImpl`; `COGHEIM<MODULENAME>_API` macro naming; **nested `Public/Private` zip structure for UE modules**; `IMPLEMENT_MODULE` always explicit.

9. **Cross-check vehicle and system references against the current Index section before treating them as live** (added V32) → the "Grindwalker aerial" Alpha vehicle line survived four Index versions after §13C superseded it.

10. **Read the real file before proposing a fix, and verify every edit actually landed** (added V32, from the consolidation build) → during the build pass **two edits silently failed** and were only caught by an explicit verification step. Every scripted rename or patch gets a `Select-String` readback before the next build.

11. **PowerShell scripts are ASCII-only with a BOM** (added V32) → a UTF-8 BOM plus an em dash caused a parse failure under Windows PowerShell 5.1. No typographic dashes in script bodies.

12. **Locks can be reopened deliberately and visibly** (added V32, from the Wingworks footprint) → the founder overturned the "Axiom's Chamber and Boiler Well are the two largest rooms" lock to fit the 8×6m Wingworks. Changing a lock is legitimate; changing one *silently* is not. Record the overturn.

13. **Never conflate logic-mirror counts with in-engine test results** (added V32) → prior Index versions summed Python-mirror checks and reported them as a project-wide test total. Those are design verification, not evidence a build works. Keep the two numbers separate, especially in anything investor-facing.

14. **`Error`-severity logging fails Automation tests** (added V33) → UE's Automation framework captures `UE_LOG(..., Error, ...)` and **fails the enclosing test even when every assertion passes**. Three of the ten failures were misdiagnosed as inverted assertions because of this. **Convention: expected validation and rejection paths log at `Warning`. Reserve `Error` for genuine faults.** `AddExpectedError` is used nowhere in this codebase and should stay that way — fix the severity instead of suppressing the check.

15. **Specs reference canon constants, never magic numbers** (added V33) → the World Map discovery specs hardcoded `10.0f` query radii and literal coordinates, which is exactly why the 2,500km rescale silently invalidated them. They now use `SensorRangeOnFootKm` / `SensorRangeGrindwalkerKm` / `SensorRangeBlackjackKm` from `CogheimWorldMapConstants.h`, so a future tuning change surfaces as an **intentional test update** rather than a stale-data mystery.

16. **Spec-constructed subsystems need a valid Outer** (added V33) → `NewObject<T>()` with no outer ensures *"created in invalid Outer /Script/CoreUObject.Package"*. Use `NewObject<T>(GetTransientPackage())`.

17. **A wrong diagnosis that shipped in a handoff gets corrected in the Index, not overwritten** (added V33) → the Group 2/3 "inverted assertions" reading was wrong and was published in `COGHEIM_Handoff_TestFailures_V1.md`. It is corrected in §35B **with the original error named**, because a silently-replaced wrong answer can still be acted on from the stale document.

18. **File access is not a shell** (added V33) → an assistant with file access to the Shadow PC can write and re-read edits, and can verify BOMs, brace balance, and arithmetic. It **cannot** claim anything compiles, links, or passes. Those claims require a build the founder actually runs. Every session must state which of the two it is doing.

19. **Defend design pillars with tests, not memory** (added V35) → the four chassis-scale specs mean "Drifter interiors stay human-scale" cannot erode silently: widen a doorway or grow the chassis and the suite fails. **Every pillar that can be expressed as a constraint should be.**

20. **`PublicDependencyModuleNames` gets you linkage, not headers** (added V35) → flat modules (sources at module root, no `Public/` folder) need an explicit `PublicIncludePaths.Add(ModuleDirectory)` or cross-module includes fail with `C1083`. Latent in six modules.

21. **Convert at the readout, never in the simulation** (added V35) → engine stays centimetres, stored constants stay metric and named for their unit, **display converts**. Applies to any future unit, locale, or presentation change.

22. **An unwired seam gets a blunt header block, not a quiet TODO** (added V35) → `UJumpComponent` computes a height nothing consumes, and says so in the header in terms that survive being read by someone in a hurry. **The seam being explicit is what makes the verified number honest.**

23. **Hand-placed modules must be entry-point-checked by hand** (added V39, after the `CogheimDialogueUI` editor-init blocker) → `Deploy-CogheimAlpha.ps1`’s post-placement sanity check — every module has a `Build.cs` **and** an `IMPLEMENT_MODULE` — is this project’s only automated guard against a missing module entry point, and it runs **only** on modules deployed through the script. `CogheimDialogueUI` was written into `Source/` and registered in the `.uproject` by hand, bypassed the check, compiled and linked cleanly, and crashed the editor at load. That is the **fourth** such incident (`CogheimNetcode`; `SRF` + `Weather`; `CogheimDialogueUI`), and std. #8 already stated the rule — restating it is not the fix. **A missing entry point is invisible to the compiler and the linker**, so a green build is never evidence a module initializes. Any module added outside the deploy script gets a manual two-file check before its build is called green, and “compiles and links” is reported separately from “initializes” (§35D).

24. **Ask the engine for the API; do not recall it** (added V39 amended, after six wrong guesses in one session) → in a single session, `parameter_info`, `get_assets_by_class("...")`, `unreal.ELoginUIState`, `parent_class`, `get_super_class` and `widget_tree` were all wrong — recalled from memory instead of checked. Every one threw. **`dir(unreal.SomeClass)` filtered for a keyword answers the question in one line and has been right every time it was actually used** — it is how `get_blueprint_parent_class` was found, and how "UMG widget trees are unreachable from Python" was established as fact rather than assumed from a failure. Corollary: **every editor script prints a read-back verification of what it actually wrote.** That is what caught all six; none reached shipped C++ or exported art. A script that reports success without re-reading the asset is not evidence.

25. **A write cannot verify itself by reading its own write back** (added V40, from the Boot Flow session) → two PIE verifications were reported as passing because the check re-read a Blueprint CDO in the *same session* that had just written it in memory — `set_editor_property` changes the CDO in memory only, and `compile_blueprint()` is what commits it to disk; skipping the compile (on a mistaken half-remembered concern that compiling would discard the write) meant nothing had actually persisted, and the same-session readback could not tell the difference. **Verification must read something the write had to pass through**: package dependencies on disk, the editor's own Details panel, or runtime behaviour in a fresh PIE session. `cogheim_widget_audit.py`'s dependency-list read is the house pattern. Also this session (§35I): a landscape import that "looked right" from a screenshot was tiling at exactly 2000m — found only by building a proxy-bounds-vs-source measurement script. **Measure first, in both cases** — a same-session readback and an eyeballed screenshot fail the same way.

26. **A persistent level is a container, never a canvas** (added V43, from the level architecture pass) → a level may hold only landscape actors and proxies, data layer definitions, Level Instance / Packed Level Actor references, a level bounds volume, its own sky/atmosphere/fog/post-process, and streaming or gameplay volumes. **Everything else is content and belongs in a Level Instance.** The evidence is a measured 2,800× file-size gap inside this project on the same day: `L_Drifter_Blockout.umap` is 36,471,238 bytes because its content is authored straight into the map, while `L_Meridian_Region.umap` is 13,074 bytes because World Partition and OFPA put its actors in `__ExternalActors__`. A loose static mesh in a persistent level is a defect, not a shortcut. Corollary: **canonical position is a fraction of disc radius resolved to centimetres by script — never a transform whose only record is inside a map file** (§35J).

27. **Classify every engine feature you raise, and state experimental status every time** (added V43, from the UE 5.8 research pass) → Networked Physics, Physics Replication LOD, Bubble Resimulation, Physics Replication Quantization, Mesh Terrain, the Unreal MCP server and the Additive Look-At Control Rig are all **Experimental in 5.8** with public APIs Epic says will change between releases. Never place an experimental system on a critical path without an explicit founder decision that names the risk, and **always give the fallback** — what Cogheim does if the feature is cut or changed. Never invent an Unreal class, function, macro, module, plugin, setting or CVar; if you are not certain a symbol exists in 5.8, say so and check official documentation or the engine source. A plausible-sounding API is worse than an admission of uncertainty, because it compiles in the reader's head and fails on the machine. This standard is now also carried in the project instructions as the **Unreal Engine Expert Mode** addendum.

28. **A verification that reads the same in-memory session that just wrote the data is not a verification** (added V43, extending std. #25 from a one-off incident to a general rule) → §38 std. #25 recorded two PIE checks that reported passing because they re-read data from the session that had written it. That is a class of error, not an incident. **Read back through something the write had to pass through** — the filesystem, a fresh editor session, a re-opened asset. Applied deliberately this session: `cogheim_create_levels.py` reported `VERIFY PASSED` via `does_asset_exist`, which reads the in-memory asset registry, so the twelve `.umap` files were then re-read directly off the Shadow PC filesystem before the result was accepted. **The Shadow PC project folder (`C:\CogheimAlpha\CogheimAlpha`) is now directly readable by Cowork sessions**, which makes this cheap rather than expensive — build logs and `Saved/Logs/CogheimAlpha.log` can be read rather than pasted. This does **not** relax the BUILD AND LOG VERIFICATION rules; an unread log is still an unknown result.

29. **Read the project knowledge base before writing a Master Index, not just the previous Index** (added V45, after the failure that produced this version) → V43 and V44 were both written from V42 alone. Between them, **eleven session documents and one corrections memo written specifically against V43** were produced in parallel and went unread. Both versions therefore carried claims that were already known to be false when written: 674/674 as the citable figure, 13 modules, a "Pass 2a built + validated 38/38" that does not exist in the repository, a retired 900×260×320 hull at 4 km/h, and "magic is parked, nothing assumes it" while a 25-section magic system design with four founder rulings sat in project knowledge. **The Index is the continuity mechanism; writing it from a stale copy of itself is the one failure that corrupts every session downstream.** Procedure, mandatory: call `project_info` for the full document list, sort by creation time, read everything newer than the Index being superseded, and read any document whose name contains "Correction". Where a session document and the Index disagree, **the session document wins unless it is itself superseded** — it was written closer to the evidence. Corollary: the previous Index's *version number is not proof it is current*; another session may have written a higher one, and creation timestamps are the only reliable ordering.


30. **A delete is proven by the file being gone, never by the return value of the call that deleted it** (added V46, from the `L_Drifter_Blockout` retirement) → `EditorAssetLibrary.delete_asset()` returned **`True` twice** while the `.umap` stayed on disk and in the registry; `unload_packages()` also reported success and a forced `collect_garbage()` ran. The real cause was `PermissionError [WinError 32]` — **the running editor held an OS file handle from having opened that map earlier in the session. Releasing the `UPackage` does not release the handle, and no Python API can drop it; the handle dies only with the editor process.** This is the same class of error as BUILD AND LOG VERIFICATION: `delete_asset() == True` is *"it should have deleted"*, not a result. **Check `os.path.exists` and `does_asset_exist` afterwards, and if either is still true, say so.** Working sequence: repoint every reference → verify 0 referencers → **restart the editor so it never opens the doomed asset** → delete on the fresh session.

31. **A number formatted for a human to read is not a number to assert against** (added V46, from the Drifter V5 C++ port) → `drifter_blockout_v5.py` prints its Z extent with `"%.0f"`, so it **reports** `−3301` while its own JSON holds `−3301.13`. The printed figure went into a spec assertion as if it were the value, and **an exact port failed against it by 0.13 cm.** Same family as `delete_asset() == True` and "it should compile" — **a display artefact mistaken for a result.** Assert against the source of the number, not against its rendering, and state the tolerance and why it is stable.

32. **Re-stage immediately before editing a shared file, and pass `expectedMtimeMs` on every commit** (added V46, after a self-inflicted regression) → `DrifterSystemsSpec.cpp` was staged into a session workspace early; three fixes landed on the Shadow PC afterwards; the hours-old stale copy was then edited and **committed whole**, reverting three green tests. **A staged copy is a point-in-time snapshot, not a live file.** `expectedMtimeMs` would have refused the write outright. Corollary, and the tell that was read past for an hour: **a test that changes name is not the same test** — `_spine_z_` versus `_world_z_` was in the log the whole time. Corollary 2: **when two sessions edit one file, say so in both handoffs.**

33. **Deleting a public symbol is followed by a sweep of the whole module, not of the file you were editing** (added V46, from RFA Increment 4b) → after removing `CurrentServerFrame` from `USRFStriderTransformComponent`, the component's own `.h/.cpp` were swept and reported clean; **the module was not**, and `SRFSpec.cpp:473` — written two increments earlier — still referenced it. The compiler caught it in 0.79 s but the cost was a full build cycle. **`grep -rn` across every `.cpp` and `.h` in the module. Specs reference production symbols and are easy to forget precisely because they are not "the code".**

34. **A gate that measures a summary statistic instead of a relationship can always be satisfied without being met** (added V46, after specifying the same metallic-mask requirement wrongly three times) → `min < 30` was satisfied by **one dark pixel**; a histogram-distribution rule was satisfied by **uniform noise**, with two different materials returning identical distributions to three decimal places; and a fixed 5%-dielectric floor, tuned on patched iron, **failed a physically correct brass mask.** In all three the *summary statistic* of the mask was measured rather than its **relationship to the thing it masks**. **Where a rule governs a mask, correlate it against what it is masking, and be suspicious of any threshold tuned on a single example.** The corrected metallic rule, and the reference distributions, are in §35M.2.

35. **A guard band fitted to a snapshot is not a guard** (added V46, from the shell collision script) → `build_drifter_shell_collision.py`'s Z band fired on a delivery where `zmin` had moved **legitimately**, because the boarding ramp deployed to the ground. The band had been fitted to the previous delivery's measured numbers. **Rederive guards from physics or from a stated invariant** — *nothing may sit below the model's ground plane* — **not from last week's data.** Corollary: **annotate any constant that has already been misread once** — the deployed-ramp bound was mistaken for beam, and the script now says so in place.

36. **When a script cannot verify its own precondition, it must not proceed as if it had** (added V46, from the terrain/vegetation work) → fail loudly, or take the path that needs no check. A warning logged and then ignored cost several rebuild cycles. Corollary from the same session, and it generalises: **read the log first.** Every one of that session's traps — the 32-sampler landscape material limit, duplicate Grass Output nodes, `StaticMesh.materials.clear()` zeroing every `material_index`, FBX dropping unused material slots, `mesh_smooth_type='FACE'`, `align_to_surface` on things that grow — was diagnosable from `Saved/Logs/CogheimAlpha.log` **before** it was diagnosable from a screenshot.
37. **The Index carries a rendered production status section, and it is never hand-edited** (added V46) → §0 is generated from `Scripts/cogheim_production_status.json` by `Scripts/render_production_status.py`, which also emits the HTML board. **Editing a bar in the markdown is the same class of error as editing a test result** — it makes the document disagree with its own source. Every Index pass: update item states in the JSON, re-render, paste. An item may only be moved to `verified` with evidence a human can re-read, and the four top states are this project's own ladder — `built_unverified` → `compiled` → `loaded` → `verified` — so a percentage can never quietly imply a log that was never opened.

38. **The Master Index is now a single-copy document, and project knowledge is not a backup** (added V47, when V41–V46 were purged to reclaim space) → every prior version has been deleted. Eight months of canon, locks, measurements, corrections and process standards exist in **exactly one file**, and an accidental overwrite or a bad edit loses all of it with nothing to restore from. **Keep a copy outside project knowledge — the site repo, Google Drive, or the Shadow PC — and refresh it every time a new version is written.** Two corollaries. **The session and founder-decision documents must not be purged alongside the indexes**: with no earlier Index to diff against, they are the only remaining evidence trail, and they are individually small — the indexes were the expensive files, not the records. And **a version's historical changelog tail is not dead weight**; deleting the history at the same moment you delete every prior copy of it is precisely the combination that cannot be undone.

**Decision protocol:**
- Kaelic Can presents binary or multiple-choice options
- Boom locks with minimal words
- Kaelic Can writes to spec without deviation
- Boom corrects drift firmly and immediately — acknowledge and correct without argument

**File delivery standards:**
- All zips: flat-root (`zip -j`); validate with Python before presenting
- Present via `present_files` tool
- Code output: `/mnt/user-data/outputs/`

**Naming Graveyard:** Check §32 before proposing any new name

**Founder Lock Protocol:** All creative decisions, naming decisions, and canon locks are founder-held. Kaelic Can proposes. Boom locks.

**Memory management (established July 12, 2026, updated July 13):** Since the person confirmed the Master Index is never shared externally and is reloaded nearly every session, memory was cleared to **zero entries** — even sealed/founder-secret lore now lives only in this private Index rather than duplicated in memory. The 30-entry cap is confirmed **hard, with no configurable increase** (verified via web search). If memory usage resumes for any reason, consolidate before the cap is hit rather than after — do not wait for a forced merge mid-session.

*COGHEIM Master Index V35*
*Limitless Game Makers LLC — Confidential*
*Compiled by Kaelic Can — August 20, 2026*
*Supersedes all prior versions (V1–V34)*

**What's new in V35 vs V34 — the suite went fully green:**

- 🏆 **674 / 674 PASSING, ZERO FAILURES, VERIFIED.** `Rebuild All: 1 succeeded, 0 failed.` The 10 outstanding failures were fixed *and confirmed*, then 23 new specs were added on top and all passed. **No known-broken runtime system remains in Alpha scope.** The 641/651 ceiling that governed this document since V32 is **retired**.
- ✅ **All six §13H Founder Locks recorded** — chassis height **8 ft 6 in**; Drifter interiors stay human-scale; `JumpVelocityPerMobilityPoint` **2.0 → 6.0**; committed jump arc (facing turns, heading and speed do not); **MAXIOM reads imperial**; jump explicitly flagged as an **unwired seam**.
- ⚠ **V33's parked jump-tuning item is closed by overturning V33's own decision.** V33 said "fix the test only, don't touch locomotion" and warned the numbers would read as broken. V35 changes the locked value instead. **Recorded as a visible overturn**, per §38 rule 12 — which is exactly how a lock is supposed to change.
- 🔴 **Lock 6 is the one to quote externally.** Jump math is verified; **jump traversal is not implemented** — nothing consumes the computed height. The vertical figures are sound; the horizontal gap figures are a **design target, not a measured result.**
- 🔴 **A wrong claim is recorded as wrong** — "no `Build.cs` change needed" was incorrect; flat modules need `PublicIncludePaths.Add(ModuleDirectory)` or cross-module includes fail with `C1083`. **The same trap is latent in six other modules**, logged rather than pre-emptively patched.
- ⚠ **File-count discrepancy flagged, not averaged** — the session record says 9 files (3 new); a status summary says 20 (3 new). **Needs founder confirmation before external use.**
- 🎨 **Art handoff is now fully specified** — and the **Iron Frame chassis mesh is identified as the highest-leverage art asset in the project**, because it unblocks jump wiring, animation, and the visibility pass at once.
- ⬜ **New carry-forwards:** Python mirror parity unchecked for the three new constants; two benign unexplained log lines (`LogRHICore` budget, `CleanupWorld` called twice on every Wingworks spec — will bite as those specs grow); stale "never compiled" flag in `CogheimDrifter.Build.cs`.
- ✅ **§38 gained rules 19–22:** defend pillars with tests; linkage ≠ headers; convert at the readout; unwired seams get blunt header blocks.
- 🔴 **What 674/674 does NOT prove, stated plainly:** it is a **single-machine verification of game logic.** Concurrency, load, and multi-client replication (**M2**) remain entirely untested. That gap is the honest next milestone and should lead every investor conversation rather than trail it.

- ✅ **Iron-Hail LOCKED** — inverted-cone ore-hopper, 60m, retractable rake booms 44m deployed, central sluice gate, crane-loading rearm. Two airship concepts (90m, 45m) retired to the graveyard as deliberate overturns.

- 🎵 **§16B expanded substantially** — 10-genre base pool plus per-culture signature genres, 45–55 tracks per block calibrated to real Suno output length, 24 PSI-dialled zone stations, a 50-track album across 8 movements, and three signature pieces. **The access model is locked so that culture never gates music** — which is what keeps it clear of §18.
- 🔴 **Beneficiary conflict surfaced** (§1) — **Solitude Shore vs. Metamorphosis Ministry.** Both in circulation. Neither should appear in any external document until settled, and the entity's charitable status and the definition of "net proceeds" are both unverified.
- ✅ **Funding picture corrected** — $6M cap defensible **on evidence** post-compile-gate, with concurrency/load testing (M2) named as the unproven gap. NJ Digital Media Tax Credit added as a non-dilutive opportunity (requires professional review). 1Up Ventures flagged as strongest fit.
- ⏰ **Epic MegaGrants framing flagged stale** (§31) — still describes a C#→UE5 migration. Reframe around **UE5.8-native RFA**. **Window closes September 4, 2026.**

*COGHEIM Master Index V33*
*Limitless Game Makers LLC — Confidential*
*Compiled by Kaelic Can — August 17, 2026*
*Supersedes all prior versions (V1–V32)*

**What's new in V33 vs V32 — two sessions, two founder locks, one correction of my own:**

- 🟡 **All 10 in-engine test failures fixed across 12 files — ON DISK, NOT VERIFIED** (§35B). No rebuild, no re-run. **641/651 remains the only citable number** until the build runs; 651/651 is the expectation, not a result.
- ⚠️ **The "748 tests" figure is withdrawn** — founder confirmed it as a misrecollection. The registered spec count is **651**. It does not appear anywhere in this Index as a result.
- 🔴 **I was wrong, and it's recorded rather than overwritten** (§35B) — I diagnosed three of the ten failures as inverted assertions. They weren't. **UE's Automation framework fails a test on captured `Error`-severity logs even when every assertion passes.** The wrong reading shipped in a handoff document, so the correction names the original error. New §38 rule 14 makes Warning-vs-Error a house convention.
- 🔒 **FOUNDER LOCK — sensors do not scale with the disc.** World grew ×4.17, sensors kept their 2× bump. Densest cluster fell 7→5 and relocated to an Ironvast river braid; Grindwalker sees 3 at Coldforge Landing, Blackjack 6, on foot 1. Strengthens the 4X exploration pillar and gives Scavenger scouting a real job — **but it is unplaytested and labelled Requires Validation, not a proven win.**
- 🔒 **FOUNDER LOCK — Iron Frame jump was a test bug (Option A).** The spec set Tier 5 on Locomotion but not on Jump; a 0.1s Euler step is 61% of the Untrained hop. **Locked locomotion untouched.** 🔴 **Tuning parked, not closed:** 3.3 cm untrained / 20.4 cm Tier 5 will read as broken to a playtester. Decide before Alpha.
- ✅ **Two Group 1 canon calls resolved in favour of the data** — Academy count **8**; naming **site-prefixed** (`"Coldforge Maritime Academy"`).
- ✅ **NEW §35C — Drifter blockout in-editor spawn**, and the **Z-down finding**: `SpineZ` grows with deck number, so local +Z points toward the keel and the ship spawns upside down at identity. `Roll = 180°` unblocks tonight. 🔴 **But it is a display fix over a convention mismatch, and SRF/RFA resolves in frame-local space — decide `Roll` vs. negate-at-generation before any content is placed in that level.**
- ⬜ **New carry-forwards surfaced:** stale "338 registry locations" comment in the founder-locked `CogheimWorldMapConstants.h` (registry holds 738); SyndicateResearch empty `FUniqueNetIdRepl` re-flagged as **SHIP-BLOCKER**; Login `PreLogin` merge; Dialogue `ApplyValidatedFlags` unwired seam.

Two real deployment blockers resolved (`.uproject` file association, VS2026 workload substitution for the now-unavailable VS2022). Phase 2 Enhanced Input automation script delivered, not yet executed.
- **§5B NEW — Strider Reference Frame (SRF) Netcode.** Solves the floating-origin problem for a 900m Strider moving at 4 km/h with players walking inside it. Approach A (local-frame replication) locked over Approach B, with the false-positive world-frame speed check formally proven. 31/31 tests passing.
- **§13B NEW — Iron Frame System, full redesign.** Single-chassis mount/armor system replaces the earlier Breach/Sentry variant concept. 5 mounts, 8 armor slots, paired mount-armor dependency, 3-stage resourcing chain, external-combat counter role. **Fully coded and ported to UE5.8 C++ this session** — the first complete system to make it all the way through the new pipeline.
- **§13C NEW — Vehicle Roster & Universal Modular Armament System.** 10-platform vehicle roster, slot-cost weapon system (any weapon mounts any platform with sufficient budget), 9-weapon catalog across 3 damage categories, full crafting recipes. Crafting quest ledger updated 625 → 634.
- **§16 MAXIOM & Axiom rebuilt as V3.0.** 8th module (SIGNAL — World Radio) added. Real gap found and fixed: Drifter Mode tab-locking wasn't previously specced. Honest architecture — stubs where subsystems don't exist yet rather than fabricated data.
- **§16A NEW — Player Communications System.** 4 subsystems (channel routing, direct links, voice rooms, chain-of-command). No global chat channel, by design, at 150K-player scale. 2 of 4 subsystems already ported to UE5.8 C++.
- **§26A NEW — Character Progression Framework.** Model C (Strider-heritage-tied Role specialization) locked over two rejected alternatives. Alpha scope deliberately minimal — no class picker, no XP, no skill points.
- **§35 Code Deliverables — major status shift.** Three systems now show ✅ **complete UE5.8 C++ ports**, not just specs: Iron Frame, CommsRouter, TeamManager. This is the first tranche of the post-§2B rebuild actually landing. Verified test total climbs to 1,150+.
- **§38 Memory management updated** — memory confirmed cleared to zero entries (up from the lean-but-nonzero state in V19/V20), since the person confirmed this Index is never shared and is reloaded nearly every session. 30-slot cap confirmed hard via web search, no override exists.
